# Schools of Vedānta — Complete Learning Session

> **Catalogue identity:** Philosophy Optional · Philosophy Paper I — Indian Philosophy · Syllabus item 19.
> **Official syllabus wording (verbatim, printed spelling retained):** `Schools of Vedanta : Brahman; Isvara; Atman; Jiva; Jagat; Maya; Avida; Adhyasa; Moksa; Aprthaksiddhi; Pancavidhabheda.`
> **Terminology rule:** the English concept leads; the accurate Sanskrit term in IAST follows immediately in brackets.
> **Evidence discipline:** doctrine, textual attribution and previous-year wording follow the repository's canonical owners. Facts are separated from interpretation, and genuinely contested matters — the identity or distinction of illusion and ignorance, the locus of ignorance, the ownership of later explanatory models, and the graded destiny of souls in dualism — are reported as contested rather than settled. Model answers are independent learner practice and are never presented as official keys.

## BASIC LEARNING SESSION

### How this Basic block is organised

The nineteen sessions below run in teaching order, not in source order. Each session opens with a
definition, gives a gateway idea in plain language, shows the concept before it argues it, then
supplies the named evidence, the answer-grabbing line, the must-write keywords, a worked
answer-paragraph model, the exam route, the traps and a rapid revision strip.

Everything needed for a complete 10-, 15- or 20-mark answer on any of the twenty previous-year
questions owned by this syllabus item is inside this Basic block. The Optional Advanced block near
the end of this document is enrichment; **no core answer depends on it.**

Syllabus item 19 is the only clause in Paper I that begins with the plural word **Schools**. That
plural is the whole examination. Eleven doctrines are printed — ultimate reality (**brahman**), the
Lord (**īśvara**), the true self (**ātman**), the individual self (**jīva**), the world
(**jagat**), indescribable illusion (**māyā**), ignorance (printed `Avida`, normalised
**avidyā**), superimposition (**adhyāsa**), liberation (**mokṣa**), inseparable dependence
(**apṛthaksiddhi**) and fivefold difference (**pañcavidhabheda**) — but not one of them has a
single Vedāntic meaning. Each is a contested reading of the same three texts. The sessions
therefore follow the dispute's own order: first the shared canon and the interpretive problem it
creates, then ultimate reality and its causality, then the instruments by which each school claims
to know it, then the descending series self, individual, world, then the machinery of bondage
(illusion, ignorance, superimposition, error), then liberation, then the two doctrines that name
the rival systems outright — inseparable dependence and fivefold difference — then the wider
schools, and finally the external debates and the graded verdict.

| Session | What it settles | Why it comes here |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The threefold canon (**prasthāna-trayī**) and why one canon produced six schools | Fixes the interpretive problem that generates every later dispute |
| 2 | Ultimate reality (**brahman**): two definitions, qualityless versus qualified | The root fork from which all eleven syllabus terms descend |
| 3 | Ultimate reality as efficient and material cause | The 2025 fifteen-marker and the causation comparison |
| 4 | Six means of valid knowledge (**pramāṇa**) and why testimony is primary | How non-dualism claims to know a non-objectifiable absolute |
| 5 | The Lord (**īśvara**) and the original–reflection model (**bimba-pratibimba-vāda**) | The 2025 twenty-marker and half of the 2022 twenty-marker |
| 6 | The true self (**ātman**) and the witness (**sākṣī**) | The 2020 fifteen-marker |
| 7 | The individual self (**jīva**): three non-dual models, two realist rivals | The 2021 and 2022 questions in one place |
| 8 | The world (**jagat**), apparent transformation and the levels of reality | The 2021 and 2022 world-status questions |
| 9 | Indescribable illusion (**māyā**) and ignorance (**avidyā**) | The 2018 ten-marker on two indescribabilities |
| 10 | The seven objections (**sapta-anupapatti**) and the qualified-realist alternative | The 2025 ten-marker and the 2018 fifteen-marker |
| 11 | Superimposition (**adhyāsa**) and indescribable-error theory (**anirvacanīya-khyāti**) | The 2021 twenty-marker on the problem of error |
| 12 | The great sentence (**mahāvākya**) "That thou art" (**tat tvam asi**) | The 2023 ten-marker and the semantics behind identity |
| 13 | Liberation (**mokṣa**) across the three schools | The 2022 and 2023 liberation questions |
| 14 | Inseparable dependence (**apṛthaksiddhi**) and the body–soul relation | The 2024 ten-marker; the named syllabus term |
| 15 | Fivefold difference (**pañcavidhabheda**) | The 2020 ten-marker; the named syllabus term |
| 16 | Nimbārka, Vallabha and the Caitanya tradition | The 2018 twenty-marker and the 2020 matter question |
| 17 | Śaṃkara against Sāṃkhya — the chief-wrestler (**pradhāna-malla**) debate | Why the absolute must be conscious |
| 18 | The logical consequences of non-dual absolutism | The 2019 twenty-marker |
| 19 | Inter-thinker debates, criticisms and the graded verdict | The evaluative close every high-mark answer needs |

> **One-line map of the whole topic.** Vedānta is not one doctrine but three answers to a single
> question — is the relation of the finite to the absolute **identity**, **qualification** or
> **difference**? — and every printed syllabus term is that same fork applied to a different
> object.

---
### SESSION 1 — ONE CANON, SIX COMMENTARIAL SCHOOLS: THE INTERPRETIVE PROBLEM

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Vedānta is the family of systems that read the closing portion of
the Veda as teaching about what ultimately is, rather than about what ought ritually to be done.
Its schools are commentarial: they do not differ about which texts are authoritative, they differ
about what those texts mean.

**Technical definition.** Later inquiry (**uttara-mīmāṃsā**), that is Vedānta, interprets the
knowledge-portion (**jñāna-kāṇḍa**) of the Veda through the **threefold canon
(prasthāna-trayī)**: the revealed foundation (**śruti-prasthāna**), the remembered synthesis
(**smṛti-prasthāna**) and the reasoned systematization (**nyāya-prasthāna**). A "school of
Vedānta" is constituted by a commentary (**bhāṣya**) on that canon and by the relation it assigns
between ultimate reality, selves and world.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Imagine a single constitution, three centuries old, that everybody accepts as binding and nobody
may amend. One clause says the sovereign is one and without a second. Another describes the
sovereign as ruler, creator and object of worship, which presupposes subjects distinct from him.
A third says that the subject *is* the sovereign.

You cannot delete a clause. You must therefore decide which clause states the literal rule and
which clauses are read in its light. One reading says the identity clause is literal and the
difference clauses describe a provisional order. A second says the difference clauses are literal
and the identity clause means inseparable dependence rather than sameness. A third says difference
is literal all the way down, and the identity clause means only that nothing exists independently
of the sovereign.

Those three constitutional readings are Advaita, Viśiṣṭādvaita and Dvaita. Nothing in this topic is
an arbitrary preference: each school is defending a way of keeping *all* the clauses without
contradiction, and each pays a different price for doing so.

#### VISUAL — ONE CANON, THE FORK, AND WHAT EACH BRANCH COSTS

```text
                  THE THREEFOLD CANON (prasthāna-trayī)
   principal Upaniṣads  +  Bhagavad Gītā  +  Brahma Sūtra of Bādarāyaṇa
   revealed foundation     remembered synthesis   reasoned systematization
                                    |
                 the Sūtra is COMPACT: aphorisms, not finished doctrine
                 => the common site of COMPETING COMMENTARIES
                                    |
        "Is the relation of the finite to the absolute IDENTITY,
                    QUALIFICATION, or DIFFERENCE?"
                                    |
   +--------------------------+-----+------------------------+
   |                          |                              |
 IDENTITY                 QUALIFICATION                  DIFFERENCE
 Advaita — Śaṅkara        Viśiṣṭādvaita — Rāmānuja      Dvaita — Madhva
 difference is            selves and world are          God, selves and matter
 ignorance-conditioned    REAL inseparable modes        are really and eternally
 appearance               of one qualified whole        different; only God is
                                                        independent (svatantra)
   |                          |                              |
 PRICE PAID:              PRICE PAID:                   PRICE PAID:
 world, individuality,    real modes press on           non-dual Upaniṣadic
 ethics and devotion      divine simplicity and         sentences become harder
 become lower-order       changelessness                to read; hierarchy enters
   |                          |                              |
   +------------------- FURTHER READINGS ----------------------+
   Dvaitādvaita (Nimbārka) ..... difference AND non-difference both real
   Śuddhādvaita (Vallabha) ..... world is a REAL UNCHANGED manifestation
   Acintyabhedābheda (Caitanya)  difference-and-non-difference, INCONCEIVABLE
```

*What the diagram shows: the six schools are not six opinions about Brahman but six settlements of
one hermeneutic conflict inside a canon nobody may edit. Read the branch labels and you already
have the comparative spine that every question on this clause demands.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **The canon is shared, the interpretation is not.** The revealed foundation
   (**śruti-prasthāna**) is the principal Upaniṣads. The remembered synthesis
   (**smṛti-prasthāna**) is the Bhagavad Gītā, which coordinates knowledge, devotion and action.
   The reasoned systematization (**nyāya-prasthāna**) is Bādarāyaṇa's *Brahma Sūtra*, which
   harmonises Upaniṣadic teaching and answers rival views. Because all three are common property,
   no school can win by citing a text the others reject.
2. **The Sūtra under-determines the doctrine.** Its aphorisms are compact and do not contain every
   later school-formulation ready-made. That compactness is precisely why it became the common
   site of competing commentaries rather than a settlement of them.
3. **Ownership boundary with earlier inquiry.** Earlier Mīmāṃsā (**pūrva-mīmāṃsā**) owns the
   injunction-centred reading of the action-portion; Vedānta owns the Brahman-centred
   interpretation of the Upaniṣads. The boundary is not merely a division of chapters: it is a
   disagreement about whether Vedic sentences can independently reveal an accomplished reality
   (**siddha-vastu**) or must ultimately serve an act to be done (**kārya**).
4. **The six commentarial schools.** Each is fixed by a teacher, a textual orientation and a root
   relation.

| School | Foundational teacher and secure textual orientation | Root relation |
|---|---|---|
| **Non-dualism (advaita)** | Śaṅkara's *Brahma-sūtra-bhāṣya* and his Upaniṣad and Gītā commentaries | identity; difference is ignorance-conditioned appearance |
| **Qualified non-dualism (viśiṣṭādvaita)** | Rāmānuja's *Śrī-bhāṣya*, *Vedārthasaṅgraha* and Gītā commentary | real selves and world inseparably qualify ultimate reality |
| **Dualism (dvaita)** | Madhva's *Brahma-sūtra-bhāṣya*, *Anuvyākhyāna* and Gītā commentary | God, selves and matter are really different; only God is independent |
| **Dualistic non-dualism (dvaitādvaita)** | Nimbārka's *Vedānta-pārijāta-saurabha* | real difference and real non-difference through dependence |
| **Pure non-dualism (śuddhādvaita)** | Vallabha's *Aṇubhāṣya* and Bhāgavata-centred works | the world is a real unchanged manifestation of ultimate reality |
| **Inconceivable difference–non-difference (acintyabhedābheda)** | the Caitanya tradition systematized by Rūpa, Jīva and Baladeva Gosvāmin | real difference and non-difference through divine powers |

5. **Why the plural in the syllabus matters.** The printed clause names doctrines, not a closed
   list of philosophers; but the verified previous-year papers directly require Nimbārka, Vallabha
   and the Caitanya tradition. The "big three" therefore remain the comparative spine, not the
   whole examinable taxonomy.
6. **The exam thumb-rule that follows.** Every term in this clause must be answered in
   **three-school comparative mode**, even when the question names only one school. A
   single-school answer on a shared term is structurally incomplete, because the term's meaning is
   fixed only by contrast.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **Bādarāyaṇa, *Brahma Sūtra*** — the reasoned systematization on which all six schools comment;
  its compactness is the structural cause of the disagreement.
- **Śaṅkara, *Brahma-sūtra-bhāṣya*** — the non-dual commentary, and the source of the opening
  discussion of superimposition treated in Session 11.
- **Rāmānuja, *Śrī-bhāṣya* and *Vedārthasaṅgraha*** — the qualified non-dual commentary and the
  independent doctrinal digest.
- **Madhva, *Brahma-sūtra-bhāṣya* and *Anuvyākhyāna*** — the dualist commentary and its extended
  versified exposition.
- **Nimbārka, *Vedānta-pārijāta-saurabha*; Vallabha, *Aṇubhāṣya*; Jīva Gosvāmin, *Ṣaṭ-sandarbha***
  — the three later systematizations required by the 2018 and 2020 papers.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Vedānta's schools do not disagree about which texts are authoritative; they disagree about what
> an unamendable canon can be made to mean, and every doctrine in syllabus item 19 is the price
> one school pays to keep the clauses it cannot delete.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

threefold canon (**prasthāna-trayī**) · revealed foundation (**śruti-prasthāna**) · remembered
synthesis (**smṛti-prasthāna**) · reasoned systematization (**nyāya-prasthāna**) · commentary
(**bhāṣya**) · accomplished reality (**siddha-vastu**) · act to be done (**kārya**) · non-dualism
(**advaita**) · qualified non-dualism (**viśiṣṭādvaita**) · dualism (**dvaita**) · dualistic
non-dualism (**dvaitādvaita**) · pure non-dualism (**śuddhādvaita**) · inconceivable
difference–non-difference (**acintyabhedābheda**)

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** The plurality of Vedānta is generated by a shared canon, not by disagreement over
authority.
**Named evidence.** All six schools comment on the same threefold canon (**prasthāna-trayī**), and
Śaṅkara's *Brahma-sūtra-bhāṣya*, Rāmānuja's *Śrī-bhāṣya* and Madhva's *Brahma-sūtra-bhāṣya* are
commentaries on one and the same aphoristic text.
**Analysis.** Because the aphorisms are compact and the Upaniṣads contain both identity-sentences
and difference-sentences, the interpretive question — which set is literal? — cannot be settled by
citation alone, so each school must supply a metaphysics that makes the remaining sentences
intelligible.
**Qualification.** This does not make the schools arbitrary: each is testable by asking what it
must sacrifice — world-status, divine simplicity or the non-dual sentences — to keep the rest.

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

This session is never asked alone, and that is exactly why it matters: it supplies the opening
frame for all twenty owned questions. Use it in one or two sentences of the introduction for any
comparative demand — the 2018 twenty-marker on liberation across four schools, the 2021
fifteen-marker comparing Śaṅkara and Rāmānuja on the world, the 2022 fifteen-marker comparing
Madhva and Rāmānuja on liberation, and the 2023 twenty-marker on Rāmānuja's criticism of Śaṅkara.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Do not write that the schools "differ about scripture." They agree about scripture and differ
   about its interpretation; the second claim is worth marks and the first is not.
2. Do not treat the *Brahma Sūtra* as already containing Advaita, or already containing
   Viśiṣṭādvaita. Its compactness is the reason the commentaries diverge.
3. Do not reduce Vedānta to Advaita. Roughly two-thirds of the owned questions in the 2018–2025
   window require Rāmānuja, Madhva or a wider school.
4. Do not smuggle earlier Mīmāṃsā's ritual-hermeneutic questions into a Vedānta answer, or vice
   versa; the ownership boundary is itself examinable.

#### RAPID REVISION

- One canon (**prasthāna-trayī**), six commentarial settlements.
- Fork: identity · qualification · difference.
- Śaṅkara / Rāmānuja / Madhva = the comparative spine; Nimbārka / Vallabha / Caitanya = required by
  the 2018 and 2020 papers.
- Each school is priced: appearance-cost, simplicity-cost, or non-dual-sentence-cost.

---
### SESSION 2 — ULTIMATE REALITY (BRAHMAN): TWO DEFINITIONS AND THREE ANSWERS

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Ultimate reality (**brahman**) is what does not depend on anything
else for its being, and on which everything else depends. Every Vedānta school accepts that much;
they divide on whether it can be described by attributes.

**Technical definition.** Vedānta defines ultimate reality twice. The **essential definition
(svarūpa-lakṣaṇa)** states its intrinsic nature as **being–consciousness–bliss (sat-cit-ānanda)**.
The **accidental or relational definition (taṭastha-lakṣaṇa)** indicates it through its relation to
the world: **janmādyasya yataḥ** — that from which the world originates, in which it is sustained,
and into which it dissolves.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Two ways of identifying a person: "she is by nature courageous and truthful," and "she is the one
who lives in the third house from the corner." The first tells you what she *is*; the second tells
you nothing about her nature but picks her out unmistakably by a relation.

Vedānta needs both. The essential definition answers the metaphysical question — what is the
absolute in itself? The relational definition answers the cosmological question — why should we
call *that* the ground of the world? A good answer uses the first to define and the second to
explain the absolute's explanatory role.

The dispute then begins. If you say the absolute is free of *all* determinate attributes, you
protect its unconditioned status but risk making it unknowable and religiously empty. If you say
it possesses infinite perfect attributes, you keep it knowable, worshipable and causally rich but
must show that qualification does not limit it. Advaita takes the first road; Viśiṣṭādvaita and
Dvaita take the second, for different reasons.

#### VISUAL — THE FORK AT THE ROOT OF THE WHOLE CLAUSE

```text
        ULTIMATE REALITY (brahman) — DEFINED TWICE
   +--------------------------------+---------------------------------+
   | ESSENTIAL (svarūpa-lakṣaṇa)    | RELATIONAL (taṭastha-lakṣaṇa)   |
   | being-consciousness-bliss      | janmādyasya yataḥ — that from   |
   | (sat-cit-ānanda)               | which origin, sustenance and    |
   | = what it IS                   | dissolution proceed = WHY it is |
   |                                | called the ground of the world  |
   +----------------+---------------+---------------------------------+
                    |
     "Does the absolute HAVE determinate attributes?"
                    |
   +----------------+-----------------------------+
   | NO — qualityless (nirguṇa)                   | YES — qualified (saguṇa)
   | ADVAITA                                      |
   |  * nirguṇa ≠ blank; it means free of         |  VIŚIṢṬĀDVAITA (Rāmānuja)
   |    LIMITING, relational, objectifiable       |   * saguṇa in the highest sense:
   |    attributes                                |     ananta-kalyāṇa-guṇa-viśiṣṭa
   |  * sat-cit-ānanda = ESSENCE, not a list of   |     endowed with infinite auspicious
   |    accidental predicates                     |     qualities
   |  * creatorhood, omniscience, lordship,       |   * qualities are PERFECTIONS, not
   |    moral governance belong to the LORD       |     limiting adjuncts
   |    (īśvara) at the empirical level           |   * NO nirguṇa level above the Lord
   |                                              |
   |                                              |  DVAITA (Madhva)
   |                                              |   * Viṣṇu with perfect infinite attributes
   |                                              |   * brahman alone INDEPENDENT (svatantra);
   |                                              |     selves and world DEPENDENT (paratantra)
   |                                              |   * difference itself is ultimate — NOT
   |                                              |     absorbed into qualified unity
   +----------------------------------------------+
                    |
   CONSEQUENCE CASCADE (why this session governs all the others)
   nirguṇa read =>  world becomes appearance · the Lord becomes lower truth ·
                    liberation becomes identity-recognition · scripture is finally sublatable
   saguṇa read  =>  world and devotion become philosophically central ·
                    liberation becomes communion or service · scripture stays final
```

*What the diagram shows: one question — attributes or no attributes — fixes the answer to every
other printed term in syllabus item 19. This is why examiners can ask about the world, the self or
liberation and still be testing this single fork.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **Two definitions, two jobs.** Use the essential definition (**svarūpa-lakṣaṇa**) to say what
   ultimate reality is in itself; use the relational definition (**taṭastha-lakṣaṇa**) to explain
   why it functions as the metaphysical ground of cosmology. Answers that give only one of these
   lose either the metaphysics or the cosmology.
2. **Non-dualism: qualityless ultimate reality (nirguṇa-brahman).** For Śaṅkara it is the sole
   ultimately real (**pāramārthika**) reality. Qualityless (**nirguṇa**) does **not** mean sheer
   blankness or absence of nature; it means freedom from limiting, relational, empirical or
   objectifiable attributes. Being–consciousness–bliss is not a list of accidental predicates
   added to it but the expression of its very essence. All determinate qualities — creatorhood,
   omniscience, lordship, moral governance — belong to the **Lord (īśvara)**, that is, ultimate
   reality as conditioned by indescribable illusion (**māyā**) at the empirical
   (**vyāvahārika**) level. Advaita therefore maintains a strict distinction between qualityless
   ultimate reality as final and qualified ultimate reality (**saguṇa-brahman**) or the Lord as
   empirical theology.
3. **Qualified non-dualism: qualified only.** Rāmānuja rejects any level above the personal Lord.
   Ultimate reality is qualified (**saguṇa**) in the highest sense —
   **ananta-kalyāṇa-guṇa-viśiṣṭa**, endowed with infinite auspicious qualities. These are not
   limiting adjuncts but perfections constitutive of its fullness. It is identified with
   Viṣṇu–Nārāyaṇa, the supreme personal reality who includes within himself all selves and matter
   as inseparable modes. Hence his ultimate reality is not less absolute because qualified;
   qualification is what makes absoluteness intelligible.
4. **Dualism: supreme independent reality.** Madhva also rejects a qualityless level. Ultimate
   reality is the personal Viṣṇu possessing perfect and infinite attributes. It alone is
   **independent (svatantra)**, while individual selves and the world are **dependent
   (paratantra)**. Unlike Rāmānuja, Madhva does not subsume difference within qualified unity; he
   makes difference itself metaphysically ultimate.
5. **Rāmānuja's critique of the qualityless absolute** (asked in 2023). An entity without
   attributes cannot be known. If utterly indeterminate, it cannot be the referent of scriptural
   description. If it has no internal intelligibility, it cannot be the cause of a richly
   qualified world. Scriptural passages that negate defects do not negate all qualities; they deny
   limiting or undesirable predicates while affirming perfection. Therefore, for Rāmānuja,
   Śaṅkara's absolute becomes either unknowable emptiness or an abstraction too thin to ground
   religion, devotion, causality or ethics.
6. **The Advaita reply that must accompany the critique.** Ultimate reality is not known as an
   object with predicates; it is self-revealed as the very ground of all knowing. Denying limiting
   predicates is not denying reality; it is refusing to reduce the condition of all objectification
   to one more object.
7. **Why this is the highest-yield session.** Most questions on this clause reduce to a hidden
   dispute about attributes. Once the absolute is read as qualityless, the world becomes
   appearance, the Lord becomes lower truth, liberation becomes identity, and scripture becomes
   finally sublatable. Once it is read as qualified, world and devotion become philosophically
   central.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **janmādyasya yataḥ** — the relational definition drawn from the opening of the *Brahma Sūtra*'s
  inquiry into the source of the world.
- **sat-cit-ānanda** — the standard formulation of the essential definition.
- **ananta-kalyāṇa-guṇa-viśiṣṭa** — Rāmānuja's formula: qualified by infinite auspicious qualities.
- **svatantra / paratantra** — Madhva's independence–dependence division, the single formula that
  organises his whole system.
- **Rāmānuja, *Śrī-bhāṣya*** — the sustained argument that all cognition is of the qualified, hence
  a wholly unqualified object is not establishable.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The Vedāntic dispute over ultimate reality is not whether it is one, but whether unity survives
> description: Śaṅkara secures the absolute by refusing predication, Rāmānuja secures it by
> perfecting predication, and Madhva secures it by making dependence, not predication, the mark of
> the finite.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

essential definition (**svarūpa-lakṣaṇa**) · relational definition (**taṭastha-lakṣaṇa**) ·
being–consciousness–bliss (**sat-cit-ānanda**) · **janmādyasya yataḥ** · qualityless
(**nirguṇa**) · qualified (**saguṇa**) · **ananta-kalyāṇa-guṇa-viśiṣṭa** · independent
(**svatantra**) · dependent (**paratantra**) · ultimately real (**pāramārthika**) · empirically
real (**vyāvahārika**)

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Qualitylessness in Advaita is a denial of limitation, not a denial of nature.
**Named evidence.** Śaṅkara retains **sat-cit-ānanda** as the essential definition
(**svarūpa-lakṣaṇa**) while assigning creatorhood, omniscience and moral governance to the Lord
(**īśvara**) as ultimate reality conditioned by indescribable illusion (**māyā**).
**Analysis.** The division shows that the target of negation is the *relational and objectifiable*
predicate: what would make the absolute one item among knowable items is denied, while what
constitutes it is affirmed as its very being rather than as an added quality.
**Qualification.** Rāmānuja's objection nonetheless retains force, because a nature that cannot be
expressed by any determinate predicate is difficult to distinguish, in practice, from an
unknowable; Advaita must therefore rely on self-luminosity rather than on description.

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

- **2023, Question 6(a), 20 marks** — Rāmānuja's criticism of Śaṅkara's conception of ultimate
  reality and the Lord. This session supplies the whole first half; Session 5 supplies the second.
- **2025, Question 6(b), 15 marks** — the efficient-and-material-cause question presupposes the
  essential/relational distinction taught here.
- Any comparative question: open with the attributes fork in one sentence, then move to the term
  actually asked.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Never write that qualityless means "without any nature" or "blank." That single sentence
   converts a defensible Advaita into the caricature Rāmānuja attacks.
2. Never write that "Rāmānuja accepts both a qualityless and a qualified ultimate reality." He
   rejects a qualityless level above the personal Lord outright.
3. Do not treat being–consciousness–bliss as three properties. In Advaita it expresses one essence,
   not a conjunction of attributes.
4. Do not attribute Madhva's independence–dependence formula to Rāmānuja. Rāmānuja's relation is
   inseparable qualification; Madhva's is dependence with unerased difference.

#### RAPID REVISION

- Two definitions: essential (what it is) · relational (why it grounds the world).
- Advaita: qualityless, sole ultimately real; qualities belong to the Lord at the empirical level.
- Viśiṣṭādvaita: qualified only; infinite auspicious qualities; no higher level.
- Dvaita: qualified, personal, and above all **independent**; everything else dependent.
- 2023 twenty-marker begins here.

---
### SESSION 3 — ULTIMATE REALITY AS EFFICIENT AND MATERIAL CAUSE

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** To ask whether ultimate reality is the world's material cause is to
ask whether the world is made *out of* the absolute or merely made *by* it. Vedānta's schools give
three answers, and the answer each gives is forced by its position on attributes.

**Technical definition.** **Efficient cause (nimitta-kāraṇa)** is the intelligent agency that
brings an effect about; **material cause (upādāna-kāraṇa)** is that out of which the effect is
constituted. Advaita and Viśiṣṭādvaita hold ultimate reality to be both, in different senses;
Dvaita holds God to be efficient cause only, with dependent primordial matter (**prakṛti**) as the
material cause.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

A potter and a pot: the potter is the maker, the clay is the stuff. Two causes, two things. Now
ask what happens if there is nothing besides the maker — no second stuff lying about. Then the
maker must also be the stuff, and the classic Indian image changes from the potter to the
**spider**, which spins the web out of its own body.

But a spider genuinely loses substance when it spins. If the absolute genuinely turned into the
world, it would change — and an absolute that changes is no longer unconditioned. Advaita's answer
is that the absolute is the material basis of the world's *appearance* without undergoing real
change: the rope is the "stuff" of the illusory snake without ever becoming a snake.

Rāmānuja refuses that escape. For him the transformation is real, but it happens to the absolute's
*modes*, not to its essential perfection. Madhva refuses both: God is the maker, and dependent
primordial matter is the stuff, and the two never collapse.

#### VISUAL — THREE ROUTES THROUGH ONE CAUSAL QUESTION

```text
   QUESTION: is ultimate reality the world's MATERIAL cause as well as its EFFICIENT cause?
   (asked directly in 2025, Q6(b), 15 marks)

  ADVAITA ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    premise .... nothing exists independently of ultimate reality
    therefore .. there is NO SECOND MATERIAL out of which the world is made
    so ......... it is BOTH the intelligence behind the world AND the material basis
                 of its appearance
    image ...... the SPIDER spinning the web out of itself
    but ........ qualified by APPARENT TRANSFORMATION (vivarta): it does not really
                 become the world; the world is an appearance dependent on it
    net ........ material cause in the sense of SUBSTRATE, not of transformed stuff

  VIŚIṢṬĀDVAITA ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    both causes, but causation is REAL
    subtle conscious (cit) and non-conscious (acit) modes  ==>  gross manifestation
    ultimate reality remains their controlling SUBSTANTIVE WHOLE
    the transformation belongs to it THROUGH the real transformation of the
    non-conscious mode — NOT through corruption of God's auspicious essence
    cause-state = subtle · effect-state = gross

  DVAITA ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    God = EFFICIENT cause (nimitta-kāraṇa) ONLY
    primordial matter (prakṛti) = REAL DEPENDENT material cause, energised and
    governed by God
    dependence on God is universal — YET God, souls and matter remain really and
    eternally different
    ! therefore "brahman is both efficient and material cause" is TRUE OF ADVAITA
      AND VIŚIṢṬĀDVAITA IN DIFFERENT SENSES, and FALSE of Dvaita

  RIVAL SCHOOLS OUTSIDE VEDĀNTA (one-line contrast bank)
    Sāṃkhya ..... prakṛti is material cause; NO efficient creator-God
    Nyāya ....... God is primarily EFFICIENT; eternal atoms are MATERIAL
    formula ..... Sāṃkhya splits material causality from intelligence;
                  Nyāya splits efficient from material causality;
                  Vedānta REUNITES both in ultimate reality
```

*What the diagram shows: the same two technical terms produce three incompatible cosmologies, and
the 2025 question is answerable only if the Advaita sense of "material cause" is immediately
qualified by apparent transformation.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **Why the question arises at all.** Vedānta frequently defines ultimate reality as both
   efficient cause (**nimitta-kāraṇa**) and material cause (**upādāna-kāraṇa**) of the world. The
   claim is scripturally motivated: if the world came from something else, that something else
   would be a second independent principle, and the Upaniṣadic teaching of one source would fail.
2. **The Advaita route.** Since nothing exists independently of ultimate reality, there can be no
   second material out of which the world is made. It is therefore both the intelligence behind
   the world and the material basis of its appearance. The classic image is the **spider** that
   spins the web out of itself. Śaṅkara then qualifies the material-cause claim through **apparent
   transformation (vivarta)**: ultimate reality does not really transform into the world; the
   world is an appearance dependent on it. Without that qualification the doctrine would import
   change into the changeless.
3. **The Viśiṣṭādvaita route.** Rāmānuja also makes ultimate reality both efficient and material
   cause, but here causation is **real**: subtle conscious and non-conscious modes become gross
   manifestation, while ultimate reality remains their controlling substantive whole. The
   transformation belongs to it through the real transformation of the non-conscious mode, not
   through corruption of God's essential auspicious nature. Cause-state and effect-state are
   subtle and gross states of one qualified whole.
4. **The Dvaita route.** Madhva makes God the **efficient cause (nimitta-kāraṇa)**, not the
   material cause. Primordial matter (**prakṛti**) is the real dependent material cause energized
   and governed by God. Dependence on God is universal, yet God, souls and matter remain really
   and eternally different. Therefore the claim "ultimate reality is both efficient and material
   cause" belongs to Advaita and Viśiṣṭādvaita in different senses, not uniformly to every Vedānta
   school.
5. **The comparative contrast that earns the extra mark.** In Sāṃkhya, primordial matter
   (**prakṛti**) is material cause and there is no efficient creator-God. In Nyāya, God is
   primarily the efficient cause while eternal atoms are the material cause. A compact contrast
   line: **Sāṃkhya splits material causality from intelligence, Nyāya splits efficient from
   material causality, Vedānta reunites both in ultimate reality.**
6. **What "suitable examples" means in the 2025 wording.** The examiner's phrase invites the
   spider–web image for self-derived production and the rope–snake image for dependence without
   real change. Both must be *interpreted*, not merely named: the spider shows that one source can
   supply both agency and material; the rope shows that a substrate can support an appearance it
   never becomes.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **Spider and web** — the standard image for a single source supplying both agency and material.
- **Rope and snake** — the standard image for a substrate that supports an appearance without
  becoming it; it converts "material cause" into "substrate."
- **Milk and curd** — the contrast case of *real* transformation, used to mark off Sāṃkhya's
  position and, in a qualified way, Rāmānuja's.
- **2025, Question 6(b)** — the printed demand: "How is Brahman conceptualised in Advaita
  philosophy as both Nimitta and Upādāna Kāraṇa of the World? Discuss with suitable examples."

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Advaita calls ultimate reality the material cause of the world only in order to deny that there
> is any other material; the moment the claim is made, apparent transformation is invoked to
> ensure that being the world's stuff does not mean becoming it.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

efficient cause (**nimitta-kāraṇa**) · material cause (**upādāna-kāraṇa**) · apparent
transformation (**vivarta**) · real transformation (**pariṇāma**) · spider–web (**ūrṇanābhi**
image) · subtle cause-state and gross effect-state · primordial matter (**prakṛti**) · dependent
(**paratantra**)

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** In Advaita the double causality is a negative thesis before it is a positive one.
**Named evidence.** The spider–web image is invoked to show that one source can supply both agency
and material, and the doctrine is immediately governed by apparent transformation (**vivarta**),
illustrated by the rope that supports the snake-appearance without altering.
**Analysis.** The positive content — "the world is made of ultimate reality" — is therefore best
read as: there is no *second* material principle, and the world's being is borrowed from its
substrate rather than constituted by a genuine modification of it.
**Qualification.** Rāmānuja replies that a substrate which never changes is not a material cause in
any recognisable sense, and Madhva replies that dependence is fully explained by an efficient cause
governing a real dependent matter; the Advaitin's answer therefore holds only if sublatability is
accepted as a criterion of reality.

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

- **2025, Question 6(b), 15 marks** — directly and exclusively this session, plus one paragraph of
  apparent transformation from Session 8.
- **2022, Question 5(e), 10 marks** — the apparent-transformation/real-transformation relation
  taught here is the doctrinal base of that question.
- Use the Sāṃkhya and Nyāya contrast lines in any twenty-marker that mentions causation.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Do not write "all Vedānta schools hold that Brahman is both efficient and material cause."
   Madhva denies material causality of God explicitly.
2. Do not present the spider image without the apparent-transformation qualification; unqualified,
   it commits Advaita to real change.
3. Do not confuse the *cause-state/effect-state* language of Viśiṣṭādvaita with Advaita's
   substrate language; the first asserts real transformation of modes, the second denies real
   transformation altogether.
4. Do not say Sāṃkhya has "no cause" of the world. It has a material cause and denies an efficient
   creator-God; those are different claims.

#### RAPID REVISION

- Advaita: both causes; spider image; but material = substrate under apparent transformation.
- Viśiṣṭādvaita: both causes; real transformation of modes; essence uncorrupted.
- Dvaita: efficient only; dependent primordial matter is the material cause.
- One-line bank: Sāṃkhya splits material from intelligence · Nyāya splits efficient from material ·
  Vedānta reunites both.

---
### SESSION 4 — SIX MEANS OF VALID KNOWLEDGE, AND WHY TESTIMONY IS PRIMARY

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** A means of valid knowledge (**pramāṇa**) is a way of getting a
cognition you are entitled to trust. The Vedānta schools accept different lists, and the length of
each list is dictated by what that school needs to prove.

**Technical definition.** Advaita accepts **six means of valid knowledge**, following the Bhāṭṭa
Mīmāṃsā list: perception (**pratyakṣa**), inference (**anumāna**), comparison (**upamāna**),
verbal testimony (**śabda**), postulation (**arthāpatti**) and non-apprehension
(**anupalabdhi**). Viśiṣṭādvaita and Dvaita accept three each. Advaita additionally holds that all
six operate only at the empirical (**vyāvahārika**) level.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Instruments are chosen by the job. A thermometer cannot weigh; a balance cannot measure heat. Ask
what kind of object you are after, and the instrument list follows.

Now ask after an object that is not an object at all — the very consciousness in which all
objectification occurs. No sense can reach it, because senses reach things placed before them. No
inference can reach it, because inference needs a mark regularly found with the thing, and there is
no observed instance of the absolute from which such a rule could be drawn. Worse, an inferred
absolute would be an inferred *object*, which is exactly what it is not.

That leaves testimony — and only a very particular testimony, the great sentences of the Upaniṣads.
But this creates a puzzle Advaita must answer: if every instrument presupposes knower, means and
known, and the final truth has none of these, how can any instrument reach it? The answer is that
scripture works like a clearing-nut dropped in muddy water: it settles the mud and settles with it.

#### VISUAL — WHY THE LIST IS SIX, AND WHY ONLY ONE OF THEM REACHES THE ABSOLUTE

```text
   ADVAITA'S SIX INSTRUMENTS (following the Bhāṭṭa Mīmāṃsā list)
  ┌──────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┐
  │ perception       │ immediate cognition through sense-    │ "this pot is blue"        │
  │ (pratyakṣa)      │ contact with a present object         │                           │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │ inference        │ unperceived fact via a perceived mark │ smoke on the hill => fire  │
  │ (anumāna)        │ (liṅga) + concomitance (vyāpti)       │                           │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │ comparison       │ fresh cognition of an absent familiar │ gavaya seen => the remem-  │
  │ (upamāna)        │ object's similarity to what is seen    │ bered cow is like this    │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │ verbal testimony │ valid cognition from reliable words,   │ the great sentence teaches│
  │ (śabda)          │ supremely the AUTHORLESS (apauruṣeya)  │ self–absolute identity    │
  │                  │ Veda in matters beyond ordinary means  │                           │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │ postulation      │ presumption of an unseen fact needed   │ fat Devadatta does not eat│
  │ (arthāpatti)     │ to reconcile incompatible known facts  │ by day => he eats at night │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │ non-apprehension │ cognition of ABSENCE through non-      │ "there is no pot on the   │
  │ (anupalabdhi)    │ perception under proper conditions     │ floor"                    │
  └──────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────┘
                                     |
        EVERY instrument presupposes the triad  knower – means – known
                    (pramātṛ – pramāṇa – prameya)
                                     |
        that triad presupposes DUALITY  =>  all six work only at the
        EMPIRICAL level (vyāvahārika); at the ULTIMATE level (pāramārthika)
        there is no second object, no separate knower, no instrument
                                     |
   REFLEXIVE PUZZLE ─► if all instruments are only empirically valid, how does
                       testimony establish the ULTIMATE truth?
   REPLY ───────────► scripture removes ignorance and then loses operative
                       relevance ALONG WITH the ignorance it removed
   IMAGES ──────────► the clearing-nut (kataka) that settles the mud and settles
                       with it · the stick that stirs the pyre and is consumed in it

   WHY NOT THE OTHER FIVE?
     perception ..... the absolute is qualityless, non-objectifiable, not sensed
     inference ...... no perceived instance, no mark uniquely co-present; and an
                      inferred absolute would become an inferred OBJECT (prameya)
     comparison, postulation, non-apprehension ..... derivative / empirically
                      parasitic: similarity, presumption and absence all operate
                      INSIDE the field of knowable objects
   => Śaṅkara: the absolute is śāstra-yoni / śāstra-pramāṇaka — known through
     scripture, not as an ordinary object of sense or inference
```

*What the diagram shows: the list is generous at the empirical level and ruthless at the ultimate
one. Advaita does not shrink epistemology; it relocates it.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **The six, precisely.** Perception is immediate cognition generated through sense-contact with a
   present object. Inference is knowledge of an unperceived fact through a perceived mark
   (**liṅga**) and universal concomitance (**vyāpti**). Comparison is the fresh cognition of an
   absent familiar object's similarity to a presently perceived object, following the Bhāṭṭa
   account accepted by Advaita. Verbal testimony is valid cognition from reliable words, supremely
   the authorless (**apauruṣeya**) Veda in matters beyond ordinary means. Postulation is the
   presumption of an unseen fact required to reconcile otherwise incompatible known facts.
   Non-apprehension is cognition of absence through non-perception under proper conditions.
2. **The two-level qualification.** Every instrument presupposes the triad knower–means–known
   (**pramātṛ–pramāṇa–prameya**). Since that triad presupposes duality, all six function only at
   the empirical (**vyāvahārika**) level. At the ultimate (**pāramārthika**) level there is no
   second object to be known, no separate knower and no instrument of knowing; in the realisation
   of the absolute the whole apparatus is sublated.
3. **The reflexive puzzle and its reply.** If all instruments are only empirically valid, how can
   testimony establish the ultimate truth? Advaita treats revelation as an instrument that removes
   ignorance and then loses operative relevance along with the ignorance it removed. Classical
   teaching compares it to the **clearing-nut (kataka)** that settles muddy water and settles with
   the mud, or to a stick used to stir a funeral pyre which is itself consumed in the fire.
   Scripture is therefore not an eternal second reality alongside the absolute; it is the empirical
   ladder by which the non-empirical truth is recognised.
4. **Why testimony alone reaches the absolute.** Perception cannot, because the absolute is
   qualityless, non-objectifiable and not an object of the senses. Inference cannot, because it
   requires a perceived concomitance and a present mark, and there is no perceived instance of the
   absolute and no mark uniquely co-present with it; further, any inference would turn it into an
   inferred object (**prameya**), contradicting its status as the non-objective ground of all
   knowing. Comparison, postulation and non-apprehension are derivative or empirically parasitic:
   similarity, presumption and absence-cognition all operate within the field of knowable objects.
   Hence Śaṅkara treats the absolute as **śāstra-yoni / śāstra-pramāṇaka** — known through
   scripture. Only testimony, specifically the great sentences (**mahāvākya**) *tat tvam asi*,
   *ahaṃ brahmāsmi*, *prajñānaṃ brahma* and *ayam ātmā brahma*, can generate the liberating
   knowledge that removes ignorance.
5. **The semantic machinery in outline** (developed fully in Session 12). The Advaita reading of
   *tat tvam asi* depends on **abandoning-and-retaining implication (jahad-ajahal-lakṣaṇā)**, also
   called **part-abandoning implication (bhāga-tyāga-lakṣaṇā)**. The direct meaning
   (**vācyārtha**) of *tat* is the omniscient, remote Lord; of *tvam*, the limited, immediate
   individual. These conflict, so the incompatible portions are abandoned and the common implied
   meaning (**lakṣyārtha**) — pure consciousness — is retained. The canonical parallel is
   *so'yaṃ devadattaḥ*, "this is that Devadatta."
6. **The fault-line with earlier Mīmāṃsā.** For earlier Mīmāṃsā, Vedic authority is primarily
   authority for injunction (**vidhi**) concerning duty (**dharma**), and statements of
   accomplished fact are normally read as subordinate to ritual action. For Advaita, the
   Upaniṣadic statements about accomplished reality (**siddha-vastu**) are independently and
   supremely valid, because knowledge (**jñāna**) is not an act to be performed (**kārya**) but
   cognition of what is already the case. The Mīmāṃsaka objects: if the Veda is for action, how
   can a sentence that merely states identity produce liberation? The Advaitin answers: bondage is
   ignorance, not absence of ritual action, so the direct remover must be knowledge, and the great
   sentence has precisely that knowledge-producing function. The fault-line is therefore not merely
   ritual versus metaphysics, but whether Vedic sentences can independently reveal a non-actional
   accomplished reality.
7. **Three-school contrast on means of knowledge.**

| School | Count | Accepted instruments | Philosophical consequence |
|---|---:|---|---|
| **Advaita** | 6 | perception, inference, comparison, testimony, postulation, non-apprehension | A wide epistemology is retained empirically, but testimony alone reveals the non-objective absolute and is then sublated. |
| **Viśiṣṭādvaita** | 3 | perception, inference, testimony; Rāmānuja reduces the rest to these | Rāmānuja **accepts** indeterminate perception (**nirvikalpaka**) but reinterprets it: it is the *first* cognition of an individual already apprehended with its structure or configuration (**saṃsthāna**), while determinate perception (**savikalpaka**) is the second and later cognition that additionally recognises the generic character as recurrent. What he rejects is cognition of a **wholly qualifierless (nirviśeṣa) object** — *sarvaṃ vijñānaṃ saviśeṣam*, all cognition is of the qualified — so a qualityless absolute is not establishable. |
| **Dvaita** | 3 | perception, inference, scriptural tradition (**āgama**); Madhva gives special weight to scripture, to the **kevala-pramāṇa / anu-pramāṇa** distinction, and to the **witness (sākṣin)** as ultimate internal validator of cognition | The independent ultimate standard of truth is separated from dependent instruments; expositions of the distinction vary, but the consequence is realist theism rather than non-dual sublation. |

8. **Comparative insight.** A school's instrument-count tracks its metaphysics: Advaita needs
   testimony to disclose the non-objective absolute, Viśiṣṭādvaita's qualified realism requires all
   cognition to be of the qualified, and Dvaita's realist theism requires instruments that secure
   real difference.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **The four great sentences** — *tat tvam asi*, *ahaṃ brahmāsmi*, *prajñānaṃ brahma*, *ayam ātmā
  brahma*.
- **The clearing-nut (kataka)** and the **stick consumed in the pyre** — the two standard images of
  a self-cancelling instrument.
- ***sarvaṃ vijñānaṃ saviśeṣam*** — Rāmānuja's compressed epistemological thesis that all cognition
  is of the qualified.
- **Devadatta eating at night** — the standard example of postulation.
- **The gavaya (wild ox)** — the standard example of comparison.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Advaita's epistemology is deliberately generous and deliberately self-limiting: it admits six
> instruments so that ordinary life is fully accounted for, and then confines all six to the
> empirical order so that the one instrument which reaches the absolute can dissolve along with the
> ignorance it removes.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

means of valid knowledge (**pramāṇa**) · knower–means–known (**pramātṛ–pramāṇa–prameya**) ·
authorless (**apauruṣeya**) · scripture-sourced (**śāstra-yoni / śāstra-pramāṇaka**) · great
sentence (**mahāvākya**) · clearing-nut (**kataka**) · accomplished reality (**siddha-vastu**) ·
indeterminate perception (**nirvikalpaka**) · determinate perception (**savikalpaka**) ·
qualifierless (**nirviśeṣa**) · configuration (**saṃsthāna**) · scriptural tradition (**āgama**) ·
witness (**sākṣin**)

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Advaita's restriction of all instruments to the empirical level is a doctrinal
requirement, not an evasion.
**Named evidence.** Every instrument presupposes the triad knower–means–known
(**pramātṛ–pramāṇa–prameya**), and the clearing-nut image is used to explain how testimony can
operate and then cease.
**Analysis.** If the final truth is non-dual, an instrument that survived realisation would leave a
residual duality of knower and known, so the self-cancellation of testimony is entailed by the
thesis rather than added to protect it.
**Qualification.** The reply is nevertheless internally circular for an opponent: Rāmānuja can
insist that an instrument valid only until it works is indistinguishable from an instrument whose
validity was never established, and Advaita must answer by appeal to self-luminosity rather than
by a further instrument.

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

- **2023, Question 5(c), 10 marks** — the great sentence question is answered from §5 here plus
  Session 12.
- **2021, Question 7(a), 20 marks** — the error question requires the instrument framework
  established here before the theory of error can be argued.
- Use §7 as the standing comparative table for any "compare the three schools" demand.
- **Boundary note.** The 2018 Question 8(a) twenty-marker on self-luminosity and intrinsic validity
  is primary-owned by Mīmāṃsā; Vedānta contributes the Advaita position only. Do not claim it as a
  Vedānta question.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Do not write that Advaita accepts three instruments. Six is the Advaita count; three is
   Viśiṣṭādvaita and Dvaita.
2. Do not write that Rāmānuja rejects indeterminate perception. He accepts it and redefines it;
   what he rejects is cognition of a wholly qualifierless object.
3. Do not say Advaita "denies the validity of perception." It denies ultimacy, not empirical
   validity — the distinction is worth marks.
4. Do not treat the four great sentences as a closed liturgical list to be recited. Name one and
   show what it does semantically.
5. Do not confuse Madhva's witness (**sākṣin**) with Advaita's witness (**sākṣī**). The same word
   hides opposed metaphysics: for Madhva it validates real difference, for Advaita it is non-dual
   consciousness.

#### RAPID REVISION

- Six for Advaita, three for the other two.
- All six are empirical only; the triad of knower–means–known is the reason.
- Testimony alone reaches the absolute, then self-cancels — clearing-nut.
- Rāmānuja: all cognition is of the qualified; Madhva: scripture plus the witness.

---
### SESSION 5 — THE LORD (ĪŚVARA) AND THE ORIGINAL–REFLECTION MODEL

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** The Lord (**īśvara**) is God considered as creator, ruler and object
of worship. The Vedāntic question is not whether God exists but where God stands in the order of
reality: is God the final truth, or a supremely real appearance of something still more ultimate?

**Technical definition.** In Advaita the Lord is **ultimate reality conditioned by indescribable
illusion (māyā-upahita brahman)**, equivalently **māyā-viśiṣṭa brahman** — fully real at the
empirical level and sublated at the ultimate level. In Viśiṣṭādvaita and Dvaita, the Lord simply
*is* ultimate reality, with no higher level above him.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

One face, many mirrors. The face is unaffected by how many mirrors there are, by whether a mirror
is cracked, tilted, dusty or moving. Each mirror yields an image conditioned by that mirror, and
none of the images alters the face.

Advaita uses this to explain how one consciousness appears as a cosmic Lord and as countless
finite selves without dividing. The mirror is ignorance; the images are conditioned appearances;
the face is untouched. Notice at once what this does to religion: if God is an image in the cosmic
mirror, worship is real and effective at the level where mirrors operate, but it cannot be the last
word.

Rāmānuja finds this intolerable — not sentimentally, but philosophically. If the personal God
belongs to a level that will be cancelled, then every scriptural description of God is provisional
truth, and devotion is a ladder to be kicked away. Madhva goes further: the Lord is independent and
everything else dependent, so no cancellation is even conceivable.

#### VISUAL — WHERE THE LORD STANDS, AND THE THREE ADVAITA MODELS OF INDIVIDUATION

```text
   THE LORD (īśvara) — PLACED BY EACH SCHOOL
  ADVAITA ....... ultimate reality CONDITIONED BY illusion (māyā-upahita brahman)
                  omniscient · omnipotent · moral governor · creator of the empirical world
                  FULLY REAL at the empirical level (vyāvahārika)
                  YET sublated into qualityless ultimate reality at the ultimate level
                  => theism is neither false in a crude sense nor final in the last sense
  VIŚIṢṬĀDVAITA . the Lord IS ultimate reality — supreme personal Viṣṇu-Nārāyaṇa
                  no qualityless level above him · qualities real and eternal
                  IMMANENT because world and souls are his body; TRANSCENDENT because he
                  exceeds them
  DVAITA ........ Viṣṇu absolutely supreme, alone independent and self-sufficient
                  all selves and the world depend on him ontologically, morally,
                  soteriologically · difference NEVER erased by liberation

   ORIGINAL–REFLECTION MODEL (bimba-pratibimba-vāda) — asked 2025, Q8(a), 20 marks
        ORIGINAL (bimba) = pure consciousness / ultimate reality
                    │
        reflected in ▼ a conditioning medium (internal organ under ignorance)
        REFLECTION (pratibimba) = the empirical self
        * the original is UNAFFECTED by defect, motion or multiplicity in the media,
          as one face is unchanged though many mirrors yield differing images
        * SOME later formulations: reflection in TOTAL cosmic appearance = the Lord;
          reflections in INDIVIDUAL ignorance / internal organs = the selves
        ? OTHER formulations make the Lord the original relative to the self, or prefer
          limitation / appearance models instead
        ! do NOT write that the Lord is simultaneously "the original" and "consciousness
          reflected in illusion" without naming the relative or later model being used
                    │
        SOTERIOLOGICAL POINT (the half of the 2025 question most answers miss)
        the reflected image has only DERIVATIVE reality => when ignorance is removed the
        finite self is known NEVER to have had independent reality apart from the original
        => liberation = RECOGNITION of non-difference, not movement, merging or destruction
        ! limit: a formless original, formless medium and non-dual reality make LITERAL
          reflection impossible; use the model for suggestive value, not optical physics

   THREE ADVAITA MODELS OF THE SAME PROBLEM
     limitation (avaccheda-vāda) ..... one space appearing as pot-space (ghaṭākāśa);
                                       emphasis on DELIMITATION
     reflection (pratibimba-vāda) .... consciousness reflected in ignorance;
                                       emphasis on DERIVATIVE APPEARANCE
     semblance (ābhāsa-vāda) ......... the self is a mere semblance (ābhāsa);
                                       emphasis on ONTOLOGICAL THINNESS

   THE 2022 HIERARCHY (Q8(a), 20 marks)
     witness (sākṣī) ..... pure witnessing consciousness · unaffected · immediate ·
                           non-objectifiable — CLOSEST to the ultimate standpoint
     individual (jīva) ... consciousness reflected/limited by ignorance — empirical BONDAGE
     the Lord (īśvara) ... consciousness conditioned by illusion — empirical COSMIC ORDER
```

*What the diagram shows: one metaphor is doing three jobs at once — explaining individuation,
placing God, and defining liberation as recognition. That is why the 2025 question asks for the
model **and** its soteriological significance.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **Advaita's placement.** The Lord is ultimate reality conditioned by illusion; the standard
   expressions are **māyā-upahita brahman** or **māyā-viśiṣṭa brahman**. He is the omniscient,
   omnipotent, moral governor and creator of the empirical world, and fully real at the empirical
   level. Yet at the ultimate level the Lord too is sublated into qualityless ultimate reality.
   Hence theism in Advaita is neither false in a crude sense nor ultimate in the final sense.
2. **Viśiṣṭādvaita's placement.** For Rāmānuja the Lord is not a lower appearance. He **is**
   ultimate reality — the supreme personal Viṣṇu–Nārāyaṇa. There is no qualityless level above
   him; his infinite auspicious qualities are real and eternal; he is immanent because world and
   souls are his body, and transcendent because he exceeds them.
3. **Dvaita's placement.** In Madhva, Viṣṇu is the absolutely supreme Lord, alone independent and
   self-sufficient. All selves and the entire world depend upon him ontologically, morally and
   soteriologically, and the difference between the Lord and all else is never erased by
   liberation.
4. **The original–reflection model (bimba-pratibimba-vāda)**, asked in 2025 as a twenty-marker. The
   basic model takes ultimate reality or pure consciousness as the original (**bimba**) and the
   empirical self as its reflection (**pratibimba**) in the internal organ conditioned by
   ignorance. The original remains unaffected by defects, motion or multiplicity in the reflecting
   media, just as one face remains unchanged while mirrors yield many differently conditioned
   images. Some later Advaita formulations distinguish the reflection of consciousness in total
   cosmic appearance as the Lord and its reflections in individual ignorance or internal organs as
   individual selves. Other formulations treat the Lord as original relative to the individual, or
   prefer limitation and appearance models instead; these are later explanatory alternatives, not
   one uniform proposition attributable without qualification to Śaṅkara. Do not write that the
   Lord is simultaneously "the original" and "consciousness reflected in illusion" without naming
   the relative or later model being used.
5. **Its soteriological significance.** The theory is not an ontological curiosity; it serves
   liberation. The reflected image has only derivative reality. When ignorance is removed, the
   finite self is known never to have possessed independent reality apart from original
   consciousness. Liberation is therefore recognition of the non-difference of reflected
   consciousness from its original, not the physical movement, merging or destruction of one
   substance by another. The metaphor protects the absolute's non-division; but critics object that
   a formless original, a formless medium and a non-dual reality make literal reflection
   impossible, so the model should be used for suggestive value rather than pushed as optical
   physics.
6. **The two rival Advaita models.** Under **limitation (avaccheda-vāda)** the finite self is
   ultimate reality as though limited by adjuncts, like one space appearing as pot-space
   (**ghaṭākāśa**); the emphasis falls on delimitation rather than reflection. Under **semblance
   (ābhāsa-vāda)** the individual is a mere semblance (**ābhāsa**) of ultimate reality; the
   emphasis falls on the ontological thinness of individuality. Reflection, limitation and
   semblance are three solutions to one problem, and naming all three shows internal plurality
   within Advaita without losing the common conclusion.
7. **The 2022 hierarchy: Lord, individual and witness.** The Lord is ultimate reality
   reflected or conditioned by illusion — empirically real and cosmically authoritative. The
   individual (**jīva**) is ultimate reality reflected or limited by ignorance — empirically
   finite, agentive and bound. The witness (**sākṣī**) is pure witnessing consciousness,
   unaffected, immediate and non-objectifiable. The hierarchy is crucial: the witness is closest
   to the ultimate standpoint, the individual belongs to empirical bondage, and the Lord belongs
   to cosmic-ordering empirical reality.
8. **Rāmānuja's critique of Advaita's Lord.** If the Lord belongs only to the empirical level,
   devotion becomes merely preparatory; scriptural descriptions of God become lower truth instead
   of ultimate truth; religious life is structurally subordinated to non-personal absolutism.
   Rāmānuja objects that this hollows out devotion and makes scriptural theism provisional rather
   than final. This is one half of the 2023 twenty-marker; the other half is the critique of the
   qualityless absolute taught in Session 2.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **māyā-upahita brahman / māyā-viśiṣṭa brahman** — the two standard Advaita expressions for the
  Lord's status.
- **The face and the mirrors** — the canonical image of the original–reflection model.
- **Pot-space (ghaṭākāśa)** — the canonical image of the limitation model.
- **2025, Question 8(a)** — "Discuss the idea of Bimba-pratibimbavāda as presented in Vedānta
  philosophy along with its soteriological significance."
- **2022, Question 8(a)** — the printed verse *Brahma satyam jaganmithyā, Jīvo Brahmaiva nāparaḥ*,
  used to frame the Lord–individual–witness hierarchy.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The original–reflection model is not a picture of how consciousness divides but an argument that
> it never did: what is reflected borrows all its being from the original, so liberation can only
> be the collapse of a claim, never the movement of a substance.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

Lord (**īśvara**) · conditioned by illusion (**māyā-upahita / māyā-viśiṣṭa**) · original
(**bimba**) · reflection (**pratibimba**) · original–reflection model (**bimba-pratibimba-vāda**) ·
limitation model (**avaccheda-vāda**) · pot-space (**ghaṭākāśa**) · semblance model
(**ābhāsa-vāda**) · witness (**sākṣī**) · empirically real (**vyāvahārika**) · ultimately real
(**pāramārthika**)

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** The original–reflection model earns its place in Advaita because it converts a
metaphysical thesis into a soteriological one.
**Named evidence.** One face in many mirrors leaves the face unaltered by cracked, tilted or moving
media; correspondingly, defects of the internal organ under ignorance do not touch original
consciousness.
**Analysis.** Since the reflection possesses no being of its own, removing the medium does not
destroy anything real; therefore liberation is the recognition of a non-difference that always
obtained, which is exactly what Advaita needs if bondage is to be ignorance rather than an event.
**Qualification.** The model cannot be pressed as optics — a formless original and a formless
medium cannot literally reflect — and later Advaitins themselves differ over whether the Lord is
original or reflection, so the model should be presented as an explanatory family with named
variants rather than a single settled doctrine.

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

- **2025, Question 8(a), 20 marks** — the model plus its soteriological significance; both halves
  are here.
- **2022, Question 8(a), 20 marks** — the Lord–individual–witness hierarchy; combine with Sessions
  6 and 7.
- **2023, Question 6(a), 20 marks** — the second half of Rāmānuja's criticism; combine with Session
  2.
- **2020, Question 8(c), 15 marks** — the witness material feeds directly into the individual and
  witness relation.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Do not write that Advaita "denies God." It places God at the empirical level and calls that
   level fully real there.
2. Do not attribute every later reflection formulation to Śaṅkara himself; name the model and note
   that expositions vary.
3. Do not answer the 2025 question with ontology alone. Half the marks lie in the soteriological
   significance.
4. Do not use "reflection" and "limitation" as synonyms; they are distinct Advaita strategies with
   different emphases.
5. Do not claim Rāmānuja's Lord is "saguṇa Brahman" in Advaita's sense. For Rāmānuja there is no
   contrasting nirguṇa level for the term to mean anything relative to.

#### RAPID REVISION

- Advaita: Lord = ultimate reality conditioned by illusion; empirically real, ultimately sublated.
- Viśiṣṭādvaita and Dvaita: the Lord simply *is* ultimate reality; no higher level.
- Original–reflection: face and mirrors; derivative reality; liberation = recognition.
- Three models: limitation (pot-space) · reflection (mirror) · semblance.
- 2022 hierarchy: witness > individual > Lord in nearness to the ultimate standpoint.

---
### SESSION 6 — THE TRUE SELF (ĀTMAN) AND THE WITNESS (SĀKṢĪ)

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** The true self (**ātman**) is what you are when everything you merely
*have* — body, senses, mind, biography — has been set aside. The Vedānta question is whether what
remains is identical with ultimate reality, a real mode of it, or a permanently distinct dependent
reality.

**Technical definition.** In Advaita the true self is identical in essence with ultimate reality
and is characterised as **witness-consciousness (sākṣī)**: self-luminous, non-objectifiable, and
the condition of all experience. In Viśiṣṭādvaita it is a real, eternal, atomic (**aṇu**) knower
whose **attributive consciousness (dharma-bhūta-jñāna)** reveals objects. In Dvaita it is a real
eternal self whose difference from God is fundamental.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

You say "I was angry, then calm, then curious." Three states passed; something registered all
three. Whatever registered them cannot be identical with any one of them, or it would have vanished
with it.

Push the same test through sleep. On waking, people report: "I slept well; I knew nothing." That is
a *report about* a period of no cognition — which is odd, because a report needs something that was
there to make it. Advaita takes this as evidence for a witness that is not any particular mental
mode.

Then comes the decisive step, and it is a step no other school takes. Advaita says the witness is
not merely *your* inner observer, one per person; it is consciousness as such, and therefore
identical with ultimate reality. Rāmānuja accepts the enduring self and denies the identity. Madhva
accepts the enduring self and makes its difference from God permanent. One argument, three
destinations.

#### VISUAL — THE THREE STATES, THE WITNESS, AND THE THREE SETTLEMENTS

```text
   THE STATE-ANALYSIS ARGUMENT (Advaita)
     waking (jāgrat) ......... an EXTERNAL object-world is presented
     dream (svapna) .......... INTERNALLY generated objects are presented
     deep sleep (suṣupti) .... NO manifest subject–object cognition
                                    │
     report on waking: "I slept peacefully; I knew nothing"
     => presupposes CONTINUITY through deep sleep
     => supports a WITNESS not identical with any particular mental mode
                                    │
     the FOURTH standpoint (turīya) is NOT a fourth episode after the three;
     it is the non-dual consciousness that UNDERLIES and ILLUMINES all three
                                    │
     ! caution: this is an argument WITHIN Advaita, not a neutral proof.
       Rāmānuja and Nyāya retain an enduring self while REJECTING its identity
       with non-dual ultimate reality.

   WITNESS-CONSCIOUSNESS (sākṣī) — WHAT IT IS AND IS NOT
     + illumines all mental states, never altered by them
     + pleasure, pain, doubt, memory, desire are WITNESSED, not essential to it
     + not a passive blank but the CONDITION for all experience
     + self-luminous: manifestation belongs to consciousness as its very nature
     x not one more knower inside the person
     => Advaita's elegant split: CHANGING PSYCHOLOGY vs CHANGELESS CONSCIOUSNESS

   THREE SETTLEMENTS OF ONE ENDURING SELF
  ┌────────────────┬───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
  │ School         │ Status of the true self   │ Relation to ultimate reality     │
  ├────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Advaita        │ pure consciousness        │ IDENTICAL in essence             │
  │ Viśiṣṭādvaita  │ real eternal conscious    │ INSEPARABLE MODE, not identical  │
  │                │ self, atomic (aṇu)        │                                  │
  │ Dvaita         │ real eternal dependent    │ ETERNALLY DIFFERENT              │
  │                │ self                      │                                  │
  └────────────────┴───────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

   RĀMĀNUJA'S MECHANISM — HOW AN ATOMIC SELF KNOWS A WIDE WORLD
     substantive knower = ATOMIC (aṇu), self-luminous
     attributive consciousness (dharma-bhūta-jñāna) = its quality, which REVEALS objects
       · CONTRACTS under karmic obstruction
       · EXPANDS without obstruction in liberation
     => atomic size does NOT confine cognitive range
     in deep sleep the self persists, but object-manifesting cognition is unexpressed
     because no object is presented

   WHY THIS DECIDES EVERYTHING DOWNSTREAM
     self IS ultimate reality ......... liberation = RECOGNITION
     self is a MODE of it ............. liberation = COMMUNION
     self is permanently DIFFERENT .... liberation = ETERNAL SERVICE
```

*What the diagram shows: the self is the pressure-point where each school's ethics and religion
become visible. The three columns of the table are literally the three conclusions of Session 13.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **Advaita: the true self is ultimate reality.** The great sentences such as *tat tvam asi* and
   *ahaṃ brahmāsmi* express this identity. The empirical self appears individual because of
   ignorance, but its essence is non-dual consciousness. The true self is self-luminous, not an
   object among objects.
2. **The witness (sākṣī).** Advaita characterises the true self as witness-consciousness. It
   illumines all mental states but is never altered by them; pleasure, pain, doubt, memory and
   desire are witnessed, not essential to the self. The witness is not a passive blank but the
   condition for all experience. This gives Advaita its elegant distinction between changing
   psychology and changeless consciousness.
3. **The three states and the fourth standpoint.** The waking state (**jāgrat**) presents an
   external object-world; the dream state (**svapna**) presents internally generated objects; the
   deep-sleep state (**suṣupti**) lacks manifest subject–object cognition. Advaita argues that the
   later report "I slept peacefully; I knew nothing" presupposes continuity through deep sleep and
   supports a witness not identical with any particular mental mode. The fourth standpoint
   (**turīya**) is not merely another temporal episode after the first three; it is the non-dual
   consciousness that underlies and illumines all three.
4. **The honest qualification.** Memory after sleep is an argument *within* Advaita, not a neutral
   proof accepted by every school. Rāmānuja and Nyāya retain an enduring self while rejecting
   Advaita's identity of that self with non-dual ultimate reality. Self-luminosity belongs to
   consciousness as the condition of manifestation; episodic mental cognitions, objects and the
   empirical knower remain within the subject–object structure.
5. **Viśiṣṭādvaita: real but non-identical.** Rāmānuja accepts the reality, eternity and
   consciousness of the self. It is an atomic subject (**aṇu**) and self-luminous knower, not an
   impersonal mass of pure consciousness. Its **attributive consciousness (dharma-bhūta-jñāna)**
   reveals objects, can contract under karmic obstruction, and expands without obstruction in
   liberation. Atomic size therefore does not confine the range of cognition: the self remains the
   substantive knower while its attributive consciousness can be pervasive. In deep sleep the self
   persists, but object-manifesting cognition is unexpressed because no object is presented; in
   liberation its cognitive reach becomes unobstructed. It is a mode (**prakāra**) of ultimate
   reality, not identical with it — dependent, but not illusory. Selfhood is thus preserved without
   making the self an independent absolute.
6. **Dvaita: eternal difference.** In Madhva the self is real, eternal and dependent upon God.
   Difference from God is fundamental, not produced by ignorance. The self never becomes God, even
   in liberation. This provides the metaphysical basis of devotion as a permanent relation rather
   than a provisional means.
7. **The comparative formula.**

| School | Status of the true self | Relation to ultimate reality / the Lord |
|---|---|---|
| Advaita | pure consciousness | identical in essence |
| Viśiṣṭādvaita | real eternal conscious self, atomic (**aṇu**) | inseparable mode, not identical |
| Dvaita | real eternal dependent self | eternally different |

8. **Why the self is the decisive debate-point.** If the self is ultimate reality, liberation is
   recognition. If it is a mode of ultimate reality, liberation is communion. If it is permanently
   different, liberation is eternal service. Each school's ethics and religion follow from its
   account of the self, which is why examiners can test soteriology by asking about the self, and
   test the self by asking about soteriology.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **tat tvam asi** and **ahaṃ brahmāsmi** — the identity sentences.
- **"I slept peacefully; I knew nothing"** — the deep-sleep report used as the Advaita argument for
  a continuous witness.
- **dharma-bhūta-jñāna** — Rāmānuja's technical device that lets an atomic self know a wide world.
- **turīya** — the fourth standpoint that is not a fourth state.
- **2020, Question 8(c)** — "Discuss the nature and relationship of Jīva and Jīva-sākṣī according
  to non-dualism."

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Every Vedānta school agrees that something in us endures; they divide on whether that enduring
> witness is the absolute itself, a real mode of the absolute, or a real dependent that must never
> be mistaken for it — and the whole difference between recognition, communion and service follows
> from that single choice.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS
