true self (**ātman**) · witness (**sākṣī**) · waking (**jāgrat**) · dream (**svapna**) · deep sleep
(**suṣupti**) · fourth standpoint (**turīya**) · self-luminous (**svayaṃ-prakāśa**) · atomic
(**aṇu**) · attributive consciousness (**dharma-bhūta-jñāna**) · mode (**prakāra**) · karmic
obstruction

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Advaita's witness is a structural claim about the conditions of experience, not a report
of an inner spectator.
**Named evidence.** The three states are surveyed and the deep-sleep report "I slept peacefully; I
knew nothing" is used to show continuity across a period without object-cognition; the fourth
standpoint (**turīya**) is then defined as what illumines all three rather than as a fourth
episode.
**Analysis.** If the witness were one more mental state it would be absent in the state where no
states are manifest, so the argument's force lies precisely in the fact that manifestation itself
does not lapse even when the manifested does.
**Qualification.** The inference is nonetheless school-relative: Rāmānuja grants an enduring
self-luminous knower and explains the same deep-sleep data by unexpressed attributive
consciousness, so the argument supports an enduring self more securely than it supports non-dual
identity.

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

- **2020, Question 8(c), 15 marks** — the nature and relationship of the individual and the witness
  in non-dualism; use §2, §3 and §4 here plus Session 7's models.
- **2022, Question 8(a), 20 marks** — the witness is one of the three terms whose ontological status
  is demanded.
- Use the comparative table in any question that names the self, the individual or liberation.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Do not describe the witness as "the soul watching the mind" as if it were a second person inside
   the body; it is consciousness as the condition of manifestation.
2. Do not present the deep-sleep argument as a neutral proof. Say explicitly that rival schools
   accept the enduring self and reject the identity.
3. Do not call the fourth standpoint a fourth state in a temporal series.
4. Do not say Rāmānuja's atomic self can know only a small region; attributive consciousness is
   precisely the device that prevents that consequence.
5. Do not merge the true self and the individual self in an Advaita answer without saying you are
   doing so; Session 7 depends on keeping them distinct.

#### RAPID REVISION

- Witness illumines, never alters; changing psychology versus changeless consciousness.
- Three states plus the fourth standpoint that underlies them.
- Advaita identical · Viśiṣṭādvaita inseparable mode · Dvaita eternally different.
- Rāmānuja's device: atomic knower + attributive consciousness that contracts and expands.

---
### SESSION 7 — THE INDIVIDUAL SELF (JĪVA): THREE NON-DUAL MODELS AND TWO REALIST RIVALS

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** The individual self (**jīva**) is you as a bound, embodied, acting
and transmigrating person — the self that has a history. The true self (**ātman**) is the self in
its essential nature. Many answers improve immediately simply by marking this distinction.

**Technical definition.** In Advaita the individual is ultimate reality appearing finite through
ignorance, explained by three models: limitation (**avaccheda-vāda**), reflection
(**pratibimba-vāda**) and semblance (**ābhāsa-vāda**). In Viśiṣṭādvaita it is a real conscious
mode (**prakāra**) of ultimate reality. In Dvaita it is a real, distinct, dependent substance.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Take one undivided space and put pots in it. Now there is "pot-space," "jar-space," "room-space" —
apparently many spaces, actually one, differentiated only by containers. Break the pots and nothing
is destroyed; a boundary is removed.

That is the limitation model. The reflection model changes the picture: the pots become mirrors, so
individuality is not merely a bounded region but a *derivative image* with its own vivid
first-person feel. The semblance model presses further: the image is only a semblance, thinner
still.

Why three models? Because one problem has three pressure-points. Limitation explains plurality
economically but makes individuality look like mere geometry. Reflection explains why being an
individual *feels* like something. Semblance protects non-duality most strictly. Advaita's
opponents call this instability; Advaita calls it three angles on one appearance.

#### VISUAL — THREE NON-DUAL MODELS, TWO REALIST RIVALS

```text
  ĀTMAN vs JĪVA — mark this first
    true self (ātman) ..... the self in its ESSENTIAL metaphysical nature
    individual (jīva) ..... the EMBODIED, empirical individual in bondage, agency
                            and transmigration

  ADVAITA — THREE MODELS OF ONE APPEARANCE
  ┌───────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┐
  │ limitation            │ ultimate reality appears │ one space seems many through │
  │ (avaccheda-vāda)      │ as individual when       │ pots (ghaṭākāśa)             │
  │ — Vācaspati Miśra     │ DELIMITED by ignorance   │ => no real fragment; apparent │
  │                       │ or internal adjuncts     │   limitation alone           │
  ├───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ reflection            │ the individual is the    │ face in a mirror: the image  │
  │ (pratibimba-vāda)     │ REFLECTION of conscious- │ appears individual, original │
  │ — Padmapāda,          │ ness in ignorance        │ unaffected                   │
  │   the Vivaraṇa line   │                          │ => explains DEPENDENCE and    │
  │                       │                          │   phenomenological vividness │
  ├───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ semblance             │ the individual is a MERE │ pushes derivative status     │
  │ (ābhāsa-vāda)         │ SEMBLANCE (ābhāsa)       │ further than reflection      │
  └───────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘
      common conclusion of all three: INDIVIDUALITY LACKS ULTIMATE REALITY

  VIŚIṢṬĀDVAITA — real conscious mode
    the individual is a REAL conscious mode (prakāra) of ultimate reality
    neither an illusion nor a detached independent substance
    body–soul analogy (śarīra-śarīrī): the individual is God's body in the sense that it
    depends entirely on God for existence, support and activity
    ! "body" here does NOT mean gross physical body; it means THAT WHICH IS CONTROLLED,
      SUSTAINED AND USED by an indwelling self

  DVAITA — real distinct dependent substance
    never reducible to God · never a mere mode of God · never illusory
    difference AMONG individuals is also real
    standard taxonomy: eternally free (nitya) · liberated (mukta) · bound (baddha)
    bound souls further described as liberation-eligible (mukti-yogya) ·
      eternal transmigrators (nitya-saṃsārin) · darkness-eligible (tamo-yogya)
    ! a real Madhva doctrine, but NOT needed in every fivefold-difference answer.
      Use it where hierarchy, grace or differentiated liberation is directly relevant,
      and EVALUATE its severe ethical implications rather than reporting predestination
      casually.

  COMPACT STATEMENT BANK (use verbatim)
    Advaita ......... the individual is ultimate reality appearing finite through ignorance
    Viśiṣṭādvaita ... the individual is a real conscious mode of ultimate reality
    Dvaita .......... the individual is a real dependent self eternally different from God
```

*What the diagram shows: three Advaita models converge on one conclusion while two realist rivals
converge on the opposite one — and the exam reward lies in naming the internal plurality without
losing the shared conclusion.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **The distinction to state first.** The true self often designates the self in its essential
   metaphysical nature; the individual (**jīva**) usually denotes the embodied, empirical
   individual caught in bondage, agency and transmigration.
2. **Limitation (avaccheda-vāda)**, associated especially with **Vācaspati Miśra**. Ultimate
   reality appears as an individual when delimited by ignorance or internal adjuncts. The classic
   analogy is space in a pot: one undivided space seems many because of limiting containers. The
   individual is not a real fragment of ultimate reality; apparent limitation alone produces
   plurality.
3. **Reflection (pratibimba-vāda)**, associated with **Padmapāda** and the **Vivaraṇa** line. The
   individual is the reflection of consciousness in ignorance. Like a face reflected in a mirror,
   the reflected self appears individual while the original remains unaffected. This model explains
   both dependence and phenomenological individuality more vividly than sheer delimitation.
4. **Semblance (ābhāsa-vāda).** The individual is a mere semblance (**ābhāsa**) of ultimate
   reality. This pushes the derivative status of individuality even further than reflection theory.
   Naming it is useful in answers to show internal plurality within Advaita without losing the
   common conclusion: individuality lacks ultimate reality.
5. **Viśiṣṭādvaita: a real mode.** For Rāmānuja the individual is a real, conscious **mode
   (prakāra)** of ultimate reality — neither an illusion nor a detached independent substance. Its
   relation to God is explained through the **body–soul (śarīra-śarīrī)** analogy: the individual
   is God's body in the sense that it depends entirely on God for its existence, support and
   activity. Body here does not mean gross physical body only; it means that which is controlled,
   sustained and used by an indwelling self.
6. **Dvaita: a real distinct dependent substance.** Madhva treats the individual as real, distinct
   and dependent — never reducible to God, never a mere mode of God, never illusory. Difference
   among individuals is also real. The standard Dvaita taxonomy distinguishes the eternally free
   (**nitya**), the liberated (**mukta**) and the bound (**baddha**); bound souls are further
   described as **liberation-eligible (mukti-yogya)**, **eternal transmigrators
   (nitya-saṃsārin)** and **darkness-eligible (tamo-yogya)**, a scheme corresponding broadly to
   qualitative gradation among souls. This is a real Madhva doctrine, but it is not needed in every
   fivefold-difference answer; use it where hierarchy, grace or differentiated liberation is
   directly relevant, and evaluate its severe ethical implications rather than presenting
   predestination casually.
7. **Why the individual is asked in two directions.** The 2021 paper asked Madhva's account of the
   individual and the world; the 2022 paper asked the ontological status of the Lord, the
   individual and the witness in non-dualism. The topic must therefore always be prepared in both
   *intra-Advaita* detail and *inter-school* comparison.
8. **The compact statement bank.** Advaita: the individual is ultimate reality appearing finite
   through ignorance. Viśiṣṭādvaita: the individual is a real conscious mode of ultimate reality.
   Dvaita: the individual is a real dependent self eternally different from God.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **Vācaspati Miśra** and the *Bhāmatī* line — associated with the limitation model.
- **Padmapāda** and the *Vivaraṇa* line — associated with the reflection model.
- **Pot-space (ghaṭākāśa)** — the limitation analogy; **face and mirror** — the reflection analogy.
- **śarīra-śarīrī-bhāva** — Rāmānuja's body–soul relation, asked directly in 2024.
- **nitya / mukta / baddha** and **mukti-yogya / nitya-saṃsārin / tamo-yogya** — Madhva's taxonomy
  of souls.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Advaita explains the individual three times over because individuality resists a single
> explanation: limitation accounts for plurality, reflection for its vividness, and semblance for
> its thinness — and all three arrive at the same verdict, that the individual is a way ultimate
> reality appears, never a further thing that exists.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

individual self (**jīva**) · limitation model (**avaccheda-vāda**) · reflection model
(**pratibimba-vāda**) · semblance model (**ābhāsa-vāda**) · pot-space (**ghaṭākāśa**) · mode
(**prakāra**) · body–soul relation (**śarīra-śarīrī-bhāva**) · eternally free (**nitya**) ·
liberated (**mukta**) · bound (**baddha**) · gradation among souls

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** The plurality of Advaita models for the individual is explanatory division of labour,
not doctrinal confusion.
**Named evidence.** Vācaspati Miśra's limitation model uses pot-space, Padmapāda's Vivaraṇa
reflection model uses the mirrored face, and the semblance model reduces the individual to a
semblance (**ābhāsa**).
**Analysis.** Each model answers a different objection — how can one be many, why does being many
feel real, and how is the many kept from acquiring ontological weight — so the three are best read
as complementary pressures on a single thesis rather than as rival doctrines.
**Qualification.** Rāmānuja is entitled to press the opposite reading: a doctrine needing three
incompatible metaphors to explain one fact may be signalling that the fact has not been explained,
and the honest answer concedes that Advaita's own tradition never fully unified them.

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

- **2021, Question 8(c), 15 marks** — the status of the individual and the world in Madhva; §6 here
  plus Session 8 and Session 15.
- **2022, Question 8(a), 20 marks** — the individual's ontological status alongside the Lord and
  the witness.
- **2020, Question 8(c), 15 marks** — the individual and witness relation, using §2–§4 with Session
  6.
- **2024, Question 5(e), 10 marks** — the body–soul analogy is introduced here and completed in
  Session 14.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Do not use the true self and the individual interchangeably in an Advaita answer without
   flagging the distinction; the distinction itself carries marks.
2. Do not attribute the limitation model to Padmapāda or the reflection model to Vācaspati Miśra;
   the association runs the other way.
3. Do not describe Rāmānuja's individual as "part of God" in a quantitative sense. It is a mode
   inseparably qualifying a substantive whole.
4. Do not present Madhva's gradation of souls as a neutral classificatory fact. State it, then
   evaluate the ethical difficulty.
5. Do not say the pot-space analogy proves non-duality. It illustrates how apparent limitation can
   generate apparent plurality; it presupposes rather than establishes the thesis.

#### RAPID REVISION

- True self = essential nature; individual = embodied, bound, transmigrating.
- Three Advaita models: limitation (Vācaspati) · reflection (Padmapāda, Vivaraṇa) · semblance.
- Viśiṣṭādvaita: real conscious mode; body–soul dependence.
- Dvaita: real distinct dependent substance; nitya/mukta/baddha; gradation contested ethically.
- Statement bank: appears finite · real mode · eternally different.

---
### SESSION 8 — THE WORLD (JAGAT): APPEARANCE, DIVINE BODY, OR DISTINCT DEPENDENT ORDER

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** The world (**jagat**) is the ordered field of names, forms, bodies
and events. The Vedāntic question is not whether it appears — everyone grants that — but what grade
of reality the appearing deserves.

**Technical definition.** Advaita holds the world to be an **apparent transformation (vivarta)** of
ultimate reality and therefore **dependently real (mithyā)** — neither absolutely real nor
absolutely unreal, and specifically not **wholly non-existent (tuccha)**. Viśiṣṭādvaita holds it to
be real and to constitute the non-conscious (**acit**) dimension of ultimate reality's body. Dvaita
holds it to be real and eternally distinct from God.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Three grades, not two. A rope taken for a snake: the snake is not nothing — you jumped — but it is
not real either, because when the rope is seen, the snake does not go anywhere; it was never a
thing that could go. Compare the son of a barren woman, which never appears at all, and compare the
rope itself, which does not get cancelled.

Advaita organises everything with these three grades: the ultimately real, the empirically real,
and the merely apparent. The world sits in the middle. It behaves, coheres, obeys laws, supports
science, ethics and cooking. What it lacks is *independence*: it is cancelled by the knowledge of
its substrate, as the snake is cancelled by the sight of the rope.

Rāmānuja replies that a world which gets cancelled cannot be the field of duty, devotion and divine
purpose, and that scripture describing creation would then describe a lower truth. Madhva replies
more bluntly: a real world that depends on God is already fully explained, and the middle grade is
an unnecessary invention.

#### VISUAL — THREE GRADES, ONE FORK IN CAUSATION, THREE VERDICTS ON THE WORLD

```text
   THE THREE LEVELS OF REALITY (Advaita)
     ULTIMATE (pāramārthika) ...... ultimate reality alone; never sublated (trikālābādhita)
     EMPIRICAL (vyāvahārika) ...... the world, the Lord and individual selves — VALID,
                                    coherent, public, law-governed; sublated only by
                                    knowledge of the absolute
     APPARENT (prātibhāsika) ...... dream, rope-snake, shell-silver — private, cancelled
                                    within empirical experience itself
     => the world is denied ABSOLUTE reality, NOT empirical functioning
     => dependently real (mithyā) ≠ wholly non-existent (tuccha, e.g. a hare's horn)

   THE CAUSATION FORK ─ asked 2022, Q5(e), 10 marks
     Sāṃkhya: REAL transformation (pariṇāma-vāda) — the effect is a real modification
              of the cause .......... milk => curd
     Advaita: APPARENT transformation (vivarta) — the substrate is unchanged and the
              effect is a sublatable appearance ...... rope => snake
     ARGUMENT FOR "logical development":
        if the cause is the ABSOLUTE, and the absolute admits NO real modification,
        then the effect cannot be equally real AS TRANSFORMATION
        => transformation must be only APPARENT: not pariṇāma but vivarta
        => vivarta PRESERVES causal dependence while PREVENTING real change in the absolute
     ARGUMENT AGAINST:
        real transformation and apparent transformation give CONTRARY ontological status
        to the effect => vivarta may be a REJECTION, not a completion, of pariṇāma
     GRADED VERDICT (the mark-winning line):
        vivarta develops the search for an effect DEPENDENT on its cause, but ABANDONS
        pariṇāma's central commitment to REAL transformation.
        Agreement is therefore STRUCTURAL, not ONTOLOGICAL.

   THREE VERDICTS ON THE WORLD
     ADVAITA ......... apparent transformation; the rope-snake substrate is real, the
                       appearance dependent and sublatable; mithyā not tuccha
     VIŚIṢṬĀDVAITA ... the world is REAL and forms the non-conscious (acit) dimension of
                       ultimate reality's BODY; creation is a real manifestation involving
                       the non-conscious mode (prakṛti); cause-state subtle, effect-state
                       gross; effects real => the universe is a meaningful field of duty,
                       devotion and divine manifestation
     DVAITA .......... the world is REAL; dependence on God does NOT cancel distinctness;
                       God is its efficient governor, dependent primordial matter its
                       material cause; matter is NOT absorbed into identity by knowledge
                       => the strongest realist posture among the three

   ŚAṄKARA vs RĀMĀNUJA ON WORLD-STATUS ─ asked 2021, Q8(b), 15 marks
  ┌──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┐
  │ Issue                │ Śaṅkara                  │ Rāmānuja                     │
  ├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ reality of world     │ mithyā, dependent        │ real manifestation           │
  │                      │ appearance               │                              │
  │ change in absolute   │ denied                   │ accepted in a qualified      │
  │                      │                          │ sense, through modes         │
  │ soteriology          │ world sublated in        │ world meaningful in          │
  │                      │ knowledge                │ devotion                     │
  │ God–world relation   │ empirical                │ intrinsic body–soul relation │
  └──────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘
     evaluative line: Śaṅkara protects ABSOLUTENESS by reducing world-status;
                      Rāmānuja protects WORLD-STATUS by qualifying absoluteness.

   MADHVA ON INDIVIDUAL AND WORLD ─ asked 2021, Q8(c), 15 marks
     both individual and world are REAL and DISTINCT from God
     difference is not merely practical but METAPHYSICAL
     => prepares directly for FIVEFOLD DIFFERENCE (Session 15)
```

*What the diagram shows: the three-level scheme is what allows Advaita to deny ultimacy without
denying experience — and the 2022 question is decided entirely by whether the candidate reaches the
graded verdict rather than a yes or a no.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **Advaita: apparent transformation.** For Advaita the world is an apparent transformation
   (**vivarta**) of ultimate reality; ultimate reality does not undergo real change. The classic
   example is the **rope–snake** illusion: the substrate is real, the appearance is dependent and
   sublatable. The world is therefore **mithyā** — neither absolutely real nor absolutely unreal —
   and it is not **tuccha** like a hare's horn. This is one of the most important trap-points in
   the whole clause.
2. **The three levels of reality.** Ultimate (**pāramārthika**): ultimate reality alone. Empirical
   (**vyāvahārika**): the world, the Lord and individual selves as empirically real. Apparent
   (**prātibhāsika**): dream and illusion, such as rope-snake or shell-silver. The world is denied
   absolute reality, not empirical functioning.
3. **Apparent transformation as a development of real transformation** (asked in 2022). Sāṃkhya's
   real transformation (**pariṇāma-vāda**) says the effect is a real transformation of the cause.
   Advaita radicalises the logic of causality: if the cause is the absolute, and the absolute
   admits no real modification, then the effect cannot be equally real as transformation;
   therefore transformation must be only apparent. In this sense apparent transformation preserves
   causal dependence while preventing real change in the absolute. The opposing verdict is equally
   important: real transformation and apparent transformation give contrary ontological status to
   the effect, so apparent transformation may be a *rejection* rather than a logical completion of
   real transformation. Milk becoming curd illustrates a real altered effect; rope appearing as
   snake illustrates a sublatable appearance whose substrate does not change. The graded
   conclusion is that apparent transformation develops the search for an effect dependent on its
   cause but abandons real transformation's central commitment to genuine change — so the
   agreement is structural, not ontological.
4. **Viśiṣṭādvaita: the world is real.** Rāmānuja firmly rejects world-appearance doctrine. The
   world is real and forms the non-conscious (**acit**) dimension of ultimate reality's body.
   Creation is a real manifestation or transformation involving the non-conscious mode
   (**prakṛti**); the cause-state is subtle and the effect-state gross. Ultimate reality is both
   efficient and material cause because conscious and non-conscious modes are inseparable from it,
   while God's essential perfection remains unchanged. Since effects are real, the universe is a
   meaningful field of duty, devotion and divine manifestation.
5. **Dvaita: real and eternally distinct.** Madhva accepts the world as real. Its dependence on God
   does not cancel its distinctness. God is its efficient governor, while dependent primordial
   matter is its material cause. Matter is not absorbed into identity by knowledge. This gives
   Dvaita the strongest realist posture among the three schools.
6. **Śaṅkara versus Rāmānuja on world-status** (asked in 2021). On the reality of the world:
   dependent appearance versus real manifestation. On change in the absolute: denied versus
   accepted in a qualified sense through modes. On soteriology: the world is sublated in knowledge
   versus the world is meaningful in devotion. On the God–world relation: empirical versus
   intrinsic body–soul relation. A useful evaluative line: Śaṅkara protects absoluteness by
   reducing world-status; Rāmānuja protects world-status by qualifying absoluteness.
7. **Madhva on the individual and the world** (also asked in 2021). Both are real and distinct from
   God; difference is not merely practical but metaphysical; this prepares directly for fivefold
   difference.
8. **Comparative placement.** On the world Vedānta spans three positions: Advaita — ontological
   dependence with empirical validity; Viśiṣṭādvaita — a real world as divine body; Dvaita — a real
   world as a distinct dependent order.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **Rope and snake** — dependent, sublatable appearance on a real substrate.
- **Milk and curd** — real transformation, the contrast case for Sāṃkhya and, in a qualified sense,
  for Rāmānuja.
- **Hare's horn** — the standard example of the wholly non-existent, used to mark off *mithyā* from
  *tuccha*.
- **Shell and silver** — the apparent-level illusion that recurs in the theory of error.
- **2022, Question 5(e)** — "Do you agree with the view that 'Vivartavāda is the logical
  development of Pariṇāmavāda'? Give reasons in support of your answer."

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Advaita does not say the world is unreal; it says the world is not *independently* real — and the
> whole force of the doctrine lies in that middle grade, because a world that can be cancelled but
> cannot be dismissed is exactly what a non-dual absolute requires.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

world (**jagat**) · apparent transformation (**vivarta**) · real transformation (**pariṇāma**) ·
dependently real (**mithyā**) · wholly non-existent (**tuccha**) · ultimate level
(**pāramārthika**) · empirical level (**vyāvahārika**) · apparent level (**prātibhāsika**) ·
sublation (**bādha**) · non-conscious (**acit**) · conscious (**cit**) · subtle cause-state and
gross effect-state

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Apparent transformation is best described as a structural continuation and an
ontological rupture of real transformation at the same time.
**Named evidence.** Sāṃkhya's milk-into-curd requires the effect to pre-exist in and really modify
the cause; Advaita's rope-into-snake requires the effect to depend on a substrate that is not
modified at all.
**Analysis.** Both models are refusing creation from nothing and both make the effect
cause-dependent, so the *architecture* of dependence is inherited; but real transformation buys
dependence at the cost of change in the cause, and Advaita cannot pay that cost once the cause is
the absolute, so the ontological content is reversed.
**Qualification.** The correct verdict is therefore graded rather than affirmative or negative:
agreement is structural, disagreement is ontological, and an answer that says only "yes" or only
"no" has missed the question's word "development."

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

- **2022, Question 5(e), 10 marks** — apparent transformation and real transformation; the graded
  verdict is compulsory.
- **2021, Question 8(b), 15 marks** — Śaṅkara and Rāmānuja on the status of the world; use the
  four-row table and the evaluative line.
- **2021, Question 8(c), 15 marks** — the individual and the world in Madhva; combine §5 and §7 with
  Sessions 7 and 15.
- **2019, Question 8(a), 20 marks** — consequence 1 of non-dual absolutism is exactly this session.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Never equate *mithyā* with *tuccha*. The whole doctrine collapses into nihilism if you do.
2. Do not answer the 2022 question with an unqualified "yes." The graded verdict — structural
   agreement, ontological rupture — is where the marks are.
3. Do not write that Advaita denies the world's existence; it denies independent, unsublatable
   reality.
4. Do not describe Rāmānuja's world as an emanation that leaves God diminished; the essential
   perfection is untouched while the non-conscious mode really transforms.
5. Do not say Madhva accepts *māyā* in Advaita's sense; for him ignorance is real ignorance, not a
   cosmic veil over identity.

#### RAPID REVISION

- Three levels: ultimate · empirical · apparent; sublation grades them.
- Advaita: apparent transformation; rope–snake; mithyā not tuccha.
- 2022 verdict: structural agreement, ontological rupture.
- Śaṅkara reduces world-status to protect absoluteness; Rāmānuja qualifies absoluteness to protect
  the world.
- Madhva: real, distinct, dependent — the strongest realism.

---
### SESSION 9 — INDESCRIBABLE ILLUSION (MĀYĀ) AND IGNORANCE (AVIDYĀ)

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Indescribable illusion (**māyā**) is Advaita's name for whatever it
is that makes a non-dual reality appear as a plural world. Ignorance (**avidyā**) is the same
principle considered as what makes *me* take myself to be a limited person. Some Advaita texts use
the two words interchangeably; others separate them.

**Technical definition.** Illusion is beginningless (**anādi**), dependent on ultimate reality, and
**indescribable (anirvacanīya)** — neither being (**sat**) nor non-being (**asat**). It exercises
two powers: **concealment (āvaraṇa-śakti)** and **projection (vikṣepa-śakti)**. Ignorance is
beginningless yet terminable (**sānta**) by knowledge, and is held to be **positive in form
(bhāva-rūpa)** rather than a mere absence.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Two questions look alike and are not. First: *what is the status of the world-appearance?* Second:
*what is wrong with me that I take myself to be small?* The first is cosmological, the second
personal. Advaita's technical vocabulary tends to use "illusion" for the first and "ignorance" for
the second, and links the first to the Lord and the second to the individual.

Now the harder point, and the one the 2018 examiner tested. Advaita calls *two different things*
indescribable: ultimate reality and illusion. They are not indescribable in the same sense. The
absolute exceeds description because it is non-dual and non-objectifiable — describing it would
require treating it as one item among many. Illusion resists description because it cannot be
placed on either side of the being/non-being divide: it is experienced, so not nothing; it is
cancelled, so not real.

One indescribability is a *surplus*; the other is an *indeterminacy*. Mixing them is the single
most damaging error in this topic.

#### VISUAL — TWO INDESCRIBABILITIES, TWO POWERS, ONE UNSOLVED LOCUS

```text
   TWO SENSES OF INDESCRIBABILITY ─ asked 2018, Q5(c), 10 marks
  ┌───────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ ULTIMATE REALITY (brahman)            │ INDESCRIBABLE ILLUSION (māyā)             │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ non-dual, non-objectifiable reality   │ appearance that CANNOT BE CLASSIFIED as   │
  │ EXCEEDS limiting subject–predicate    │ ultimately real (because it is sublated)  │
  │ description                           │ NOR as sheer non-being (because it is     │
  │                                       │ experienced)                              │
  │ it does NOT mean uncertain reality    │ it DOES mean indeterminate ontological    │
  │                                       │ status                                    │
  │ the UNSUBLATED reality BECAUSE OF     │ a DEPENDENT and SUBLATABLE explanatory    │
  │ which description and experience are  │ principle                                 │
  │ possible                              │                                           │
  └───────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┘
   x THE TWO USES ARE NOT IDENTICAL: brahman is BEYOND FINITE PREDICATION;
     māyā is INDETERMINATE BETWEEN ultimate being and sheer non-being
   ! critical add-on: realist schools accept the absolute's transcendence far more
     readily than they accept an alleged THIRD ontological status for illusion

   TWO POWERS OF ILLUSION
     concealment (āvaraṇa-śakti) ..... hides ultimate reality's true nature
     projection (vikṣepa-śakti) ...... throws up the world of names and forms
     => together they explain how ignorance can both COVER and FABRICATE at the
       empirical level

   ILLUSION AND IGNORANCE — IDENTITY OR DISTINCTION?  (? genuinely non-uniform)
     ? some formulations treat them as broadly INTERCHANGEABLE
     ? others: illusion = COSMIC/OBJECTIVE, associated with the LORD
               ignorance = INDIVIDUAL/SUBJECTIVE, associated with the INDIVIDUAL
     + both are usually regarded as INDESCRIBABLE
     => exam formula: state the distinction carefully AND note that usage varies

   THE LOCUS-AND-OBJECT PROBLEM (āśraya / viṣaya)
     whose is ignorance?
       if it belongs to the INDIVIDUAL => circular, since the individual is itself
         supposed to arise WITHIN ignorance
       if it belongs to ULTIMATE REALITY => the absolute appears compromised
     => a MAJOR polemical opening for qualified-non-dualist critique (Session 10)

   IGNORANCE IN THE THREE SCHOOLS
     ADVAITA ......... beginningless (anādi) yet terminable (sānta) by knowledge;
                       not eternal in the way ultimate reality is eternal;
                       explains bondage, individuality, agency and misidentification;
                       through it the self identifies with body, mind, senses, doership
                       and enjoyership — so empirical existence is structured by
                       ignorance, NOT by a real transformation of the self
     VIŚIṢṬĀDVAITA ... NO indescribable cosmic principle; ignorance is REAL ignorance of
                       God's nature and of the soul's dependence; tied to karma and
                       bondage; removed by right knowledge integrated with devotion and
                       especially SELF-SURRENDER (prapatti)
     DVAITA .......... ignorance is REAL; not a metaphysical veil over non-dual identity;
                       overcome through correct knowledge, devotion and divine grace
                       => Madhva's realism preserves stronger continuity between ignorance,
                         ethics, devotion and salvation than appearance-theory does
```

*What the diagram shows: the 2018 ten-marker is won or lost in the top box, and the locus problem
in the middle is the doorway to Rāmānuja's seven objections in the next session.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **The basic Advaita definition.** Illusion is the cosmic power through which plurality appears.
   It is beginningless and dependent on ultimate reality. It is neither being (**sat**) nor
   non-being (**asat**) but **indescribable (anirvacanīya)**. It is not pure nothingness — if it
   were, no appearance could occur — yet it cannot be fully real, because knowledge sublates its
   products.
2. **Two different senses of indescribability** (asked in 2018). The absolute's indescribability
   means that non-dual, non-objectifiable reality exceeds limiting subject–predicate description;
   it does not mean that its reality is uncertain. Illusion's indescribability means that
   appearance cannot be classified as ultimately real, because it is sublated, or as sheer
   non-being, because it is experienced. The absolute is the unsublated reality because of which
   description and experience are possible; illusion is a dependent and sublatable explanatory
   principle. The two uses are therefore not identical: the absolute is beyond finite predication,
   while illusion is indeterminate between ultimate being and sheer non-being. A critical answer
   should add that realist schools accept the absolute's transcendence more readily than the
   alleged third ontological status of illusion.
3. **The two powers.** Concealment (**āvaraṇa-śakti**) hides the absolute's true nature; projection
   (**vikṣepa-śakti**) throws up the world of names and forms. These two powers explain how
   ignorance can both cover and fabricate at the empirical level, and they reappear as the two
   components of superimposition in Session 11.
4. **Illusion and ignorance: identity or distinction?** Advaita sources are not fully uniform. In
   some formulations the two are treated as broadly interchangeable. In others, illusion is
   cosmic and objective and associated with the Lord, while ignorance is individual and subjective
   and associated with the individual self. Both are usually regarded as indescribable. For the
   examination, state the distinction carefully and note that exact usage varies.
5. **The locus-and-object problem.** A classical problem asks: whose is ignorance? If ignorance
   belongs to the individual, the account seems circular, because the individual is itself supposed
   to arise within ignorance. If ignorance belongs to ultimate reality, the absolute appears
   compromised by ignorance. This is the **locus/object (āśraya/viṣaya)** problem, and it forms a
   major polemical opening for qualified-non-dualist critique.
6. **Ignorance in Advaita.** It is beginningless (**anādi**) yet terminable (**sānta**) through
   knowledge; it is not eternal in the sense that ultimate reality is eternal. It explains bondage,
   individuality, agency and misidentification. Through ignorance the self identifies with body,
   mind, senses, doership and enjoyership; empirical existence is therefore structured by
   ignorance, not by a real transformation of the self. The formula — beginningless but endable —
   is essential in every liberation answer.
7. **Ignorance in Viśiṣṭādvaita.** Rāmānuja does not admit ignorance as an indescribable cosmic
   principle. Ignorance is real ignorance of God's nature and of the soul's dependence on God. It
   is tied to karma and bondage, and is removed through right knowledge integrated with devotion
   and especially **self-surrender (prapatti)**.
8. **Ignorance in Dvaita.** In Dvaita too ignorance is real. It is not a metaphysical veil covering
   non-dual identity. It is overcome through correct knowledge, devotion and divine grace. Madhva's
   realism preserves stronger continuity between ignorance, ethics, devotion and salvation than
   appearance theory does.
9. **The trap formula to memorise.** Do not flatten the two terms into a crude synonym-pair. The
   safer formula is: *in many Advaita formulations, illusion is the cosmic power associated with
   the Lord, while ignorance is the individual ignorance associated with the individual self; both
   are indescribable, though some texts use them interchangeably.*

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **āvaraṇa-śakti** and **vikṣepa-śakti** — the two named powers.
- **anādi** and **sānta** — beginningless yet terminable, the formula that makes liberation
  possible.
- **bhāva-rūpa** — ignorance as positive in form rather than a bare absence.
- **āśraya / viṣaya** — the technical names of the locus-and-object problem.
- **2018, Question 5(c)** — "What is the difference between the indescribability (Anirvacanīyatā)
  of Brahman and the indescribability (Anirvacanīyatā) of Māyā in the Advaita Vedānta system?"

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Advaita uses one word, indescribable, for two opposite reasons: ultimate reality escapes
> description because it is too real to be an object, and illusion escapes description because it
> is not real enough to be either being or non-being — and confusing a surplus with an indeterminacy
> converts the system into the incoherence its critics allege.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

indescribable illusion (**māyā**) · ignorance (**avidyā**) · indescribable (**anirvacanīya**) ·
being (**sat**) · non-being (**asat**) · concealment (**āvaraṇa-śakti**) · projection
(**vikṣepa-śakti**) · beginningless (**anādi**) · terminable (**sānta**) · positive in form
(**bhāva-rūpa**) · locus (**āśraya**) · object (**viṣaya**) · self-surrender (**prapatti**)

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** The two indescribabilities differ in direction, not merely in degree.
**Named evidence.** Ultimate reality is called indescribable while being **trikālābādhita**, never
sublated; illusion is called indescribable precisely because its products *are* sublated by
knowledge while still being experienced.
**Analysis.** The absolute's case is a refusal of predication imposed by non-objectifiability; the
illusion's case is a refusal of classification imposed by the failure of the being/non-being
disjunction — so one term marks what is beyond the categories and the other marks what falls
between them.
**Qualification.** Critics are entitled to note that only the first refusal is widely conceded:
realist schools grant that the absolute exceeds finite predicates while denying that anything can
occupy a third ontological grade, so an answer should record the asymmetry rather than presenting
both as equally secure.

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

- **2018, Question 5(c), 10 marks** — exactly §2, with the two powers as supporting detail.
- **2018, Question 7(c), 15 marks** — the printed question also asks why illusion is "needed" by
  both thinkers; §7 gives the qualified-non-dualist half of the reply.
- **2025, Question 5(d), 10 marks** — §5 is the doorway to the first of the seven objections.
- **2019, Question 8(a), 20 marks** — the projection and concealment machinery underwrites
  consequences 1 and 2.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Do not write that illusion and ignorance are simply the same thing. Give the cosmic/individual
   distinction and add that usage varies.
2. Do not say the absolute is indescribable "because we do not know it." That converts a doctrine
   of non-objectifiability into agnosticism.
3. Do not say illusion is unreal. It is not non-being; that is precisely why it can project.
4. Do not treat ignorance as a mere absence of knowledge in Advaita; the positive-in-form thesis is
   what allows the two powers.
5. Do not attribute an Advaita-style cosmic illusion to Rāmānuja or Madhva. Both reject it, and the
   2018 fifteen-marker turns on this.

#### RAPID REVISION

- Two indescribabilities: surplus (absolute) versus indeterminacy (illusion).
- Two powers: concealment and projection.
- Illusion cosmic/Lord · ignorance individual/self · usage varies.
- Beginningless but terminable; positive in form.
- Locus problem: individual gives circularity, absolute gives contamination.

---
### SESSION 10 — THE SEVEN OBJECTIONS AGAINST ILLUSION-DOCTRINE, AND THE QUALIFIED-REALIST ALTERNATIVE

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Rāmānuja's seven objections are a systematic demonstration that
Advaita's ignorance-doctrine cannot answer seven basic questions at once: where ignorance lives,
how it can hide a self-luminous reality, what it *is*, whether a third ontological status is
legitimate, how we know it, what removes it, and what its removal amounts to.

**Technical definition.** The list is conventionally called **sapta-anupapatti** or
**sapta-vidhā-anupapatti** — the seven alleged incoherences (**anupapatti**) of the
illusion/ignorance doctrine (**māyāvāda**). Exact names and sequence vary slightly across
presentations; the standard examination list is given below.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

A good objection is not a complaint; it is a demand for an account. Rāmānuja's seven are a single
demand repeated in seven places: *you have introduced an extra entity to explain appearance — now
pay for it.*

Notice the shape of the attack. He does not begin by denying non-duality. He accepts that Advaita
must explain how the one appears as many, and then shows that every available answer costs
something Advaita cannot afford. Put ignorance in the absolute and you spoil the absolute; put it
in the individual and you argue in a circle. Say ignorance conceals and you contradict
self-luminosity. Say it is neither real nor unreal and you have named a puzzle, not solved one.

Advaita has replies to all seven, and some are strong. But two — the locus objection and the
knowledge objection — are widely acknowledged even by sympathetic readers to leave a residue. The
examination reward is exactly this: state the objection, state the reply, and state honestly what
survives.

#### VISUAL — THE SEVEN, WITH REPLY AND RESIDUE

```text
   RĀMĀNUJA'S SEVEN OBJECTIONS (sapta-anupapatti) ─ asked 2025, Q5(d), 10 marks
   ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
   1 LOCUS (āśraya-anupapatti) — untenable locus of ignorance
     OBJ ..... in the absolute => it ceases to be pure consciousness;
               in the individual => the individual is itself a product of ignorance
               => MUTUAL DEPENDENCE (anyonyāśraya)
     REPLY ... Vivaraṇa: the absolute is the LOCUS (āśraya) without being modified or
               tainted, since ignorance is not a real modification of it
     REPLY ... Bhāmatī: ignorance is located in the individual, and the
               individual–ignorance relation is BEGINNINGLESS (anāditva); a
               beginningless seed–sprout dependence is not automatically vicious
     RESIDUE . hardest objection: TWO Advaita sub-schools give TWO different answers,
               which is itself a dialectical opening
   ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   2 CONCEALMENT (tirodhāna-anupapatti)
     OBJ ..... the absolute is SELF-LUMINOUS (svayaṃ-prakāśa); to conceal it is to
               destroy its very nature as light/consciousness
     REPLY ... ignorance does not conceal the absolute AS SUCH; it conceals it AS
               RECOGNISED BY the individual, and the concealment is itself only
               apparent from the standpoint of ignorance
     EXAMPLE . a cloud does not extinguish the sun; it obstructs the VIEWER'S vision
     RESIDUE . the analogy helps against literal concealment, but one may still ask how
               ANY obstruction arises when the absolute alone is ultimately real
   ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   3 NATURE (svarūpa-anupapatti)
     OBJ ..... the nature of ignorance is incoherent if it is neither real nor unreal
     REPLY ... the being / non-being / indescribable trichotomy: experienced => not
               non-being; sublated => not being; therefore indescribable
     RESIDUE . works ONLY if SUBLATABILITY is accepted as a criterion of degrees of
               reality — which realist schools deny outright
   ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   4 INDEFINABILITY (anirvacanīyatva-anupapatti)
     OBJ ..... whatever exists must be real or unreal; a third category is a refusal
               to answer
     REPLY ... the contrast is NOT being versus non-being as bare contradictories but
               ontological GRADES measured by sublatability
     REPLY ... tu quoque: Rāmānuja's non-conscious (acit) as real-but-dependent BODY of
               the absolute also occupies a status that is neither independent
               absolutism nor non-being
     RESIDUE . Rāmānuja and Madhva call this verbal unless the third grade is
               independently grounded by a means of valid knowledge
   ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   5 MEANS OF KNOWING (pramāṇa-anupapatti)
     OBJ ..... perception gives an object, not a positive ignorance; inference lacks a
               valid mark; scripture does not straightforwardly teach a positive
               indefinable entity
     REPLY ... WITNESS-PERCEPTION (sākṣi-pratyakṣa): the immediate experience "I do not
               know" presents ignorance POSITIVELY, not as blank absence
     REPLY ... POSTULATION (arthāpatti): if knowledge removes bondage, there must be
               something positive to remove
     REPLY ... scripture itself teaches illusion/ignorance as a dependent power
               responsible for appearance
     RESIDUE . Rāmānuja: "I do not know" reports an ABSENCE of knowledge, not a
               mysterious positive entity => with (1), the most dangerous objection
   ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   6 REMOVER (nivartaka-anupapatti)
     OBJ ..... what KIND of knowledge removes ignorance? Qualityless knowledge seems
               contentless; determinate knowledge is itself within ignorance
     REPLY ... UNDIVIDED-FORM MENTAL MODE (akhaṇḍākāra-vṛtti): the great-sentence-
               generated cognition that takes the absolute as content by sublating all
               limiting adjuncts
     REPLY ... knowledge does not ACT on ignorance as one object acts on another; true
               cognition SUBLATES false cognition, then the mental mode subsides like
               the stick consumed in the fire it stirs
     RESIDUE . strong inside Advaita's ladder-model; a critic still asks how a mental
               mode WITHIN the empirical order can terminate that order
   ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   7 CESSATION (nivṛtti-anupapatti)
     OBJ ..... if ignorance is real it cannot be removed; if unreal there is nothing to
               remove; and if removed, the status of its cessation is obscure
     REPLY ... cessation is NOT a fifth entity produced after ignorance; it is simply
               the absolute REMAINING AS IT ALWAYS WAS once superimposition is sublated
     EXAMPLE . asking where ignorance went is like asking where the snake went when the
               rope was recognised
     RESIDUE . elegant IF superimposition is granted; question-begging if the opponent
               rejects the rope-snake model for world-experience
   ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
   FORCE OF THE SEVEN — three levels of attack
     (a) the STATUS of ignorance        (b) the POSSIBILITY of concealment and projection
     (c) the COHERENCE of liberation as removal of ignorance
   Advaita answers most convincingly where SUBLATION-LOGIC applies — cessation and
   concealment; it is most vulnerable on LOCUS and MEANS OF KNOWING, because the locus
   and proof of ignorance are not settled without accepting Advaita's own standpoint.

   RĀMĀNUJA'S POSITIVE ALTERNATIVE (what replaces illusion-doctrine)
     INSEPARABLE DEPENDENCE (apṛthaksiddhi) ... conscious and non-conscious are
       inseparable from the absolute yet really distinct as MODES
     BODY–SOUL RELATION (śarīra-śarīrī-bhāva) ... world and selves are the absolute's
       BODY; the absolute is their indwelling controller
     ERROR-THEORY: APPREHENSION OF THE REAL (satkhyāti) ... cognition is ALWAYS of the
       real; error is mistaking one real for another
     MECHANISM: elemental intermixture (pañcīkaraṇa / trivṛtkaraṇa) — every object
       contains a portion of every element, so a REAL silver-element in the shell can be
       misapprehended
   DVAITA'S THIRD ROUTE: error as apprehension otherwise, newly analysed
     (abhinava-anyathā-khyāti) + FIVEFOLD DIFFERENCE (Session 15)
   DVAITA ON ILLUSION: it does NOT accept illusion in the Advaita sense of ontological
     illusion; the world is real and ignorance is real ignorance. Madhva's critique is
     sharper than Rāmānuja's in one respect: it does not try to absorb plurality into
     qualified unity but makes REAL DIFFERENCE fundamental.
```

*What the diagram shows: seven objections, seven replies, seven residues. The examiner's word
"examine" in 2025 is satisfied only by the third column.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **What the list is.** The seven are conventionally called **sapta-anupapatti**; exact names and
   ordering vary slightly across presentations, but the seven headings above are the standard
   examination list, and the answer should say so rather than implying a fixed canonical sequence.
2. **Objection 1 — locus (āśraya).** If ignorance resides in the absolute, the absolute ceases to
   be pure consciousness; if it resides in the individual, the individual is already a product of
   ignorance, producing mutual dependence (**anyonyāśraya**). The Vivaraṇa school holds the
   absolute to be the locus without being modified or tainted, because ignorance is not a real
   modification of it. The Bhāmatī school locates ignorance in the individual and invokes the
   beginninglessness (**anāditva**) of the individual–ignorance relation; a beginningless
   seed–sprout dependence is not automatically a vicious regress. This remains one of the hardest
   objections, because the two Advaita sub-schools offer different strategies, which itself gives
   Rāmānuja a strong dialectical opening.
3. **Objection 2 — concealment (tirodhāna).** The absolute is self-luminous (**svayaṃ-prakāśa**);
   to conceal it would be to destroy its very nature as light. Advaita replies that ignorance does
   not conceal the absolute as such but conceals it as recognised by the individual, and that the
   concealment is itself only apparent from the standpoint of ignorance. A cloud does not
   extinguish the sun; it obstructs the viewer's vision. The analogy helps against literal
   concealment, but the critic can still ask how any obstruction can arise when the absolute alone
   is ultimately real.
4. **Objection 3 — nature (svarūpa).** Ignorance's nature is incoherent if it is said to be neither
   real nor unreal. Advaita invokes the being/non-being/indescribable trichotomy: ignorance is
   experienced, so it is not non-being; it is sublated by knowledge, so it is not being; it is
   therefore indescribable. The reply works only if sublatability is accepted as a criterion of
   degrees of reality, and realist schools deny precisely that presupposition.
5. **Objection 4 — indefinability (anirvacanīyatva).** Whatever exists must be either real or
   unreal; a third category is only a refusal to answer. Advaita replies that the contrast is not
   between being and non-being as bare contradictories but between ontological grades measured by
   sublatability, and may add a counter-charge: Rāmānuja's non-conscious (**acit**) as
   real-but-dependent body of the absolute also occupies a status that is neither independent
   absolutism nor non-being. Rāmānuja and Madhva regard the reply as verbal unless the third grade
   is independently grounded by a means of valid knowledge.
6. **Objection 5 — means of knowing (pramāṇa).** Perception gives an object, not a positive
   ignorance; inference lacks a valid mark; scripture does not straightforwardly teach a positive
   indefinable entity. Advaita appeals to **witness-perception (sākṣi-pratyakṣa)**: the immediate
   experience "I do not know" presents ignorance positively, not merely as blank absence. It also
   invokes **postulation (arthāpatti)**: if knowledge removes bondage, there must be something
   positive to remove. It further cites scripture's teaching of illusion or ignorance as a
   dependent power responsible for appearance. Rāmānuja replies that "I do not know" reports an
   absence of knowledge, not a mysterious positive entity; hence this remains, with the locus
   objection, among the most dangerous.
7. **Objection 6 — remover (nivartaka).** What kind of knowledge can remove ignorance? Qualityless
   knowledge seems contentless, while determinate knowledge seems itself within ignorance. Later
   Advaita explains the remover as the **undivided-form mental mode (akhaṇḍākāra-vṛtti)**, the
   great-sentence-generated cognition that takes the absolute as its content by sublating all
   limiting adjuncts. Knowledge does not act on ignorance as one object acts on another; the
   dawning of true cognition sublates the false cognition, after which the mental mode itself
   subsides like the stick consumed in the fire it stirs. The answer is strong within Advaita's
   ladder-model of knowledge, but a critic may still ask how a mental mode within the empirical
   order can terminate that order itself.
8. **Objection 7 — cessation (nivṛtti).** If ignorance is real it cannot be removed; if unreal
   there is nothing to remove; and if removed, the ontological status of its cessation becomes
   obscure. Advaita says cessation is not a fifth entity produced after ignorance; it is simply the
   absolute remaining as it always was when superimposition is sublated. Asking where ignorance
   went after knowledge is like asking where the snake went when the rope was recognised. This is
   elegant if one accepts superimposition, but question-begging if the opponent rejects the
   rope-snake model for world-experience.
9. **The collective force.** The seven target Advaita at three levels: the *status* of ignorance,
   the *possibility* of concealment and projection, and the *coherence* of liberation as removal of
   ignorance. Advaita answers most convincingly where it can use the familiar logic of sublation,
   especially in cessation and concealment; it is most vulnerable on locus and means of knowing,
   because the locus and proof of ignorance are not settled without accepting Advaita's own
   standpoint. The seven are therefore not a mere list; they are a systematic attack on the
   explanatory cost of making the world indescribable.
10. **Rāmānuja's positive alternative.** He replaces illusion-doctrine with **inseparable
    dependence (apṛthaksiddhi)**: conscious and non-conscious are inseparable from the absolute yet
    really distinct as modes. The **body–soul relation (śarīra-śarīrī-bhāva)** is his model: the
    world and selves are the absolute's body, while the absolute is their indwelling controller. In
    error-theory he defends **apprehension of the real (satkhyāti)**: cognition is always of the
    real, and error is mistaking one real for another. Because of elemental intermixture
    (**pañcīkaraṇa / trivṛtkaraṇa**), every object contains a portion of every element, so the
    shell-silver illusion can be explained by a real silver-element in the shell being
    misapprehended.
11. **Dvaita's position.** Dvaita does not accept illusion in the Advaita sense of ontological
    illusion. The world is real, and ignorance is real ignorance, not cosmic appearance masking
    non-dual identity. Madhva's critique is sharper than Rāmānuja's in one respect: it does not try
    to absorb plurality into qualified unity but makes real difference fundamental. Madhva's own
    error-theory is apprehension otherwise, newly analysed (**abhinava-anyathā-khyāti**), and its
    ontological base is fivefold difference.
12. **The printed anomaly of 2018 Question 7(c).** The paper asks how Rāmānuja refutes the
    illusion-doctrine *and* why illusion is "needed by both Rāmānuja and Śaṅkara." The second
    sentence is philosophically unusual and is retained as printed. Answer by flagging that
    Rāmānuja rejects Advaitic illusion outright, and by interpreting the demand as a comparison of
    how each system explains plurality — Śaṅkara through a sublatable indescribable power,
    Rāmānuja through real inseparable modes — rather than by asserting that Rāmānuja accepts
    illusion.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **The cloud and the sun** — the concealment analogy and its limit.
- **The stick consumed in the pyre** — the self-cancelling mental mode.
- **"I do not know"** — the disputed datum of witness-perception.
- **Elemental intermixture (pañcīkaraṇa / trivṛtkaraṇa)** — Rāmānuja's mechanism for
  apprehension-of-the-real.
- **2025, Question 5(d)** — "Examine Rāmānuja's seven objections against Māyāvāda of Advaita."
- **2018, Question 7(c)** — the printed refutation-plus-anomaly question.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Rāmānuja's seven objections are not seven complaints but one bill presented seven times: Advaita
> introduced an indescribable power to save non-duality, and every question about where that power
> lives, what it is, and how it is known asks Advaita to pay for the saving.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

seven incoherences (**sapta-anupapatti**) · locus (**āśraya**) · mutual dependence
(**anyonyāśraya**) · concealment (**tirodhāna**) · self-luminous (**svayaṃ-prakāśa**) ·
indefinability (**anirvacanīyatva**) · witness-perception (**sākṣi-pratyakṣa**) · postulation
(**arthāpatti**) · remover (**nivartaka**) · undivided-form mental mode (**akhaṇḍākāra-vṛtti**) ·
cessation (**nivṛtti**) · apprehension of the real (**satkhyāti**) · elemental intermixture
(**pañcīkaraṇa**) · Vivaraṇa · Bhāmatī

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** The locus objection is the most damaging of the seven because Advaita's own tradition
answers it twice.
**Named evidence.** The Vivaraṇa line makes the absolute the locus without modification; the
Bhāmatī line places ignorance in the individual and appeals to the beginninglessness
(**anāditva**) of the relation.
**Analysis.** Two incompatible strategies to one question indicate that neither is forced by the
system, and a critic can therefore argue that the doctrine of ignorance is being fitted to the
objection rather than the objection being answered by the doctrine.
**Qualification.** The point should not be overstated: beginningless mutual dependence of the
seed-and-sprout type is a recognised Indian pattern for avoiding vicious regress, so the objection
establishes unresolved pressure rather than outright refutation.

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

- **2025, Question 5(d), 10 marks** — all seven, briefly, with at least two replies and one
  residue; do not exceed the word band by listing every reply.
- **2018, Question 7(c), 15 marks** — the refutation plus the printed anomaly; §12 is compulsory.
- **2023, Question 6(a), 20 marks** — objections 1, 3 and 5 supply the sharpest material for the
  critique of the qualityless absolute.
- **2021, Question 7(a), 20 marks** — objection 4 and Rāmānuja's apprehension-of-the-real are the
  rivals against which indescribable-error theory is defended.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Do not present the seven as a fixed canonical sequence with universally agreed names. Say the
   list is conventional and then give it.
2. Do not answer 2025 with objections alone. "Examine" requires replies and a residual verdict.
3. Do not write that Rāmānuja "accepts māyā." He rejects it; the 2018 printed sentence must be
   flagged, not rationalised.
4. Do not attribute the undivided-form mental mode to Śaṅkara verbatim; it is a later systematic
   explanation of knowledge's error-removing function.
5. Do not confuse Rāmānuja's apprehension-of-the-real with Nyāya's apprehension-otherwise; the
   mechanisms differ, and elemental intermixture is Rāmānuja's device.

#### RAPID REVISION

- Seven: locus · concealment · nature · indefinability · means of knowing · remover · cessation.
- Strongest against Advaita: locus and means of knowing. Weakest: cessation and concealment.
- Advaita's sub-schools: Vivaraṇa (absolute as locus) versus Bhāmatī (individual, beginningless).
- Rāmānuja's replacement: inseparable dependence + body–soul + apprehension of the real.
- 2018 anomaly: flag it, do not rationalise it.

---
### SESSION 11 — SUPERIMPOSITION (ADHYĀSA) AND THE INDESCRIBABLE-ERROR THEORY

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Superimposition (**adhyāsa**) is taking one thing for another —
above all, taking the self for the not-self and the not-self for the self. Indescribable-error
theory (**anirvacanīya-khyāti**) is Advaita's account of what the wrongly-seen object *is* while it
is being seen.

**Technical definition.** Śaṅkara defines superimposition in the opening of the
*Brahma-sūtra-bhāṣya* as mutual superimposition of subject and object, compressed in the phrase
**atasmin tad-buddhiḥ** — cognition of one thing in what is not that thing. It is **natural
(naisargika)** and **beginningless (anādi)**. Indescribable-error theory holds that the presented
object of an erroneous cognition is neither ultimately real nor absolutely unreal.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

You say, without hesitating, "I am tall," "I am happy," "I act," "I know." Look at what has just
happened. Height belongs to a body; happiness is a mental state; acting belongs to an organism;
knowing belongs to consciousness. The single word "I" has been made to carry all four, so the
attributes of the object have been transferred to the subject *and* the attributes of the subject
transferred to the object.

Śaṅkara's claim is that this is not an occasional slip. It is the structure of ordinary experience,
prior to any philosophy, which is why he calls it natural and beginningless. Every school admits
that people make mistakes; Advaita says the ego-standpoint itself is already a mistake.

The second half of the session asks a different question. When you see silver in a shell, what is
the silver *while you see it*? It cannot be real, because it disappears when you look properly. It
cannot be nothing, because nothing does not appear. Advaita's answer — that it is indescribable —
is the epistemological miniature of the whole metaphysics: what shell-silver is to shell, the world
is to ultimate reality.

#### VISUAL — SUPERIMPOSITION, THE RIVAL ERROR-THEORIES, AND THE PRESUPPOSITION LEDGER

```text
   SUPERIMPOSITION (adhyāsa) — Śaṅkara's opening move
     definition ... MUTUAL superimposition of subject and object
     shorthand .... atasmin tad-buddhiḥ — cognition of one thing in what is not that
     fuller ....... attributes of the OBJECT put on the SUBJECT, and vice versa
     character .... NATURAL (naisargika) and BEGINNINGLESS (anādi)
                    => ordinary life is ALREADY structured by misidentification
                      BEFORE any philosophical reflection
     two components  ► concealment (āvaraṇa) — hides the true nature
                     ► projection (vikṣepa) — throws up the false appearance
                     (mirrors the two powers of illusion — Session 9)
     root of all empirical dealing (vyavahāra):
       "I am tall" · "I am happy" · "I act" · "I know"
       => bodily and mental predicates falsely attributed to the self,
         consciousness falsely attributed to body and mind
     standard examples: ROPE–SNAKE · SHELL–SILVER · DESERT–MIRAGE
     relation to error-theory: superimposition is the PROCESS;
       indescribable-error theory explains the STATUS of the object thereby presented
       => connect them, never confuse them
     comparative force: for Nyāya error is wrong cognition OF OBJECTS; for Advaita the
       EGOIC STANDPOINT ITSELF is already infected by primordial superimposition

   THE PROBLEM OF ERROR — asked 2021, Q7(a), 20 marks
   ┌──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │ NYĀYA                │ APPREHENSION OTHERWISE (anyathā-khyāti): error is         │
   │                      │ apprehending something REAL ELSEWHERE in the wrong locus  │
   │ ADVAITA'S REFUTATION │ if the silver were a real elsewhere-object somehow        │
   │                      │ presented here, sublation would not cancel its apparent   │
   │                      │ OBJECTHOOD so radically; the illusion is dissolved NOT by │
   │                      │ travel to another place but by RECOGNITION of the         │
   │                      │ SUBSTRATE => the silver cannot simply be real silver       │
   │                      │ misplaced in cognition                                   │
   ├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
   │ VIŚIṢṬĀDVAITA        │ APPREHENSION OF THE REAL (satkhyāti): cognition is always │
   │                      │ of the real; elemental intermixture supplies a REAL       │
   │                      │ silver-element in the shell                               │
   ├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
   │ BUDDHIST             │ APPREHENSION OF THE NON-EXISTENT (asat-khyāti)            │
   │ ADVAITA'S ANSWER     │ rejected: the illusory silver APPEARS, and sheer          │
   │                      │ non-being cannot appear                                   │
   ├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
   │ ADVAITA              │ INDESCRIBABLE-ERROR THEORY (anirvacanīya-khyāti):         │
   │                      │ REAL? then it would survive sublation — it does not       │
   │                      │ UNREAL? then it could never appear — but it did           │
   │                      │ => dependent, provisional, INDESCRIBABLE status            │
   └──────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

   PRESUPPOSITION-LED DEFENCE OF INDESCRIBABILITY (the 20-mark spine)
     P1 .. being (sat) / non-being (asat) / indescribable are EXHAUSTIVE and MUTUALLY
           EXCLUSIVE grades within Advaita's scheme
     D  .. sat = NEVER sublated (trikālābādhita) · asat = never presented at all
           (hare's horn, square circle) · anirvacanīya = presented, then sublated
     A  .. illusion and world fall in the THIRD grade: experienced => not asat;
           sublated by knowledge of the absolute => not sat
     P2 .. SUBLATION (bādha) is the criterion of degrees of reality
     A  .. without bādha the three-tier scheme collapses into realism or nihilism
     P3 .. experience of an object gives it SOME positive status => shell-silver must be
           something presented, not sheer nothing
     A  .. hence Advaita prefers indescribable-error theory over Buddhist
           apprehension-of-the-non-existent
     P4 .. ignorance is POSITIVE IN FORM (bhāva-rūpa), not merely privative
     A  .. if it were only absence it could not perform concealment AND projection
     OBJ  "indescribable" seems to violate excluded middle, or to invent a third box
     REP  the trichotomy is not being versus non-being as bare contradictories but
          GRADES of sublatability
     REP  the claim is not that illusion lacks determinate nature altogether, but that
          it cannot be filed under EITHER ultimate head
     RESIDUAL  Rāmānuja and Madhva call this verbal unless the third grade is
               independently established
     DIALECTICAL NOTE  Śrīharṣa's strategy in the Khaṇḍanakhaṇḍakhādya is DIALECTICAL,
               not constructive: he refuses to supply a positive definition of the real
               and instead shows that the opponent's definitions of "real" fail
```

*What the diagram shows: the 2021 twenty-marker has two halves — refute the rival, then establish
the thesis — and the presupposition ledger is what turns the second half from assertion into
argument.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **Śaṅkara's classical definition.** In the opening of the *Brahma-sūtra-bhāṣya* he defines
   superimposition as mutual superimposition between subject and object. The shorthand phrase is
   **atasmin tad-buddhiḥ** — cognition of one thing in what is not that thing. More fully: the
   superimposition of the attributes of the object on the subject, and vice versa.
2. **Natural and beginningless.** He characterises superimposition as **naisargika** (natural) and
   **anādi** (beginningless), which means ordinary empirical life is already structured by
   misidentification prior to philosophical reflection.
3. **Two components.** Superimposition includes concealment (**āvaraṇa**) of the true nature and
   projection (**vikṣepa**) of the false appearance. This mirrors the two powers of illusion and
   connects epistemology to metaphysics.
4. **The root of all empirical dealing.** Śaṅkara treats superimposition as the root of all
   empirical dealing (**vyavahāra**). Statements such as "I am tall," "I am happy," "I act" and "I
   know" presuppose the mixing of self and not-self: bodily and mental predicates are falsely
   attributed to the self, while consciousness is attributed to body and mind. Worldly experience
   is therefore not merely occasionally mistaken; it is structurally pervaded by superimposition.
5. **Relation to the theory of error.** Superimposition is the dynamic process; indescribable-error
   theory is the theory explaining the ontological status of the erroneous object thereby
   presented. The two should be connected, not confused.
6. **The standard examples.** Rope–snake, shell–silver, desert–mirage. Use at least one in every
   superimposition answer; they make the abstraction examinable.
7. **Comparative significance.** Superimposition gives Advaita a deeper diagnosis of bondage than
   schools that treat error as isolated episodic misjudgment. For Nyāya, error concerns wrong
   cognition of objects; for Advaita, the very egoic standpoint is already infected by primordial
   superimposition.
8. **Indescribable-error theory defined** (asked in 2021). In an illusion such as shell-silver, the
   presented silver is neither fully real nor absolutely unreal; it is indescribable. If the silver
   were real, it should survive sublation by correct knowledge — but it disappears once the shell
   is correctly known. If it were unreal like a hare's horn, it could never appear at all.
   Therefore it has a dependent, provisional, indefinable status.
9. **Refutation of Nyāya's apprehension-otherwise.** Nyāya says error involves apprehending
   something real elsewhere in the wrong locus. Advaita objects that if the silver were a real
   elsewhere-object somehow presented here, sublation would not cancel its apparent objecthood so
   radically; the illusion is dissolved not by travel to another place but by recognition of the
   substrate. Therefore the silver cannot simply be real silver misplaced in cognition.
10. **Relation to ignorance, and why it matters.** The erroneous object is a product of ignorance;
    it has empirical momentariness but no ultimate standing. This supports the broader claim that
    the whole world can be experienced yet ultimately sublated. Indescribable-error theory is thus
    not only about minor perceptual mistakes: it is the epistemological miniature of Advaita
    metaphysics — what shell-silver is to shell, the world is to ultimate reality.
11. **The presupposition-led defence.** Being, non-being and indescribable are exhaustive and
    mutually exclusive grades within Advaita's explanatory scheme. Being means never sublated
    (**trikālābādhita**); non-being means never presented at all, like a hare's horn or a square
    circle; indescribable means presented in experience but subsequently sublated. Illusion and
    world fall into the third grade. Sublation (**bādha**) is the criterion of degrees of reality;
    without it the three-tier scheme collapses into either realism or nihilism. The experience of
    an object gives that object some positive status, which is why Advaita prefers
    indescribable-error theory to Buddhist apprehension-of-the-non-existent (**asat-khyāti**).
    Ignorance is positive in form (**bhāva-rūpa**), not merely a privative absence of knowledge;
    if it were only absence it could not exercise both concealment and projection. To the objection
    that "indescribable as real or unreal" violates the law of excluded middle or evades the issue,
    Advaita replies that the trichotomy is not between being and non-being as bare contradictories
    but between grades of sublatability, and that the claim is not that illusion lacks a determinate
    nature altogether but that it cannot be categorised under either ultimate ontological head.
    Rāmānuja and Madhva regard this as a verbal manoeuvre unless the third grade is independently
    established.
12. **A dialectical note worth one line.** Śrīharṣa's strategy in the *Khaṇḍanakhaṇḍakhādya* is
    dialectical rather than constructive: he refuses to supply a positive definition of the real and
    instead shows that the opponent's definitions of "real" fail.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **atasmin tad-buddhiḥ** — the compressed definition of superimposition.
- **Śaṅkara's *Adhyāsa-bhāṣya*** — the opening discussion of the *Brahma-sūtra-bhāṣya* in which the
  definition is given.
- **Rope–snake, shell–silver, desert–mirage** — the three canonical illusions.
- **Hare's horn / square circle** — the standard examples of what is never presented.
- **Śrīharṣa, *Khaṇḍanakhaṇḍakhādya*** — the dialectical defence by refutation of definitions.
- **2021, Question 7(a)** — "How do the advocates of anirvacanīya-khyāti refute the position of the
  Naiyāyikas and establish the position of Advaitins regarding the problem of error?"

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Advaita's theory of error is not a footnote about optical mistakes but the whole system in
> miniature: if a presented object can be neither real nor nothing, then the world's own
> presentation can be exactly that, and superimposition names the process by which the mistake is
> made about oneself rather than about a shell.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

superimposition (**adhyāsa**) · **atasmin tad-buddhiḥ** · natural (**naisargika**) · beginningless
(**anādi**) · concealment (**āvaraṇa**) · projection (**vikṣepa**) · empirical dealing
(**vyavahāra**) · indescribable-error theory (**anirvacanīya-khyāti**) · apprehension otherwise
(**anyathā-khyāti**) · apprehension of the real (**satkhyāti**) · apprehension of the non-existent
(**asat-khyāti**) · sublation (**bādha**) · never sublated (**trikālābādhita**) · positive in form
(**bhāva-rūpa**)

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** The refutation of Nyāya's error-theory turns on the *manner* of cancellation, not on the
mere fact of it.
**Named evidence.** In the shell-silver case the corrective cognition is "this is a shell," which
cancels the silver at that very locus; on the Nyāya account the silver is real silver remembered
from elsewhere and wrongly connected here.
**Analysis.** If the presented item were genuinely real elsewhere, correction ought to relocate it
rather than annihilate its objecthood at the locus; since correction does annihilate it, the
presented object must have had a status peculiar to the erroneous cognition itself.
**Qualification.** Nyāya can reply that cancellation removes only the wrong *connection*, not the
real relatum, so Advaita's argument establishes an anomaly for the rival rather than a proof of the
third grade — which is why the presupposition ledger, not the refutation, carries the positive
thesis.

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED
