- **2021, Question 7(a), 20 marks** — refutation of Nyāya plus establishment of the Advaita
  position; both halves are here, and the "critically discuss" directive requires the residual
  assessment from §11.
- **2018, Question 5(c), 10 marks** — the indescribability of illusion is defined here and
  contrasted in Session 9.
- **2022, Question 8(a), 20 marks** — superimposition is the mechanism that explains why the
  individual appears as bound.
- **2023, Question 7(b), 15 marks** — the removal of superimposition is what "attainment of the
  already attained" means.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Do not confuse superimposition with the theory of error. One is the process, the other the
   status of the product.
2. Do not write that the illusory silver "does not exist." Advaita's whole point is that
   non-existence cannot appear.
3. Do not attribute apprehension-of-the-non-existent to Advaita; it is the Buddhist position that
   Advaita rejects.
4. Do not omit the qualification that the defence depends on sublation being accepted as an
   ontological criterion; without it the answer is assertion.
5. Do not use Śrīharṣa as a constructive definer of reality; his method is refutative.

#### RAPID REVISION

- Superimposition: mutual, natural, beginningless; concealment plus projection.
- Four error-theories: apprehension otherwise (Nyāya) · of the real (Rāmānuja) · of the non-existent
  (Buddhist) · indescribable (Advaita).
- Refutation of Nyāya: cancellation at the locus, not relocation.
- Grades: never sublated · never presented · presented then sublated.
- Residue: the third grade needs independent grounding, and realists refuse it.

### SESSION 12 — THE GREAT SENTENCE "THAT THOU ART" (TAT TVAM ASI)

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** "That thou art" is the Upaniṣadic sentence in which the teacher tells
the pupil that what he most truly is, is what the whole world most truly is. The philosophical
problem is that the two words seem to name incompatible things.

**Technical definition.** A great sentence (**mahāvākya**) is an Upaniṣadic statement of identity
between the self and ultimate reality. Advaita interprets *tat tvam asi* by **part-abandoning
implication (bhāga-tyāga-lakṣaṇā)**, also called **abandoning-and-retaining implication
(jahad-ajahal-lakṣaṇā)**: the incompatible portions of the direct meanings (**vācyārtha**) are
abandoned and the common implied meaning (**lakṣyārtha**) is retained.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Someone points across a crowded room and says, "This is that Devadatta." You met Devadatta ten years
ago in another city; he was a boy then, and this is a middle-aged man here and now. Taken literally
the sentence is false: the boy-then-there is not the man-now-here.

Yet you understand it instantly, and it is true. You drop "then and there" from one side and "now
and here" from the other, and keep what both descriptions were about — the person. Nobody thinks
you have invented a new rule of language; this is how identity-statements across different modes of
presentation ordinarily work.

Advaita says the great sentence works exactly this way. "That" presents ultimate reality as
omniscient cosmic Lord; "thou" presents it as this limited, ignorant, embodied individual. The two
descriptions cannot both be literally true of one thing. Drop the incompatible portions —
omniscience and cosmic causality on one side, finitude and agency on the other — and what remains
on both sides is the same: pure consciousness.

#### VISUAL — HOW ONE SENTENCE IS MADE TO WORK

```text
              TAT  TVAM  ASI   —  "THAT  THOU  ART"
                   asked 2023, Q5(c), 10 marks
   ┌────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  TAT ("that")                  │  TVAM ("thou")                       │
   ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
   │ DIRECT MEANING (vācyārtha)     │ DIRECT MEANING (vācyārtha)           │
   │ the Lord (īśvara) associated   │ the individual (jīva) associated     │
   │ with illusion (māyā):          │ with ignorance (avidyā) and          │
   │ omniscient · remote · cosmic   │ limitation: finite · immediate ·     │
   │ cause · lordship               │ agent · ignorant                     │
   ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
   │ IMPLIED MEANING (lakṣyārtha)   │ IMPLIED MEANING (lakṣyārtha)         │
   │ PURE CONSCIOUSNESS             │ PURE CONSCIOUSNESS                   │
   └────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────┘
                    │                                  │
        the DIRECT meanings CONFLICT: omniscience, lordship and cosmic
        causality cannot literally be identical with finitude, ignorance
        and empirical agency
                    │
        PART-ABANDONING IMPLICATION (bhāga-tyāga-lakṣaṇā)
        = ABANDONING-AND-RETAINING IMPLICATION (jahad-ajahal-lakṣaṇā)
        ► ABANDON the incompatible portions of both direct meanings
        ► RETAIN the common implied meaning
                    │
                    ▼
        IDENTITY AT THE LEVEL OF ESSENCE, not crude sameness of empirical
        conditions

   THE CANONICAL PARALLEL
        so'yaṃ devadattaḥ — "THIS is THAT Devadatta"
        drop:  then / there / youth        drop:  now / here / age
        keep:  the identical PERSON
        => likewise the Lord–individual contradiction is resolved by dropping
          ADJUNCTS and retaining underlying CONSCIOUSNESS

   THREE OTHER GREAT SENTENCES (name one, do not recite the list)
        ahaṃ brahmāsmi ...... "I am ultimate reality"
        prajñānaṃ brahma .... "consciousness is ultimate reality"
        ayam ātmā brahma .... "this self is ultimate reality"

   RIVAL READINGS
        VIŚIṢṬĀDVAITA & DVAITA reject the identity-reading; for them the great
        sentences indicate DEPENDENCE, PARTICIPATION or SPECIAL RELATION,
        not absolute identity
        => the interpretation of ONE SENTENCE becomes the battleground for the
          WHOLE architecture of Vedānta

   ! TEXTUAL CAUTION (high-value, frequently missed)
        distinguish an UPANIṢADIC GREAT SENTENCE from a later DOCTRINAL SLOGAN.
        "Brahma satyaṃ jagan-mithyā, jīvo brahmaiva nāparaḥ" — used in the printed
        2022 question — is a TRADITIONAL ADVAITA SUMMARY, NOT one of the four
        standard Upaniṣadic great sentences. Quote it as a summary verse, and say so.
```

*What the diagram shows: the semantic device is not an ad hoc rescue but the ordinary logic of
identity across different modes of presentation — and the textual caution at the bottom is a mark
that most candidates leave on the table.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **The central problem** (asked in 2023). If *tat* means the omniscient Lord and *tvam* means the
   finite individual self, literal identity seems impossible. Advaita solves this by
   **part-abandoning implication (bhāga-tyāga-lakṣaṇā)**, also called
   **abandoning-and-retaining implication (jahad-ajahal-lakṣaṇā)** in this context.
2. **Direct and implied meanings.** *Tat* has as its direct meaning (**vācyārtha**) the Lord
   associated with illusion, and as its implied meaning (**lakṣyārtha**) pure consciousness. *Tvam*
   has as its direct meaning the individual associated with ignorance and limitation, and as its
   implied meaning pure consciousness.
3. **How the device operates.** The incompatible parts of the direct meanings are abandoned:
   omniscience, lordship and cosmic causality on the one hand, and finitude, agency and ignorance
   on the other. What is retained is the common implied meaning — pure consciousness. Thus the
   sentence teaches identity at the level of essence, not crude literal sameness of empirical
   conditions.
4. **The canonical illustration.** *so'yaṃ devadattaḥ*, "this is that Devadatta." The "this" and the
   "that" involve differing temporal and spatial contexts — here and there, now and then, youth and
   age. These accidental differences are dropped and the person is identified. Likewise the
   Lord–individual contradiction is resolved by dropping adjuncts and retaining underlying
   consciousness.
5. **Why the device is not arbitrary.** Implication (**lakṣaṇā**) is a recognised semantic operation
   in Indian philosophy of language; the Advaitin's claim is only that this particular sentence
   requires the variety in which part of each direct meaning is abandoned and part retained.
   Denying the device altogether would make ordinary identity statements across modes of
   presentation unintelligible.
6. **The rival readings.** Viśiṣṭādvaita and Dvaita reject the identity-reading. For them the great
   sentences indicate dependence, participation or special relation, not absolute identity. For
   Rāmānuja, "thou" names a self whose very nature is to be an inseparable mode of "that," so the
   sentence asserts inseparability rather than sameness. For Madhva, the sentence is read
   consistently with real difference and dependence. The interpretation of one sentence therefore
   becomes a battleground for the whole architecture of Vedānta.
7. **The textual caution.** Distinguish an Upaniṣadic great sentence from a later doctrinal slogan
   or semantic apparatus. The formula "ultimate reality is real, the world is dependent appearance,
   the individual self is none other than ultimate reality" — *Brahma satyaṃ jagan-mithyā, jīvo
   brahmaiva nāparaḥ* — is a traditional Advaita summary used in the printed 2022 question; it is
   not itself one of the four standard Upaniṣadic great sentences. Say so in the answer; it is a
   discriminating mark.
8. **What a ten-mark answer must contain.** The conflict of direct meanings; the name of the device
   in both its forms; the abandoned and retained portions specified, not merely mentioned; the
   Devadatta parallel; one line on the rival reading; one line of evaluation.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- ***tat tvam asi*** — the Chāndogya great sentence at issue.
- ***so'yaṃ devadattaḥ*** — the canonical parallel case.
- **bhāga-tyāga-lakṣaṇā / jahad-ajahal-lakṣaṇā** — the two names of the device.
- **The four standard great sentences** — *tat tvam asi*, *ahaṃ brahmāsmi*, *prajñānaṃ brahma*,
  *ayam ātmā brahma*.
- **2023, Question 5(c)** — "What is Advaitin interpretation of the great sentence (mahāvākya)
  'Thou art that' (tat tvam asi)? Briefly discuss."

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Advaita does not read "that thou art" as an equation between a cosmic ruler and a village
> householder; it reads it as the ordinary logic of identity across two modes of presentation, in
> which the incompatible descriptions are dropped precisely so that the one thing both were about
> can be recognised.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

great sentence (**mahāvākya**) · direct meaning (**vācyārtha**) · implied meaning
(**lakṣyārtha**) · implication (**lakṣaṇā**) · part-abandoning implication
(**bhāga-tyāga-lakṣaṇā**) · abandoning-and-retaining implication (**jahad-ajahal-lakṣaṇā**) ·
**so'yaṃ devadattaḥ** · limiting adjunct (**upādhi**)

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** The Advaita reading avoids contradiction without weakening the sentence into a mere
comparison.
**Named evidence.** The device is part-abandoning implication (**bhāga-tyāga-lakṣaṇā**), and the
parallel is *so'yaṃ devadattaḥ*, where "then-there" and "now-here" are dropped while the person is
retained.
**Analysis.** Because only the *incompatible portions* of each direct meaning are surrendered and a
common referent is retained, the sentence still asserts strict identity of essence, which a
figurative or comparative reading would not deliver.
**Qualification.** Rāmānuja replies that the device is question-begging: if all cognition is of the
qualified, the "retained" residue of pure consciousness is not a possible object of the sentence at
all, so what Advaita calls the implied meaning is, on his account, an abstraction rather than a
referent.

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

- **2023, Question 5(c), 10 marks** — this session almost in full.
- **2022, Question 8(a), 20 marks** — the printed summary verse must be identified as a summary,
  not as a great sentence.
- **2021, Question 8(b), 15 marks** — the identity reading is the deep reason why Śaṅkara must
  lower the world's status.
- Use §6 as the one-line rival contrast in any Advaita answer.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Do not say the sentence identifies the individual with the Lord. It identifies their
   consciousness-essence after adjuncts are dropped.
2. Do not treat the Devadatta case as a decorative example. It carries the argument.
3. Do not call *Brahma satyaṃ jagan-mithyā* a great sentence.
4. Do not confuse part-abandoning implication with total abandonment (**jahal-lakṣaṇā**) or total
   retention (**ajahal-lakṣaṇā**); the Advaita device is the mixed one.
5. Do not omit the rival reading; a ten-marker without it looks like exposition rather than
   philosophy.

#### RAPID REVISION

- Conflict of direct meanings; retain the common implied meaning.
- Device: part-abandoning / abandoning-and-retaining implication.
- Parallel: "this is that Devadatta."
- Rivals read dependence, not identity.
- Summary verse ≠ great sentence.

---
### SESSION 13 — LIBERATION (MOKṢA): RECOGNITION, COMMUNION AND SERVICE

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Liberation is release from bondage. Vedānta's schools disagree about
what bondage *is*, and therefore about what release must be: if bondage is ignorance, release is
knowledge; if bondage is obstruction and separation, release is unobstructed communion; if bondage
is misrelation to a Lord who is permanently other, release is perfected service.

**Technical definition.** In Advaita liberation is **attainment of what is already attained
(prāptasya prāptiḥ)** — the removal of ignorance by knowledge, admitting **liberation while living
(jīvanmukti)**. In Viśiṣṭādvaita it is unobstructed communion and service through devotion
(**bhakti**), self-surrender (**prapatti**) and grace (**prasāda**). In Dvaita it is eternal
blissful service with **gradation among liberated souls (tāratamya)**.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

A woman searches the house for her spectacles; they are pushed up on her forehead. When she finds
them, nothing has been produced, nothing has travelled, nothing has been added to the world. What
changed was a false belief about where they were. The classical Advaita image is the same: a
necklace already round one's own neck, searched for elsewhere.

That single image decides half of Advaita's doctrine. If liberation is only the correction of a
false belief, then it can happen *now*, while the body lasts — hence liberation while living. And it
cannot be produced by action, since actions produce new things and this is not a new thing.

Rāmānuja and Madhva reject the image, and for a reason worth stating. If liberation were only a
correction of belief, devotion would be a preparatory technique to be discarded, and God would be a
means. Both insist that the liberated soul remains real and distinct, and that its relation to God
is the content of liberation, not a stage on the way to it.

#### VISUAL — ONE WORD, THREE DESTINATIONS

```text
   ADVAITA — liberation as RECOGNITION ─ asked 2023, Q7(b), 15 marks
     definition ... prāptasya prāptiḥ — ATTAINMENT OF WHAT IS ALREADY ATTAINED
     because ...... the self is ALWAYS ultimate reality; bondage is IGNORANCE ALONE
     therefore .... liberation is simply REMOVAL of ignorance through knowledge
     image ........ a necklace already hanging round one's own neck
     ONLY KNOWLEDGE LIBERATES
       action (karma) and worship (upāsanā) PURIFY the mind but do not DIRECTLY
       produce liberation => rejection of jñāna-karma-samuccaya-vāda (the doctrine
       that liberation comes from a COMBINATION of knowledge and ritual action)
     FOURFOLD QUALIFICATION (sādhana-catuṣṭaya)
       1 discrimination (viveka) — eternal vs non-eternal
       2 detachment (vairāgya)
       3 sixfold discipline (ṣaṭ-sampatti)
       4 desire for liberation (mumukṣutva)
     FROM PREPARATION TO STABLE KNOWLEDGE
       hearing (śravaṇa) ......... establishes the intended non-dual teaching under a
                                   competent teacher
       reflection (manana) ....... removes intellectual doubt, tests against contrary
                                   reasoning

       => action and worship PREPARE and PURIFY; they do NOT combine with knowledge as a

         distinctions are first employed for instruction, then withdrawn
       ! do not attribute later technical formulas (e.g. undivided-form mental mode,
         akhaṇḍākāra-vṛtti) as VERBATIM Śaṅkara; use them as later systematic explanation
     LIBERATION WHILE LIVING (jīvanmukti)

     standard sources also classify some bound souls as eternal transmigrators or
     darkness-eligible — doctrinally supportable, philosophically contested

   MADHVA vs RĀMĀNUJA — the 2022 comparison table

  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤

  │                         │ devotion + grace              │ devotion + grace           │

       AND HIERARCHY as permanent even in liberation.

     qualified non-dual ontology .. liberation as COMMUNION
     dualist ontology ............. liberation as SERVICE
```

*What the diagram shows: liberation is each school's ontology restated in soteriological form,
which is why a liberation question can be answered only by first stating the ontology.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **Advaita's definition** (asked in 2023). Liberation is not the production of a new state. It is
   **attainment of what is already attained (prāptasya prāptiḥ)**. The self is always ultimate
   reality; bondage is ignorance alone; therefore liberation is simply the removal of ignorance
   through knowledge. The metaphor is often that of finding a necklace already hanging around one's
   own neck.
2. **Knowledge alone liberates.** Śaṅkara insists that knowledge (**jñāna**) alone is the direct
   means. Action (**karma**) and worship (**upāsanā**) purify the mind but do not directly produce
   liberation. This is his rejection of **jñāna-karma-samuccaya-vāda**, the doctrine that liberation
   comes from a combination of knowledge and ritual action.
3. **The fourfold qualification (sādhana-catuṣṭaya).** Discrimination between the eternal and the
   non-eternal (**viveka**); detachment (**vairāgya**); the sixfold discipline (**ṣaṭ-sampatti**);
   and the desire for liberation (**mumukṣutva**).
4. **From preparation to stable knowledge.** Scriptural hearing (**śravaṇa**) establishes the
   intended non-dual teaching of the Upaniṣads under a competent teacher. Reasoned reflection
   (**manana**) removes intellectual doubt and tests the teaching against apparently contrary
   reasoning. Deep contemplation (**nididhyāsana**) removes entrenched contrary habits of
   identification and stabilizes direct assimilation. Action and worship prepare and purify; they
   do not combine with knowledge as a second co-producer of an already existent identity. Standard
   Advaita pedagogy also describes **superimposition and rescission (adhyāropa-apavāda)**:
   provisional distinctions are first employed for instruction and then withdrawn when non-dual
   understanding is secure. Do not attribute every later technical expression, including the
   undivided-form mental mode (**akhaṇḍākāra-vṛtti**), as a verbatim formula of Śaṅkara; use it as
   a later systematic explanation of knowledge's error-removing function.
5. **Liberation while living and after death.** A distinctive strength of Advaita is **jīvanmukti**
   — liberation while still embodied. Once ignorance is destroyed, the person knows the identity of
   the self and ultimate reality. Yet the body continues due to **commenced karma (prārabdha)**,
   just as an arrow already released continues its course. At the fall of the body comes
   **disembodied liberation (videhamukti)**, but no new liberation is produced then. Commenced
   karma is that part of past karma already set in motion; it sustains the body even after
   knowledge dawns, and no new binding karma accrues to the liberated-while-living because doership
   has been dissolved.
6. **Viśiṣṭādvaita: communion, not identity.** Liberation is the individual's recovery of its
   unobstructed nature, likeness to God, and eternal loving communion and service without numerical
   identity. The liberated self remains real and distinct. Disinterested action and scriptural
   knowledge prepare the self; sustained contemplation matures as devotion (**bhakti**).
   Self-surrender (**prapatti / śaraṇāgati**) is direct reliance on divine protection and is
   presented as an inclusive route where the longer discipline of devotion is unavailable. Divine
   grace (**prasāda**) is indispensable: devotion and surrender dispose the self toward the
   immediate God-realization that liberates. Karmic obstruction ends and attributive consciousness
   (**dharma-bhūta-jñāna**) becomes unrestricted; the self enjoys knowledge and bliss while
   remaining a dependent mode. There is no Advaitic liberation-while-living in the strict sense,
   because embodiment remains a condition of bondage and liberation is not mere epistemic sublation
   of individuality. This makes liberation relational and devotional rather than absorptive.
7. **Dvaita: blissful service and gradation.** For Madhva, liberation is eternal blissful service of
   Viṣṇu in Vaikuṇṭha. Right knowledge of Viṣṇu's supremacy, devotion and God's grace are
   indispensable. Difference from God is never abolished. A distinctive doctrine is **gradation
   (tāratamya)** — not all released souls enjoy identical bliss or rank. Standard Dvaita sources
   also classify some bound souls as eternal transmigrators or darkness-eligible; this severe
   hierarchy is doctrinally supportable but philosophically contested and should be evaluated
   rather than merely reported.
8. **Madhva versus Rāmānuja** (asked in 2022). They share a theistic ground: real God, soul and
   world, and liberation through devotion and grace. They differ on the God–self relation (an
   inseparable mode and body versus a distinct dependent substance), on the ground of liberated
   individuality (communion versus eternal difference), on knowledge and bliss (obstruction removed
   and attributive knowledge unrestricted versus degrees following a real hierarchy), on the
   equality of the liberated (alike in essential type versus retained gradation), and on the causal
   background (efficient and material cause through real modes versus efficient cause with
   dependent primordial matter as material cause). The highest-scoring conclusion is not "both are
   devotional": Rāmānuja resolves plurality through inseparable qualification, whereas Madhva
   treats qualitative difference and hierarchy as permanent even in liberation.
9. **The comparative insight.** Liberation expresses each school's ontology in soteriological form:
   non-dual ontology yields liberation as recognition, qualified non-dual ontology yields liberation
   as communion, and dualist ontology yields liberation as service.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **prāptasya prāptiḥ** — the definitional phrase quoted in the 2023 question.
- **The necklace already worn** and **the arrow already released** — the two standard images for
  recognition and for commenced karma.
- **sādhana-catuṣṭaya**; **śravaṇa–manana–nididhyāsana** — the named preparatory sequences.
- **prapatti / śaraṇāgati** and **prasāda** — Rāmānuja's surrender and grace.
- **tāratamya** — Madhva's gradation among the liberated.
- **2023, Question 7(b)** and **2022, Question 8(c)** — the two printed liberation demands.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Vedānta's three liberations are not three rewards but three diagnoses: where bondage is a false
> belief, release is recognition; where it is obstruction, release is unobstructed communion; where
> it is misrelation to a permanently other Lord, release is perfected service.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

liberation (**mokṣa**) · attainment of the already attained (**prāptasya prāptiḥ**) ·
knowledge-and-action combination (**jñāna-karma-samuccaya-vāda**) · fourfold qualification
(**sādhana-catuṣṭaya**) · hearing–reflection–contemplation
(**śravaṇa–manana–nididhyāsana**) · superimposition and rescission (**adhyāropa-apavāda**) ·
liberation while living (**jīvanmukti**) · disembodied liberation (**videhamukti**) · commenced
karma (**prārabdha**) · devotion (**bhakti**) · self-surrender (**prapatti / śaraṇāgati**) · grace
(**prasāda**) · gradation (**tāratamya**)

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Advaita's definition of liberation is forced by its diagnosis of bondage, not chosen for
its elegance.
**Named evidence.** Liberation is defined as **prāptasya prāptiḥ** and illustrated by the necklace
already worn; action and worship are assigned a purificatory role and the combination-doctrine
(**jñāna-karma-samuccaya-vāda**) is rejected.
**Analysis.** If bondage were a real state of the self, a real process would be needed to end it and
action could contribute; since bondage is ignorance about what is already the case, only cognition
can be the direct remover, and the role left for action is preparatory by strict entailment.
**Qualification.** Rāmānuja's objection is not that knowledge is useless but that a liberation
consisting in the cancellation of individuality leaves nothing to enjoy the freedom, so the
Advaitin must accept that his elegance is bought by making devotion and the person provisional.

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

- **2023, Question 7(b), 15 marks** — "attainment of that which is already attained" and how
  Śaṅkara illustrates it; §1–§5 with the necklace and the arrow, plus your own comment.
- **2022, Question 8(c), 15 marks** — Madhva's liberation versus Rāmānuja's; §8's table is the
  answer's spine.
- **2018, Question 7(a), 20 marks** — the four-school liberation comparison; combine §6, §7 with
  Session 16.
- **2019, Question 8(a), 20 marks** — consequence 5 uses the preparatory status of devotion.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Do not write that Advaita makes action irrelevant. It makes action preparatory and denies it
   direct causal efficacy for liberation.
2. Do not attribute liberation-while-living to Viśiṣṭādvaita or Dvaita in the strict Advaita sense.
3. Do not write that all liberated souls are equal in Madhva. Gradation is doctrinal.
4. Do not reduce the 2022 comparison to "both are devotional." Name the structural difference.
5. Do not present commenced karma as a residue of ignorance; ignorance is destroyed, the
   already-fructifying karma is not.

#### RAPID REVISION

- Advaita: removal of ignorance; already attained; liberation while living; commenced karma.
- Preparation: fourfold qualification; hearing–reflection–contemplation; rejection of the
  combination doctrine.
- Viśiṣṭādvaita: devotion, surrender, grace; unrestricted attributive consciousness; communion.
- Dvaita: service, grace, permanent difference, gradation.
- Formula: recognition · communion · service.

### SESSION 14 — INSEPARABLE DEPENDENCE (APṚTHAKSIDDHI) AND THE BODY–SOUL RELATION

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Inseparable dependence is Rāmānuja's answer to the question that
defeats both his rivals: how can there be *real* many inside a *real* one? His answer is that some
things are genuinely distinct yet incapable of existing or being conceived apart from what they
qualify.

**Technical definition.** **Apṛthaksiddhi** is inseparable existence: conscious selves (**cit**) and
non-conscious matter (**acit**) exist in an inseparable relation with ultimate reality, cannot exist
or be conceived independently of it, and yet are not identical with it. An **inseparable qualifier
(apṛthaksiddha-viśeṣaṇa)** is one that cannot stand apart from the substance it qualifies.

#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Think of the relation between a body and the self that lives in it. The body is not the self, and
the self is not the body; they are genuinely distinct. Yet the body is not an independent thing
standing beside the self. It is supported by the self, controlled by the self, and exists for the
self's purposes.

Rāmānuja proposes that God stands to the world and to souls exactly as a self stands to its body.
This gives him everything he needs at once. Unity: there is only one substantive whole, God
qualified by his modes. Plurality: souls and matter are real, not illusions. Religion: the relation
between God and the soul is a genuine relation, so devotion is not a ladder to be kicked away.

Two warnings must be attached immediately. "Body" here does not mean flesh; it means whatever is
supported, controlled and used by an indwelling self. And inseparability is not identity — the most
common error in this topic is to read Rāmānuja as a soft Advaitin.

#### VISUAL — THE MIDDLE PATH BETWEEN IDENTITY AND PLURALITY

```text
   THE PROBLEM RĀMĀNUJA IS SOLVING
     ADVAITA .... unity is real, plurality is appearance => world and devotion downgraded
     DVAITA ..... plurality is real, unity is only dependence => non-dual sentences strained
     RĀMĀNUJA ... can plurality be REAL and yet INSIDE unity?

   INSEPARABLE DEPENDENCE (apṛthaksiddhi)
     conscious selves (cit) + non-conscious matter (acit)
        ► exist in an INSEPARABLE relation with ultimate reality
        ► CANNOT exist or be conceived independently of it
        ► are NOT identical with it
     => the central conceptual instrument by which qualified non-dualism combines
       unity and plurality

   INSEPARABLE QUALIFIER (apṛthaksiddha-viśeṣaṇa)
     a qualifier that CANNOT STAND APART from the substance it qualifies
     the attribute is REAL and DISTINCT — but INSEPARABLE
     STRONGER than a mere conceptual relation · WEAKER than identity

   BODY–SOUL RELATION (śarīra-śarīrī-bhāva) ─ asked 2024, Q5(e), 10 marks
     selves and world = ultimate reality's BODY
     ultimate reality = their indwelling SOUL
     THREE CONDITIONS of being a body (state all three — this is the answer's spine):
       1 entirely SUPPORTED by it
       2 entirely CONTROLLED by it
       3 exists FOR ITS PURPOSES
     ! "body" ≠ gross physical body. It means WHATEVER IS SUPPORTED, CONTROLLED AND
       USED by an indwelling self.
     ! the analogy is ASYMMETRIC: an ordinary self is affected by its body's defects;
       God is NOT. The parallel holds for support-control-purpose, not for vulnerability.

   WHAT IT BUYS
     preserves NON-DUALITY .... there is only ONE ultimate substantive whole
     preserves REAL DIFFERENCE  cit and acit are genuine MODES, not illusory appearances
     preserves DEVOTION ....... the God–soul relation remains meaningful
     => in one stroke it answers Advaita's monism AND Dvaita's pluralism

   THE MAJOR TRAP
     x apṛthaksiddhi is NOT identity
     Rāmānuja does NOT say soul and world are numerically identical with God;
     he says they are INSEPARABLY RELATED as real modes of God

   FORMULA FOR QUICK RECALL
     Advaita ......... identity WITHOUT real plurality
     Viśiṣṭādvaita ... inseparable plurality WITHIN unity
     Dvaita .......... eternal plurality WITH dependence
```

*What the diagram shows: one relation does four jobs — metaphysical, hermeneutic, religious and
polemical — which is why it, rather than any doctrine of God, is the name of Rāmānuja's system in
the syllabus.*

#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **The definition.** Inseparable dependence is Rāmānuja's doctrine of inseparable existence.
   Conscious selves and non-conscious matter exist in an inseparable relation with ultimate
   reality; they cannot exist or be conceived independently of it; yet they are not identical with
   it. This is the central conceptual instrument by which qualified non-dualism combines unity and
   plurality.
2. **The inseparable qualifier.** An inseparable qualifier (**apṛthaksiddha-viśeṣaṇa**) is one that
   cannot stand apart from the substance it qualifies. The attribute is real and distinct, but
   inseparable. This is stronger than a mere conceptual relation and weaker than identity — and
   locating it precisely between those two is the whole point.
3. **The body–soul relation** (asked directly in 2024). Rāmānuja explains the God–world relation
   through the body–soul analogy. Selves and world are ultimate reality's body; ultimate reality is
   their indwelling soul. This means they are entirely supported by it, entirely controlled by it,
   and exist for its purposes. All three conditions must be stated; a two-condition answer is
   incomplete.
4. **The two indispensable qualifications.** First, body here does not mean gross physical body
   only; it means that which is controlled, sustained and used by an indwelling self. Second, the
   analogy is deliberately asymmetric: an ordinary self is affected by its body's defects and
   limitations, whereas God's essential auspicious nature is untouched by the imperfections of the
   world-body. The parallel holds for support, control and purpose, not for vulnerability — and the
   2024 question's directive, "critically discuss," is answered largely by drawing this limit.
5. **What the doctrine buys.** It preserves non-duality because there is only one ultimate
   substantive whole. It preserves real difference because conscious and non-conscious modes are
   genuine, not illusory appearances. It preserves devotion because the relation between God and
   soul remains meaningful. In one stroke it answers Advaita's monism and Dvaita's pluralism.
6. **The major trap.** Inseparable dependence is not identity. Rāmānuja does not say soul and world
   are numerically identical with God; he says they are inseparably related as real modes of God.
   Answers that gloss the doctrine as "a kind of oneness" lose the distinction on which the whole
   school stands.
7. **The recall formula.** Advaita: identity without real plurality. Viśiṣṭādvaita: inseparable
   plurality within unity. Dvaita: eternal plurality with dependence.
8. **How Madhva replies.** For Madhva, calling souls God's body already concedes too much: a body
   is an instrument of its soul, and a real self cannot be an instrument of another self without
   loss of its own substantiality. He therefore keeps dependence and drops the body-relation
   altogether — which is precisely the difference tested in the 2022 liberation comparison.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **apṛthaksiddha-viśeṣaṇa** — the technical term for an inseparable qualifier.
- **śarīra-śarīrī-bhāva** — the body–soul relation, with its three conditions.
- **cit and acit** — the two classes of mode.
- **Rāmānuja, *Śrī-bhāṣya* and *Vedārthasaṅgraha*** — where the relation is expounded.
- **2024, Question 5(e)** — "In Viśiṣṭādvaita philosophy, the relationship between God and the world
  is parallel to that between an individual self and its body. Critically discuss."

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Inseparable dependence is Rāmānuja's refusal of a false choice: he denies that the many must
> either dissolve into the one or stand outside it, and proposes instead that some things are real
> precisely *as* qualifications of something that could not be described without them.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

inseparable dependence (**apṛthaksiddhi**) · inseparable qualifier
(**apṛthaksiddha-viśeṣaṇa**) · body–soul relation (**śarīra-śarīrī-bhāva**) · conscious (**cit**) ·
non-conscious (**acit**) · mode (**prakāra**) · substantive whole · indwelling controller
(**antaryāmin**) · support, control, purpose

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** The body–soul analogy is an argument, not an illustration, because it specifies
conditions rather than resemblance.
**Named evidence.** Rāmānuja defines a body as what is entirely supported by, entirely controlled
by, and exists for the purposes of an indwelling self; selves and world satisfy all three with
respect to ultimate reality.
**Analysis.** Because the definition is functional rather than material, it applies to conscious
selves as readily as to matter, which is exactly what Rāmānuja needs if souls are to be real modes
without becoming either illusions or independent substances.
**Qualification.** The analogy nonetheless breaks at a crucial point — an ordinary self suffers the
defects of its body while God's perfection is unaffected — so the relation must be read as
one-directional dependence, and Madhva presses precisely this asymmetry to argue that a real self
cannot be another's body at all.

#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

- **2024, Question 5(e), 10 marks** — the analogy and its critical limits; §3, §4 and §8.
- **2018, Question 7(c), 15 marks** — inseparable dependence is the positive doctrine that replaces
  illusion-doctrine.
- **2022, Question 8(c), 15 marks** — the God–self relation row of the comparison table is this
  session.
- **2021, Question 8(b), 15 marks** — the body–soul relation is the ground of Rāmānuja's realism
  about the world.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. Do not treat inseparable dependence as identity or as "qualified oneness" without the mode
   language.
2. Do not restrict "body" to the gross physical body; the definition is functional.
3. Do not run the analogy symmetrically; God is not affected by the world-body's defects.
4. Do not state only two of the three conditions.
5. Do not confuse the mode (**prakāra**) with a part; parts can be separated, modes cannot.

#### RAPID REVISION

- Real, distinct, inseparable — stronger than conceptual, weaker than identity.
- Body = supported, controlled, purposed; not flesh.
- Buys unity, difference and devotion simultaneously.
- Not identity; Madhva rejects the body-relation while keeping dependence.
- Formula: identity without plurality · plurality within unity · plurality with dependence.
