transferred to the not-self and the not-self's to the self — so the correction removes a false
identification rather than adding a state. Preparation is real but indirect: the fourfold
qualification (**sādhana-catuṣṭaya**), then hearing, reflection and contemplation
(**śravaṇa–manana–nididhyāsana**). Action (**karma**) and worship (**upāsanā**) purify but do not
combine with knowledge, which is Śaṅkara's rejection of the knowledge-and-action combination
(**jñāna-karma-samuccaya-vāda**). The corollary is liberation while living (**jīvanmukti**): the
body continues by commenced karma (**prārabdha**), like an arrow already released, and disembodied
liberation (**videhamukti**) adds nothing new.

**Comment.** The definition is forced by the diagnosis, not chosen for elegance: if bondage is a
mistaken belief, only cognition can be its direct remover. Its strength is that it explains why
liberation can be present-tense and why it cannot be lost. Its cost is what Rāmānuja presses — if
individuality was never ultimately real, it is unclear who enjoys the freedom, and devotion is
reduced to preparation. The doctrine is therefore coherent within its own criterion of reality and
contestable precisely at that criterion.

**Why this earns marks.** It explains the definition, supplies both illustrations with their
mechanism, keeps the preparatory apparatus in its correct subordinate place, and delivers genuine
independent comment rather than a restatement.

#### Solved PYQ 17 — 2024, Question 5(e), 10 marks

> **Printed question.** “In Viśiṣṭādvaita philosophy, the relationship between God and the world
> is parallel to that between an individual self and its body.” Critically discuss.

**Directive read.** "Critically" in a ten-marker means one clear limit, not a survey. The three
conditions of bodyhood must be stated before the limit is drawn.

**Model answer (191 words).**

The statement is accurate as far as it goes. In Rāmānuja's system selves (**cit**) and matter
(**acit**) constitute ultimate reality's body (**śarīra**), while ultimate reality is their
indwelling soul (**śarīrin**). Bodyhood is defined by three conditions: what is entirely supported
by an indwelling self, entirely controlled by it, and existing for its purposes. All three hold of
the world with respect to God.

Two clarifications are essential. First, "body" is functional, not physical: it means whatever is
supported, controlled and used by an indwelling self, which is why conscious selves can also be
called God's body. Second, the relation is inseparable dependence
(**apṛthaksiddhi**): the modes are real and distinct but cannot exist or be conceived apart from
their substance — stronger than a conceptual relation, weaker than identity.

Critically, the parallel is asymmetric. An ordinary self is affected, limited and pained by its
body's defects; God's essential auspicious nature is untouched by the world's imperfections. The
analogy therefore holds for support, control and purpose, and fails for vulnerability. Madhva
presses this further: a real self cannot be another's instrument at all.

**Why this earns marks.** It states the three defining conditions, corrects the two predictable
misreadings, and answers "critically" with a precise structural limit and a named rival objection
rather than generalised praise.

#### Solved PYQ 18 — 2025, Question 5(d), 10 marks

> **Printed question.** Examine Rāmānuja’s seven objections against Māyāvāda of Advaita.

**Directive read.** "Examine" requires more than enumeration. All seven must appear, but at least
one reply and a verdict on relative force are needed within the word band.

**Model answer (200 words).**

The sevenfold charge of incoherence (**sapta-anupapatti**) attacks the doctrine of ignorance at
seven points; names and order vary slightly across expositions.

**Locus (āśraya):** ignorance can reside neither in the absolute, which it would defile, nor in the
individual, which it produces — mutual dependence (**anyonyāśraya**). **Concealment
(tirodhāna):** a self-luminous reality cannot be concealed without destroying its nature.
**Nature (svarūpa):** something neither real nor unreal has no coherent nature. **Indefinability
(anirvacanīyatva):** a third category names the problem instead of solving it. **Means of knowing
(pramāṇa):** no instrument establishes a positive ignorance; "I do not know" reports an absence.
**Remover (nivartaka):** contentless knowledge cannot remove it, and determinate knowledge lies
within it. **Cessation (nivṛtti):** if real it cannot be removed, if unreal there is nothing to
remove.

Advaita replies throughout: the Vivaraṇa and Bhāmatī locations of ignorance; the cloud that obstructs
the viewer, not the sun; grades measured by sublation (**bādha**); witness-perception and
postulation; the undivided-form mental mode; and cessation as the absolute simply remaining.

Examined, the force is uneven. The replies on concealment and cessation are strong; locus and means
of knowing leave a genuine residue, since neither is settled without granting Advaita's own
standpoint.

**Why this earns marks.** It names all seven with their technical terms inside a tight band,
supplies the replies compactly, and answers the directive with a verdict on which objections
actually survive.

#### Solved PYQ 19 — 2025, Question 6(b), 15 marks

> **Printed question.** How is Brahman conceptualised in Advaita philosophy as both Nimitta and
> Upādāna Kāraṇa of the World? Discuss with suitable examples.

**Directive read.** The school is specified as Advaita, and examples are demanded explicitly. The
examples must be interpreted, not merely named.

**Model answer (284 words).**

Efficient cause (**nimitta-kāraṇa**) is the intelligent agency that brings an effect about; material
cause (**upādāna-kāraṇa**) is that out of which it is constituted. Ordinarily these are two things —
potter and clay. Advaita collapses them into one, and the reason is negative before it is positive.

Since nothing exists independently of ultimate reality, there can be no second material out of which
the world could be made. Ultimate reality must therefore be both the intelligence behind the world
and the material basis of its appearance. The classic image is the **spider** spinning the web out
of itself: one source supplies both the agency and the stuff, without recourse to anything external.

But the doctrine is immediately qualified by apparent transformation (**vivarta**). If ultimate
reality really became the world, it would change, and a changing absolute is no longer
unconditioned. The second image supplies the correction: the **rope and the snake**. The rope is the
substrate of the snake-appearance without ever becoming a snake; it is not modified, yet nothing in
the appearance is independent of it. "Material cause" therefore means *substrate*, not transformed
stuff. Contrast **milk and curd**, where a real transformation genuinely alters the cause — the
model Advaita rejects.

Two comparative lines complete the answer. Rāmānuja also makes ultimate reality both causes, but
causation is real: subtle modes become gross. Madhva denies material causality of God altogether,
assigning it to dependent primordial matter.

Critically, Rāmānuja objects that a substrate which never changes is not a material cause in any
recognisable sense. The Advaitin's reply holds only if sublatability is accepted as a criterion of
reality — which is where the dispute finally rests.

**Why this earns marks.** It defines both causes, gives the negative argument that motivates the
identification, uses three examples as argument rather than ornament, qualifies with apparent
transformation, and closes with the named objection and the criterion at stake.

#### Solved PYQ 20 — 2025, Question 8(a), 20 marks

> **Printed question.** Discuss the idea of Bimba-pratibimbavāda as presented in Vedānta
> philosophy along with its soteriological significance.

**Directive read.** Two halves, explicitly. An ontological exposition without the soteriological
significance forfeits roughly half the marks.

**Model answer (400 words).**

**The model.** The original–reflection model (**bimba-pratibimba-vāda**) takes ultimate reality or
pure consciousness as the original (**bimba**) and the empirical self as its reflection
(**pratibimba**) in the internal organ conditioned by ignorance. Its governing intuition is that
the original remains unaffected by defects, motion or multiplicity in the reflecting media, as one
face is unchanged though many mirrors — cracked, tilted, moving — yield differently conditioned
images. Plurality is explained without dividing the absolute.

**Its internal variants.** Some later formulations distinguish the reflection of consciousness in
total cosmic appearance as the Lord (**īśvara**), and its reflections in individual ignorance or
internal organs as individual selves. Others treat the Lord as original relative to the individual,
or prefer alternative models: limitation (**avaccheda-vāda**), in which one space appears as many
pot-spaces (**ghaṭākāśa**), and semblance (**ābhāsa-vāda**), which stresses the ontological thinness
of individuality. These are later explanatory alternatives rather than one uniform proposition
attributable without qualification to Śaṅkara, and an answer should not assert that the Lord is
simultaneously the original and a reflection without naming the model used.

**Soteriological significance.** The theory is not an ontological curiosity; it exists to make
liberation intelligible. A reflection possesses no being of its own; it borrows all of it from the
original. Therefore when ignorance is removed, nothing real is destroyed and nothing travels: the
finite self is known never to have possessed independent reality apart from original consciousness.
Liberation is accordingly recognition of non-difference, not the movement, merging or destruction of
one substance by another. This supports the Advaita definition of liberation as attainment of what
is already attained (**prāptasya prāptiḥ**), and explains why liberation while living
(**jīvanmukti**) is possible: correcting a false claim requires no new production.

**Critical assessment.** The model's suggestive power should not be pressed as optics. A formless
original, a formless medium and a non-dual reality make literal reflection impossible; reflection
also normally requires a spatial gap between original and mirror, which non-duality forbids. Madhva
uses the same original–reflection language to argue the opposite conclusion — that the dependent
image is *really different* from its original — showing how far the metaphor under-determines
the metaphysics.

The balanced verdict is that the model is an explanatory family with named variants: strong as a
soteriological argument, weak as a literal account of how consciousness individuates.

**Why this earns marks.** It answers both halves in proportion, distinguishes the model's variants
with the required attribution caution, ties the soteriology to the school's definition of
liberation, and closes with a limit that includes a named rival use of the same metaphor.

---
### ORIGINAL SOLVED MAINS PRACTICE

> Six original questions in the examination's own idiom — two at 10 marks, two at 15 and two at 20 —
> with complete model answers in the declared bands. These are learner practice, not previous-year
> questions.

#### Original 1 — 10 marks

> **Question.** Distinguish between the dependently real (**mithyā**) and the wholly non-existent
> (**tuccha**) in Advaita Vedānta, and state why the distinction is indispensable.

**Model answer (179 words).**

The wholly non-existent (**tuccha**) is what is never presented in any experience — the standard
examples are the hare's horn and the son of a barren woman. It cannot be cancelled, because
cancellation presupposes a prior appearance.

The dependently real (**mithyā**) is what is presented and later sublated. The world appears,
coheres, obeys laws and supports action, yet is denied independent, unsublatable reality; the rope's
snake is presented vividly enough to cause flight and vanishes when the rope is recognised.

The distinction is indispensable for three reasons. First, without it Advaita collapses into
nihilism, since "the world is unreal" would place the world where the hare's horn stands. Second,
the middle grade is what makes sublation (**bādha**) meaningful: only what has appeared can be
cancelled by knowledge. Third, it protects the empirical validity of ethics, scripture and the means
of valid knowledge, all of which operate at the empirical level (**vyāvahārika**).

The cost, which critics press, is that a third ontological grade requires the sublation criterion
that realists refuse to grant.

**Why this earns marks.** It defines both terms with their standard examples, gives three distinct
reasons rather than one repeated, and ends with the objection that the distinction itself invites.

#### Original 2 — 10 marks

> **Question.** "Nirguṇa does not mean blankness." Examine this claim with reference to Śaṅkara's
> conception of ultimate reality.

**Model answer (191 words).**

The claim is correct, and its defence turns on identifying what qualitylessness negates.

Qualityless (**nirguṇa**) denies limiting, relational, empirical and objectifiable attributes — the
sort of predicates that would place the absolute as one item among knowable items. It does not deny
nature. Being–consciousness–bliss (**sat-cit-ānanda**) is retained as the essential definition
(**svarūpa-lakṣaṇa**), and it expresses the absolute's very essence rather than a list of accidental
predicates added to a substrate. Determinate qualities such as creatorhood, omniscience, lordship
and moral governance are not denied outright either; they are assigned to the Lord (**īśvara**), the
absolute conditioned by illusion at the empirical level.

The claim also has a positive ground. The absolute is self-luminous (**svayaṃ-prakāśa**): it is not
known as an object with predicates but is self-revealed as the condition of all knowing. A blank
could not perform that function.

Examined critically, the defence holds internally but does not disarm Rāmānuja. If all cognition is
of the qualified, a nature expressible by no determinate predicate is difficult to distinguish in
practice from an unknowable, and Advaita must lean on self-luminosity rather than description.

**Why this earns marks.** It states what is negated and what is retained, supplies the positive
self-luminosity ground, and concedes the precise point at which the rival objection retains force.

#### Original 3 — 15 marks

> **Question.** Compare the accounts of the individual self offered by Śaṅkara, Rāmānuja and Madhva,
> and assess which best explains both bondage and moral responsibility.

**Model answer (288 words).**

**Śaṅkara.** The individual (**jīva**) is ultimate reality appearing finite through ignorance,
explained by limitation (**avaccheda-vāda**, pot-space), reflection (**pratibimba-vāda**, the
mirrored face) and semblance (**ābhāsa-vāda**). Bondage is superimposition (**adhyāsa**), not a real
change in the self. Moral responsibility is fully binding at the empirical level and denied ultimacy.

**Rāmānuja.** The individual is a real, atomic, conscious mode (**prakāra**) inseparably dependent
on ultimate reality, related as body to indwelling soul. Bondage is karmic obstruction contracting
attributive consciousness (**dharma-bhūta-jñāna**); liberation removes the obstruction without
removing the self.

**Madhva.** The individual is a real, distinct, dependent substance eternally different from God,
with real difference among selves. Bondage is real ignorance and real karmic bondage; liberation is
eternal service with gradation (**tāratamya**).

**Assessment.** On *bondage*, Śaṅkara's account is the most economical: it explains why bondage can
end without anything real being destroyed, since a false identification simply lapses. Its
difficulty is the locus of ignorance, which his own tradition answers in two incompatible ways.
Rāmānuja's account is the most naturalistic — obstruction is a matter of degree and admits gradual
release — but it must explain why a real mode of a perfect God becomes obstructed at all.

On *moral responsibility*, the ranking reverses. Śaṅkara affirms obligation while denying its
ultimacy, which critics read as weakening moral realism. Rāmānuja and Madhva keep the agent real
throughout, and Madhva keeps difference constitutive, so responsibility is never provisional — at
the cost of a permanent hierarchy among souls that strains moral universality.

The graded verdict: Śaṅkara explains bondage best, Madhva grounds responsibility most firmly, and
Rāmānuja's inseparable dependence is the most balanced compromise between the two demands.

**Why this earns marks.** It compares on a common axis, splits the assessment by the two criteria
the question names, and gives a graded verdict with each school's specific cost.

#### Original 4 — 15 marks

> **Question.** "The disagreement between Vedānta's schools is finally a disagreement about the
> criterion of reality." Critically examine.

**Model answer (282 words).**

The claim is substantially correct, and testing it clarifies why the schools cannot refute one
another directly.

Advaita's criterion is **sublation (bādha)**: what is never sublated
(**trikālābādhita**) is ultimately real; what is presented and sublated is dependently real; what is
never presented is wholly non-existent. This single criterion generates the three levels of reality,
the status of the world, the indescribability of illusion, and liberation as the removal of
ignorance.

Rāmānuja's criterion is **qualified cognizability**: *sarvaṃ vijñānaṃ saviśeṣam*, all cognition is
of the qualified. What cannot be cognised as qualified cannot be established. From this follow the
rejection of a qualityless absolute, the reality of the world, and inseparable dependence
(**apṛthaksiddhi**) as the way real modes can inhere in one substantive whole.

Madhva's criterion is **independence**: what is independent (**svatantra**) is supremely real, and
whatever is dependent (**paratantra**) is nonetheless fully real. From this follow the reality of
selves and world, fivefold difference (**pañcavidhabheda**), and the refusal of any grade between
being and non-being.

This explains the structure of the debates. Rāmānuja's seven objections do not disprove ignorance;
they show that it cannot be established on *his* criterion. Advaita's reply to Nyāya on error does
not disprove realism; it shows an anomaly on the sublation criterion. Each school's strongest
argument is therefore internal.

Critically, the thesis should not be over-generalised. The schools also disagree about scriptural
hermeneutics — whether identity-sentences or difference-sentences govern — and about what a
soteriology must deliver. But these disagreements track the criterion: a school that grades reality
by sublation will read identity-sentences as literal, and one that grades it by independence will
not.

**Why this earns marks.** It converts a broad claim into three named criteria, shows how each
generates that school's doctrines, explains the dialectical stalemate, and qualifies the thesis
instead of merely endorsing it.

#### Original 5 — 20 marks

> **Question.** Evaluate the doctrine of indescribable illusion (**māyā**) as an explanatory
> principle, with reference to the objections it attracts and the replies available to Advaita.

**Model answer (394 words).**

**What the doctrine claims.** Illusion is the beginningless (**anādi**), dependent power through
which a non-dual absolute appears as a plural world. It is neither being (**sat**) nor non-being
(**asat**) but indescribable (**anirvacanīya**), and it exercises two powers: concealment
(**āvaraṇa-śakti**), which hides the absolute's nature, and projection (**vikṣepa-śakti**), which
throws up names and forms.

**Its explanatory work.** It performs four jobs simultaneously. It explains plurality without
dividing the absolute; it explains bondage without a real change in the self; it explains why the
world is coherent, public and law-governed while remaining sublatable; and, with superimposition
(**adhyāsa**), it explains error by the same machinery that explains cosmology. Few principles in
Indian philosophy are asked to do so much with so little ontological commitment.

**The objections.** Rāmānuja's sevenfold charge presses at every seam. Its locus (**āśraya**) is
untenable — in the absolute it defiles, in the individual it presupposes what it explains. It cannot
conceal a self-luminous reality. Its nature is incoherent if it is neither real nor unreal. A third
category is a refusal to answer. No means of valid knowledge establishes a positive ignorance.
Neither its remover nor its cessation can be coherently specified. Madhva adds that a real world and
real ignorance explain everything the doctrine explains, without the extra principle.

**The replies.** The Vivaraṇa school makes the absolute the locus without modification; the Bhāmatī
school locates ignorance in the individual and appeals to beginningless mutual dependence.
Concealment is relative to the viewer, as cloud to sun. The trichotomy is defended as grades of
sublatability rather than as a violation of excluded middle. Witness-perception of "I do not know"
and postulation from the fact that knowledge removes bondage are offered as means. The undivided-form
mental mode removes ignorance and then subsides; cessation is the absolute simply remaining.

**Evaluation.** Judged as an explanation, the doctrine is powerful and economical: one principle,
two powers, four explanatory jobs. Judged as an ontology, it is fragile at two points — the locus and
the proof — where the replies presuppose the standpoint under dispute. The decisive issue is
methodological: everything turns on whether sublatability grades reality. Grant it, and illusion is
the most economical account of appearance available. Refuse it, and the doctrine names the problem
of plurality rather than solving it.

**Why this earns marks.** It separates claim, explanatory work, objections, replies and evaluation;
names the sub-schools; and locates the verdict in the criterion at stake rather than declaring a
winner.

#### Original 6 — 20 marks

> **Question.** "Every doctrine in the Vedānta clause is a settlement of one question: is the
> relation of the finite to the absolute identity, qualification or difference?" Discuss with
> reference to at least four printed syllabus terms.

**Model answer (392 words).**

The claim is a strong organising thesis, and testing it against four printed terms shows both its
power and its limit.

**The world (jagat).** Under identity it is an apparent transformation (**vivarta**) — dependently
real (**mithyā**) but not wholly non-existent, cancelled by knowledge of its substrate as the snake
is by the rope. Under qualification it is real, the non-conscious (**acit**) dimension of the
absolute's body, produced by a real transformation of modes from subtle to gross. Under difference
it is real and eternally distinct, with God as efficient cause and dependent primordial matter as
material cause. One question, three cosmologies.

**The individual self (jīva).** Under identity it is the absolute appearing finite through
ignorance, explained by limitation, reflection or semblance. Under qualification it is a real atomic
mode (**prakāra**) whose attributive consciousness contracts and expands. Under difference it is a
real dependent substance, with difference among selves equally real.

**Liberation (mokṣa).** Under identity it is attainment of what is already attained
(**prāptasya prāptiḥ**), admitting liberation while living. Under qualification it is unobstructed
communion and service through devotion, surrender and grace. Under difference it is eternal service
with gradation (**tāratamya**).

**Inseparable dependence (apṛthaksiddhi) and fivefold difference (pañcavidhabheda).** These two
terms are the thesis made explicit. The first names the settlement "qualification": real modes that
cannot exist or be conceived apart from their substance — stronger than a conceptual relation,
weaker than identity. The second names the settlement "difference": five real and eternal
differences, including the two internal ones among souls and among material things that make
plurality irreducible.

**The limit of the thesis.** Two qualifications are needed. First, the wider schools show that the
three settlements are not exhaustive: Nimbārka treats difference and non-difference as equally real
aspects of one relation, Vallabha proposes unchanged real manifestation
(**avikṛta-pariṇāma**), and the Caitanya tradition treats the relation as inconceivable by finite
reduction. Second, the settlements are downstream of a prior question — the criterion of reality —
since sublation, qualified cognizability and independence generate identity, qualification and
difference respectively.

The thesis therefore holds as a map of the clause and fails as a complete taxonomy. Its real value
is examination-facing: it converts eleven printed terms into one question asked eleven times, which
is exactly how a comparative answer should be built.

**Why this earns marks.** It applies the thesis systematically to four named syllabus terms, uses
each school's technical vocabulary, and then limits the thesis with the wider schools and the prior
criterion question instead of merely confirming it.

---
## OPTIONAL ADVANCED DEPTH — NOT REQUIRED FOR A CORE ANSWER

### Prerequisite before this block is usable

Nothing in this block is needed for a complete answer to any of the twenty owned previous-year
questions. The Basic block above is independently answer-complete: it contains every printed
syllabus term, every school position, every named objection with its reply, every verified question
with a full solution, and the graded verdicts. Read this block only after that material is secure,
and use at most one enrichment move per answer — normally in the evaluative or comparative
paragraph, never in place of a core doctrine.

### ADVANCED MODULE 1 — The locus of ignorance as an unresolved explanatory problem

**The issue.** Does ignorance belong to the individual self, to ultimate reality in some qualified
sense, or to neither straightforwardly? The Basic block records the two standard strategies; the
advanced point is what the disagreement *shows*.

**The readings.** The Bhāmatī line, associated with Vācaspati Miśra, emphasises a locus in the
individual and appeals to the beginningless (**anādi**) character of the individual–ignorance
relation. The Vivaraṇa line, associated with Prakāśātman, emphasises ultimate reality as the
support (**āśraya**) of ignorance without being modified by it. A deflationary exam-safe view keeps
the dispute secondary and treats it as a post-experiential explanatory problem rather than a claim
about a second reality.

**Objection and counter-reply.** If ultimate reality is pure consciousness, ignorance cannot belong
to it; if the individual is ignorance-produced, it cannot ground ignorance either. Later Advaitins
reply that the question arises only from within the standpoint that ignorance itself generates, so
it is a problem about explanation rather than about ontology.

**Where one line of this may be used.** In the evaluative paragraph of a twenty-marker on illusion,
error or Rāmānuja's objections — to show that the residue on the locus objection is acknowledged
inside the tradition, not merely alleged from outside.

**Source trail.** Śaṃkara, *Brahma-sūtra-bhāṣya*; Vācaspati Miśra, *Bhāmatī*; Prakāśātman,
*Pañcapādikā-vivaraṇa*.

### ADVANCED MODULE 2 — Beyond one model of individuality

**The issue.** Is the individual best explained by reflection, limitation or semblance once the
Basic treatment is understood? The Basic block names all three and their common conclusion; the
advanced point is how to read their coexistence.

**The readings.** Each model addresses a different explanatory pressure: limitation answers how one
can appear as many, reflection answers why individuality is phenomenologically vivid, and semblance
answers how the many can be kept ontologically weightless.

**Objection and counter-reply.** Multiple models suggest doctrinal instability. The reply is that
they can be read as pedagogic metaphors solving different pressures rather than as mutually
exclusive new doctrines — while conceding that the tradition never fully unified them.

**Where one line of this may be used.** In a fifteen-marker on the individual and the witness, or
on Madhva's individual, as a single sentence acknowledging internal plurality without abandoning
the shared conclusion.

**Source trail.** Śaṃkara, *Adhyāsa-bhāṣya*; later Advaita sub-commentarial literature; T.M.P.
Mahadevan.

### ADVANCED MODULE 3 — Madhva's hierarchy of souls and differentiated liberation

**The issue.** Does Dvaita merely insist on five real differences, or also on a graded ontology of
souls with unequal destinies?

**The readings.** The minimal examination reading centres the five differences. The fuller doctrinal
reading adds gradation (**tāratamya**) among liberated souls and the classification of bound souls
as liberation-eligible, eternal transmigrators or darkness-eligible.

**Objection and counter-reply.** Graded souls compromise moral universality. Madhva treats the
hierarchy as a metaphysical fact rather than an injustice produced by history; many readers
nonetheless find this the sharpest normative difficulty in the system.

**Where one line of this may be used.** In the evaluative close of a liberation comparison, to name
the cost of Dvaita's realism precisely rather than generically.

**Source trail.** Madhva, *Anuvyākhyāna*; S. Radhakrishnan; C.D. Sharma.

### ADVANCED MODULE 4 — Later Vedānta as live alternatives rather than appendices

**The issue.** Should the schools beyond the principal three be treated as marginal appendices, or
as live options about difference, manifestation and devotion?

**The readings.** A narrow examination habit privileges Śaṅkara, Rāmānuja and Madhva. A broader
doctrinal reading keeps Nimbārka, Vallabha and the Caitanya tradition as genuine alternatives on
world-status and liberation — which is why the Basic block treats them as core, since the verified
papers ask about them directly.

**Objection and counter-reply.** Treating them expansively risks turning optional notes into hidden
syllabus expansion. That is exactly why only the enrichment stays here: mention a further later
development only when a question or comparison already opens the door.

**Where one line of this may be used.** In a comparative twenty-marker, as a single sentence showing
that the identity/qualification/difference fork is not exhaustive.

**Source trail.** Nimbārka and Vallabha traditions in standard surveys of Indian philosophy; S.
Radhakrishnan; S. Chatterjee and D. Datta.

### What not to over-study

Exhaustive sub-commentarial taxonomies, every internal Advaita school label, and later
devotional-Vedānta genealogies add nothing once the printed terms and the twenty owned questions are
fully covered. The same applies to detailed doxographies of the unique differentiator in Dvaita and
to exhaustive lists of great sentences beyond the standard four.

### When this block would stop being optional

If a future paper directly asks the locus of ignorance, the limitation or semblance models by name,
or Madhva's gradation and hierarchy as the principal demand, the corresponding module would move
into the Basic block. Until then, one enrichment move per answer is the ceiling; two is already too
many.

### Three genuinely contested matters to report as contested

1. **The relation of illusion and ignorance.** Some Advaita formulations treat them as broadly
   interchangeable; others separate cosmic illusion associated with the Lord from individual
   ignorance associated with the individual self. State the distinction and record the variation.
2. **Ownership of later explanatory models.** The original–reflection family, the limitation model
   and the undivided-form mental mode are later systematic explanations. Do not present them as
   verbatim formulations of Śaṅkara, and name the relative model whenever the Lord is described as
   original or as reflection.
3. **Dvaita's unique differentiator and graded destinies.** Expositions of the unique differentiator
   (**viśeṣa**) vary, and the classification of some bound souls as eternal transmigrators or
   darkness-eligible is doctrinally supportable but philosophically contested. Report the doctrine,
   then evaluate it.

---
## CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

> Compressed revision notes for the complete topic. Headings are topic-specific rather than a fixed
> template, they follow the teaching order of the nineteen sessions, and they prioritise
> definitions, distinctions, evidence, comparisons, traps and answer routes over repeated
> introduction.

### A. One canon, six settlements

- Threefold canon (**prasthāna-trayī**): principal Upaniṣads (revealed foundation), Bhagavad Gītā
  (remembered synthesis), Bādarāyaṇa's *Brahma Sūtra* (reasoned systematization).
- The aphorisms under-determine doctrine; hence competing commentaries, not competing scriptures.
- Six schools: non-dualism (Śaṅkara), qualified non-dualism (Rāmānuja), dualism (Madhva), dualistic
  non-dualism (Nimbārka), pure non-dualism (Vallabha), inconceivable difference–non-difference
  (Caitanya tradition).
- Ownership boundary: earlier Mīmāṃsā owns injunction-centred reading; Vedānta owns the
  Brahman-centred reading of the Upaniṣads and the claim that scripture reveals accomplished reality
  (**siddha-vastu**), not only an act to be done (**kārya**).
- Master fork: identity · qualification · difference. Answer every printed term in three-school
  comparative mode.

### B. Ultimate reality: two definitions and the attributes fork

- Essential definition (**svarūpa-lakṣaṇa**): being–consciousness–bliss (**sat-cit-ānanda**) — what
  it is.
- Relational definition (**taṭastha-lakṣaṇa**): **janmādyasya yataḥ** — origin, sustenance,
  dissolution — why it grounds the world.
- Advaita: qualityless (**nirguṇa**) = free of limiting, relational, objectifiable attributes; NOT
  blank. Determinate qualities belong to the Lord at the empirical level.
- Viśiṣṭādvaita: qualified only (**ananta-kalyāṇa-guṇa-viśiṣṭa**); no level above the personal Lord.
- Dvaita: qualified and, decisively, independent (**svatantra**); all else dependent
  (**paratantra**).
- Rāmānuja's critique (2023): an attribute-less entity cannot be known, cannot be scripture's
  referent, cannot ground a qualified world. Reply: it is self-revealed, not objectively known.

### C. Causation: efficient and material

- Efficient cause (**nimitta-kāraṇa**) · material cause (**upādāna-kāraṇa**).
- Advaita: both; no second material possible; spider–web image; qualified by apparent transformation
  (**vivarta**) — material cause means *substrate*.
- Viśiṣṭādvaita: both; real causation; subtle modes become gross; God's essence unaltered.
- Dvaita: efficient only; dependent primordial matter (**prakṛti**) is the material cause.
- Contrast bank: Sāṃkhya splits material causality from intelligence · Nyāya splits efficient from
  material · Vedānta reunites both.
- 2025 Q6(b) route: define both causes → negative argument → spider → apparent transformation → rope
  → milk/curd contrast → rival schools → objection.

### D. Means of valid knowledge

- Advaita: six — perception, inference, comparison, testimony, postulation, non-apprehension.
- All six presuppose knower–means–known (**pramātṛ–pramāṇa–prameya**) => empirical level only.
- Testimony alone reaches the absolute, then self-cancels: clearing-nut (**kataka**), stick consumed
  in the pyre. The absolute is scripture-sourced (**śāstra-yoni / śāstra-pramāṇaka**).
- Why not the others: no sense-contact · no perceived instance or mark, and inference would make it
  an object · comparison, postulation and non-apprehension are empirically parasitic.
- Viśiṣṭādvaita: three; accepts indeterminate perception but denies cognition of a qualifierless
  (**nirviśeṣa**) object — *sarvaṃ vijñānaṃ saviśeṣam*.
- Dvaita: three, with scriptural tradition (**āgama**), the kevala/anu-pramāṇa distinction, and the
  witness (**sākṣin**) as internal validator.

### E. The Lord and the original–reflection model

- Advaita: the Lord = the absolute conditioned by illusion (**māyā-upahita brahman**); empirically
  real, ultimately sublated.
- Viśiṣṭādvaita and Dvaita: the Lord *is* the absolute; no higher level.
- Original–reflection (**bimba-pratibimba-vāda**, 2025 Q8(a)): original unaffected by defects,
  motion, multiplicity of media; face and mirrors.
- Variants: some later formulations make cosmic reflection the Lord and individual reflections the
  selves; others make the Lord original relative to the individual. Name the model; do not attribute
  uniformly to Śaṅkara.
- Soteriological point: the reflection has only derivative reality => liberation is recognition of
  non-difference, not movement, merging or destruction.
- Limit: formless original and formless medium make literal reflection impossible; Madhva uses the
  same metaphor for the opposite conclusion.
- Rival models: limitation (pot-space, **ghaṭākāśa**) · semblance (**ābhāsa**).

### F. The true self and the witness

- Advaita: the true self is the absolute in essence; witness (**sākṣī**) illumines all states, is
  altered by none, is self-luminous, and is the condition of experience — not a spectator.
- Three states: waking · dream · deep sleep; the report "I slept peacefully; I knew nothing" grounds
  continuity; the fourth standpoint (**turīya**) underlies all three, not a fourth episode.
- Qualification: the argument establishes an enduring witness more securely than non-dual identity;
  Rāmānuja and Nyāya accept the first and reject the second.
- Viśiṣṭādvaita: real, eternal, atomic (**aṇu**) knower; attributive consciousness
  (**dharma-bhūta-jñāna**) contracts under karmic obstruction and expands in liberation.
- Dvaita: real, eternal, dependent; difference from God is fundamental, never erased.
- Formula: identical in essence · inseparable mode · eternally different.

### G. The individual self

- True self = essential nature; individual (**jīva**) = embodied, bound, agentive, transmigrating.
- Advaita's three models: limitation (**avaccheda-vāda**, Vācaspati Miśra, pot-space) · reflection
  (**pratibimba-vāda**, Padmapāda, Vivaraṇa line, mirrored face) · semblance (**ābhāsa-vāda**).
- Common conclusion: individuality lacks ultimate reality.
- Viśiṣṭādvaita: real conscious mode (**prakāra**); body–soul dependence; "body" is functional, not
  gross.
- Dvaita: real distinct dependent substance; difference among selves real; taxonomy eternally free
  (**nitya**) · liberated (**mukta**) · bound (**baddha**), with bound souls further classified as
  liberation-eligible, eternal transmigrators, darkness-eligible — contested, evaluate it.
- Statement bank: appears finite through ignorance · real conscious mode · real dependent self
  eternally different.

### H. The world and the levels of reality

- Advaita: apparent transformation (**vivarta**); rope–snake; dependently real (**mithyā**), not
  wholly non-existent (**tuccha**).
- Three levels: ultimate (**pāramārthika**) · empirical (**vyāvahārika**) · apparent
  (**prātibhāsika**); sublation (**bādha**) grades them.
- Viśiṣṭādvaita: real; the non-conscious (**acit**) dimension of the absolute's body; subtle
  cause-state → gross effect-state; a meaningful field of duty and devotion.
- Dvaita: real, eternally distinct; God efficient governor; dependent matter the material cause;
  strongest realism.
- Śaṅkara vs Rāmānuja (2021 Q8(b)): reality of world · change in the absolute · soteriology ·
  God–world relation. Line: Śaṅkara protects absoluteness by reducing world-status; Rāmānuja
  protects world-status by qualifying absoluteness.

### I. Apparent versus real transformation

- Real transformation (**pariṇāma-vāda**): effect is a genuine modification — milk to curd.
- Apparent transformation (**vivarta**): substrate unchanged, effect sublatable — rope to snake.
- For: if the cause is the absolute and admits no modification, dependence must be preserved without
  change => apparent transformation.
- Against: contrary ontological status of the effect => rejection rather than completion.
- Graded verdict (2022 Q5(e)): agreement structural, disagreement ontological.
- Five-position bank: apparent · real · real-of-modes (Rāmānuja) · real-of-powers (Nimbārka) ·
  unchanged-real manifestation (Vallabha).

### J. Illusion and ignorance

- Illusion (**māyā**): beginningless, dependent, indescribable (**anirvacanīya**); neither being
  (**sat**) nor non-being (**asat**).
- Two powers: concealment (**āvaraṇa-śakti**) hides · projection (**vikṣepa-śakti**) fabricates.
- Two indescribabilities (2018 Q5(c)): the absolute exceeds predication and is never sublated
  (surplus); illusion is indeterminate between being and non-being because experienced yet sublated
  (indeterminacy).
- Illusion/ignorance: cosmic-and-Lord versus individual-and-self in many formulations; usage varies —
  state and qualify.
- Ignorance (**avidyā**): beginningless (**anādi**) yet terminable (**sānta**); positive in form
  (**bhāva-rūpa**), else it could not both conceal and project.
- Locus problem (**āśraya/viṣaya**): individual => circularity; absolute => contamination.
- Rivals: Rāmānuja — real ignorance of God, removed by knowledge with devotion and surrender; Madhva
  — real ignorance, removed by knowledge, devotion and grace.

### K. The seven objections and the alternative

- Locus · concealment · nature · indefinability · means of knowing · remover · cessation.
- Replies in order: Vivaraṇa/Bhāmatī · cloud not extinguishing the sun · being/non-being/indescribable
  trichotomy · grades by sublatability plus the counter-charge against the non-conscious mode ·
  witness-perception, postulation, scripture · undivided-form mental mode (**akhaṇḍākāra-vṛtti**) ·
  cessation as the absolute simply remaining.
- Verdict: strongest replies on concealment and cessation; genuine residue on locus and means of
  knowing.
- Rāmānuja's alternative: inseparable dependence · body–soul relation · apprehension of the real
  (**satkhyāti**) with elemental intermixture (**pañcīkaraṇa / trivṛtkaraṇa**).
- Dvaita: no illusion in the Advaita sense; real world, real ignorance; error as apprehension
  otherwise, newly analysed (**abhinava-anyathā-khyāti**).
- 2018 Q7(c) printed anomaly: flag it; Rāmānuja rejects illusion; read the demand as comparing
  explanations of plurality.

### L. Superimposition and the theory of error

- Superimposition (**adhyāsa**): mutual transference of subject and object; **atasmin tad-buddhiḥ**;
  natural (**naisargika**) and beginningless (**anādi**); components concealment and projection;
  root of all empirical dealing — "I am tall," "I know."
- Process versus status: superimposition is the process, indescribable-error theory
  (**anirvacanīya-khyāti**) states the status of the presented object.
- Four theories: apprehension otherwise (Nyāya) · apprehension of the real (Rāmānuja) · apprehension
  of the non-existent (Buddhist) · indescribable (Advaita).
- Refutation of Nyāya: correction cancels at the locus rather than relocating; the illusory silver is
  practically effective.
- Presupposition ledger: never sublated (**trikālābādhita**) · never presented · presented then
  sublated; sublation as criterion; positive-in-form ignorance; the excluded-middle objection and the
  grades reply.
- Śrīharṣa: dialectical refutation of definitions of the real, not a constructive definition.
- Examples: rope–snake · shell–silver · desert–mirage · hare's horn.

### M. The great sentence

- Direct meaning (**vācyārtha**) of *tat* = the Lord with illusion; of *tvam* = the individual with
  ignorance. They conflict.
- Device: part-abandoning implication (**bhāga-tyāga-lakṣaṇā**) = abandoning-and-retaining
  implication (**jahad-ajahal-lakṣaṇā**).
- Abandon: omniscience, lordship, cosmic causality / finitude, agency, ignorance. Retain: pure
  consciousness (**lakṣyārtha**).
- Parallel: *so'yaṃ devadattaḥ* — drop then-there and now-here, keep the person.
- Four standard great sentences: *tat tvam asi*, *ahaṃ brahmāsmi*, *prajñānaṃ brahma*, *ayam ātmā
  brahma*.
- Rivals read dependence or special relation, not identity.
- Textual caution: *Brahma satyaṃ jagan-mithyā, jīvo brahmaiva nāparaḥ* is a traditional summary, not
  a great sentence.

### N. Liberation

- Advaita: attainment of what is already attained (**prāptasya prāptiḥ**); bondage is ignorance;
  knowledge alone is the direct means; rejection of the knowledge-and-action combination
  (**jñāna-karma-samuccaya-vāda**).
- Preparation: fourfold qualification (**sādhana-catuṣṭaya**) — discrimination, detachment, sixfold
  discipline, desire for liberation; then hearing, reflection, contemplation
  (**śravaṇa–manana–nididhyāsana**); pedagogy of superimposition and rescission
  (**adhyāropa-apavāda**).
- Liberation while living (**jīvanmukti**); body continues by commenced karma (**prārabdha**) — the
  released arrow; disembodied liberation (**videhamukti**) adds nothing new.
- Viśiṣṭādvaita: unobstructed attributive consciousness; communion and service without identity;
  devotion (**bhakti**), surrender (**prapatti / śaraṇāgati**), grace (**prasāda**); no strict
  liberation while living.
- Dvaita: eternal blissful service; grace indispensable; difference never abolished; gradation
  (**tāratamya**).
- 2022 Q8(c) rows: God–self relation · content · knowledge and bliss · equality of the liberated ·
  causal background. Verdict: inseparable qualification versus permanent qualitative difference.
- Formula: recognition · communion · service.

### O. Inseparable dependence and the body–soul relation

- Inseparable dependence (**apṛthaksiddhi**): conscious and non-conscious are real, distinct, and
  incapable of existing or being conceived apart from the absolute.
- Inseparable qualifier (**apṛthaksiddha-viśeṣaṇa**): stronger than a conceptual relation, weaker
  than identity.
- Body–soul relation (**śarīra-śarīrī-bhāva**, 2024 Q5(e)): three conditions — entirely supported,
  entirely controlled, existing for its purposes.
- "Body" is functional, not gross. The analogy is asymmetric: God is untouched by the world-body's
  defects.
- Buys unity, real difference and devotion simultaneously; answers monism and pluralism at once.
- Not identity. Madhva rejects the body-relation while keeping dependence.
- Formula: identity without plurality · plurality within unity · plurality with dependence.

### P. Fivefold difference

- Soul–God · soul–soul · soul–matter · God–matter · matter–matter — real, eternal, metaphysically
  ultimate.
- Rejects Advaita's identity *and* Rāmānuja's qualified inseparability.
- Consequences: devotion never transcended · hierarchy among souls possible => gradation · plural
  world ontologically robust.
- Supremacy is defined by independence, not absorption.
- Unique differentiator (**viśeṣa**): blocks regress about substance and attribute; expositions vary.
- Criticism: ultimate pluralism and hierarchy; reply: plurality is created reality, not defect;
  residue: graded destinies.

### Q. The wider Vedānta

- Nimbārka (**dvaitādvaita**): difference and non-difference equally real; non-conscious reality =
  ordinary primordial matter (**prākṛta**) · non-prakritic divine substance (**aprākṛta**) · time
  (**kāla**); placement of time varies; creation is a real transformation of dependent powers.
- Vallabha (**śuddhādvaita**): unchanged real manifestation (**avikṛta-pariṇāma**); path of grace
  (**puṣṭi-mārga**); world not sublated; liberation as participation in bliss and loving service.
- Caitanya tradition (**acintyabhedābheda**): really non-different as dependent powers, really
  different as manifestations; "inconceivable" marks a limit on finite reduction, not a licence for
  contradiction; loving devotion is the sole direct path.
- 2018 Q7(a) matrix columns: metaphysical relation · direct path · liberated condition.
- Common ground devotion and grace; decisive difference the account of the soul.

### R. Śaṃkara against Sāṃkhya

- Chief wrestler (**pradhāna-malla**): the nearest rival, sharing pre-existence of the effect
  (**satkāryavāda**) and a single material ground, but making it unconscious.
- Four arguments: the cause must be conscious · teleology requires intelligence · the witness–matter
  relation is unintelligible · the Upaniṣadic cause is one, not dual.
- Sāṃkhya's reply: constitutional orientation of matter to the subject; scriptural appeal is internal
  to Vedānta.
- Ownership: the 2018 Sāṃkhya question on the Advaita reaction to real transformation is
  primary-owned by Sāṃkhya.

### S. Consequences, criticisms and the graded verdict

- Five consequences of absolutism (2019 Q8(a)): world as appearance · individuality provisional ·
  ethics empirical · devotion preparatory · instruments empirically valid only.
- Self-referential tension: reason and scripture used to relativise reason and scripture; ladder-model
  reply; cancellation coincides with the removal of ignorance.
- Criticism bank with replies: unknowable qualityless absolute · self-contradictory illusion ·
  collapse of ethics · inexplicable bondage · compromised absoluteness of a qualified God · ultimate
  pluralism and hierarchy.
- Crypto-Buddhist charge (**pracchanna-bauddha**): fails as identification — positive self-luminous
  absolute, scripture accepted, consciousness-only doctrine refuted — succeeds as pressure on shared
  world-negation.
- Graded verdicts: Advaita strong on non-duality, weak on lower-order world and devotion, contested on
  the locus and proof of ignorance · Viśiṣṭādvaita strong on religious realism, weak on divine
  simplicity, contested on whether qualification enriches or limits · Dvaita strong on devotional
  realism, weak on non-dual sentences, contested on hierarchy and the unique differentiator.
- Closing lines by marks: 10 — sublates, qualifies, absolutises. 15 — judged by different criteria of
  reality. 20 — a three-way contest between sublation, inseparability and real difference.
- Western comparison discipline: one or two lines only; Advaita is **not** subjective idealism.

> graphical package and is validated against the same Basic, Advanced and previous-year ledger. The
> same 22 panels are installed as a standalone text artifact and rendered as a standalone PDF.

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 1/22: Syllabus item 19, the controlling question, and the plural

  "Schools of Vedanta : Brahman; Isvara; Atman; Jiva; Jagat; Maya; Avida; Adhyasa;
   Moksa; Aprthaksiddhi; Pancavidhabheda."
  normalised: ultimate reality (brahman) · the Lord (īśvara) · true self (ātman) ·
  individual self (jīva) · world (jagat) · indescribable illusion (māyā) ·
  ignorance (avidyā, printed "Avida") · superimposition (adhyāsa) · liberation (mokṣa) ·
  inseparable dependence (apṛthaksiddhi) · fivefold difference (pañcavidhabheda)
                                  |
  THE PLURAL IS THE EXAMINATION: this is the ONLY Paper I clause beginning with
  "Schools". Not one of the eleven terms has a single Vedāntic meaning.
                                  |
  CONTROLLING QUESTION ...... is the relation of the FINITE to the ABSOLUTE
                              IDENTITY, QUALIFICATION, or DIFFERENCE?
                                  |
  +---- THE THUMB-RULE ------------------------------------------------------------+
  |  every printed term must be answered in THREE-SCHOOL COMPARATIVE MODE, even    |
  |  when the question names only one school, because a shared term's meaning is   |
  |  fixed only by contrast                                                        |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                  |
  CORPUS SIGNAL .... 20 primary-owned parts of 112 in the 2018-2025 corpus, second
                     only to Nyāya-Vaiśeṣika. Owned in EVERY year of the window:
                     2018×3 · 2019×1 · 2020×3 · 2021×3 · 2022×3 · 2023×3 · 2024×1 ·
                     2025×3
  SUPPORTING ONLY .. 2018 Q7(b) real transformation (owner: Sāṃkhya) ·
                     2018 Q8(a) self-luminosity and intrinsic validity (owner: Mīmāṃsā)

  +--------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
  |  ANSWER-GRABBING SPINE                                                          |
  |  Vedānta is not one doctrine but three answers to one question, and every       |
  |  printed syllabus term is that same fork applied to a different object.         |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

  ! TRAP 1: do not reduce "Vedānta" to Advaita; two-thirds of the owned questions
    require Rāmānuja, Madhva or a wider school.
  ! TRAP 2: do not treat the eleven terms as eleven topics; they are one dispute
    applied eleven times.
```

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 3/22: Ultimate reality — two definitions and the attributes fork

        ULTIMATE REALITY (brahman) — DEFINED TWICE
  +--------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
  | ESSENTIAL (svarūpa-lakṣaṇa)    | RELATIONAL (taṭastha-lakṣaṇa)                |
  | being-consciousness-bliss      | janmādyasya yataḥ — that from which origin,  |
  | (sat-cit-ānanda)               | sustenance and dissolution proceed           |
  | = WHAT IT IS in itself         | = WHY it is called the ground of the world   |
  +--------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
                    |
     "Does the absolute HAVE determinate attributes?"
                    |
   NO — qualityless (nirguṇa)              |  YES — qualified (saguṇa)
   ADVAITA                                  |
    * sole ULTIMATELY REAL (pāramārthika)   |  VIŚIṢṬĀDVAITA — Rāmānuja
    * nirguṇa ≠ blankness; it means FREE OF |   * saguṇa in the HIGHEST sense:
      LIMITING, relational, empirical,      |     ananta-kalyāṇa-guṇa-viśiṣṭa
      objectifiable attributes              |     endowed with infinite auspicious
    * sat-cit-ānanda = ESSENCE, not a list  |     qualities
      of accidental predicates              |   * qualities are PERFECTIONS, not
    * creatorhood, omniscience, lordship,   |     limiting adjuncts
      moral governance => belong to the LORD |   * identified with Viṣṇu-Nārāyaṇa
      (īśvara) at the EMPIRICAL level       |   * NO qualityless level above him
                                            |
                                            |  DVAITA — Madhva
                                            |   * personal Viṣṇu, perfect infinite
                                            |     attributes
                                            |   * alone INDEPENDENT (svatantra);
                                            |     selves and world DEPENDENT
                                            |     (paratantra)
                                            |   * difference itself is ULTIMATE —
                                            |     not absorbed into qualified unity

  RĀMĀNUJA'S CRITIQUE OF THE QUALITYLESS ABSOLUTE — asked 2023 Q6(a), 20 marks
    an entity WITHOUT attributes cannot be known
    if utterly indeterminate => cannot be the referent of scriptural description
    if no internal intelligibility => cannot cause a richly qualified world
    scriptural passages that NEGATE DEFECTS do not negate ALL qualities
    => Śaṅkara's absolute becomes unknowable emptiness OR an abstraction too thin to
      ground religion, devotion, causality or ethics
  ADVAITA'S REPLY: it is not known as an OBJECT with predicates; it is SELF-REVEALED
    as the ground of all knowing. Denying limiting predicates ≠ denying nature.

  CONSEQUENCE CASCADE (why this panel governs all the others)
    nirguṇa read => world becomes appearance · the Lord becomes lower truth ·
                   liberation becomes identity-recognition · scripture finally sublatable
    saguṇa read  => world and devotion become central · liberation becomes communion or
                   service · scripture stays final

  ! TRAP 1: "Brahman has no nature at all" destroys the answer at the outset.
  ! TRAP 2: "Rāmānuja accepts both nirguṇa and saguṇa Brahman" inverts his whole critique.
```

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 4/22: Efficient and material causality across the schools

  QUESTION: is ultimate reality the world's MATERIAL cause as well as its EFFICIENT
  cause?   — asked 2025 Q6(b), 15 marks, "with suitable examples"

  DEFINITIONS
    efficient cause (nimitta-kāraṇa) .... the intelligent agency that brings about
    material cause (upādāna-kāraṇa) ..... that OUT OF WHICH the effect is constituted

  ADVAITA ------------------------------------------------------------------------
    premise .... nothing exists independently of ultimate reality
    therefore .. there is NO SECOND MATERIAL out of which the world is made
    so ......... it is BOTH the intelligence behind the world AND the material basis
                 of its APPEARANCE
    image ...... the SPIDER spinning the web out of itself
    qualified by APPARENT TRANSFORMATION (vivarta): it does NOT really become the
    world; the world is an appearance dependent on it
    image ...... the ROPE and the SNAKE — substrate supports what it never becomes
    net ........ "material cause" = SUBSTRATE, not transformed stuff

  VIŚIṢṬĀDVAITA -------------------------------------------------------------------
    both causes, but causation is REAL
    subtle conscious (cit) and non-conscious (acit) modes => gross manifestation
    ultimate reality remains their controlling SUBSTANTIVE WHOLE
    the transformation belongs to it THROUGH the real transformation of the
    NON-CONSCIOUS mode — NOT through corruption of God's auspicious essence
    cause-state = SUBTLE · effect-state = GROSS

  DVAITA --------------------------------------------------------------------------
    God = EFFICIENT cause (nimitta-kāraṇa) ONLY
    primordial matter (prakṛti) = REAL DEPENDENT material cause, energised and
    governed by God
    dependence on God is universal — YET God, souls and matter remain really and
    eternally different
    ! therefore "brahman is both efficient and material cause" is TRUE of Advaita and
      Viśiṣṭādvaita IN DIFFERENT SENSES, and FALSE of Dvaita

  RIVAL SCHOOLS OUTSIDE VEDĀNTA — one-line contrast bank
    Sāṃkhya .... prakṛti is material cause; NO efficient creator-God
    Nyāya ...... God is primarily EFFICIENT; eternal atoms are MATERIAL
    formula .... Sāṃkhya splits material causality from intelligence; Nyāya splits
                 efficient from material causality; Vedānta REUNITES both

  ! TRAP: "all Vedānta schools hold Brahman is both causes" — Madhva denies it
    explicitly.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 5/22: Six means of valid knowledge and the primacy of testimony

```ascii-master

  ADVAITA'S SIX INSTRUMENTS (following the Bhāṭṭa Mīmāṃsā list)
  +------------------+--------------------------------------+------------------------+
  | perception       | immediate cognition through sense-   | "this pot is blue"     |
  | (pratyakṣa)      | contact with a PRESENT object        |                        |
  | inference        | unperceived fact via a perceived     | smoke on the hill =>    |
  | (anumāna)        | mark (liṅga) + concomitance (vyāpti) | fire                   |
  | comparison       | fresh cognition of an ABSENT         | gavaya seen => the      |
  | (upamāna)        | familiar object's similarity         | remembered cow is like |
  | verbal testimony | valid cognition from reliable words, | the great sentence     |
  | (śabda)          | supremely the AUTHORLESS             | teaches self-absolute  |
  |                  | (apauruṣeya) Veda                    | identity               |
  | postulation      | presumption of an unseen fact to     | fat Devadatta does not |
  | (arthāpatti)     | reconcile incompatible known facts   | eat by day => eats at   |
  |                  |                                      | night                  |
  | non-apprehension | cognition of ABSENCE through non-    | "there is no pot on    |
  | (anupalabdhi)    | perception under proper conditions   | the floor"             |
  +------------------+--------------------------------------+------------------------+
                                     |
     EVERY instrument presupposes the triad KNOWER - MEANS - KNOWN
                 (pramātṛ - pramāṇa - prameya)
                                     |
     that triad presupposes DUALITY => all six work ONLY at the EMPIRICAL level
     (vyāvahārika); at the ULTIMATE level (pāramārthika) there is no second object,
     no separate knower, no instrument — the whole apparatus is sublated
                                     |
  REFLEXIVE PUZZLE ─► if all instruments are only empirically valid, how does
                      testimony establish the ULTIMATE truth?
  REPLY ───────────► scripture REMOVES IGNORANCE and then loses operative relevance
                      ALONG WITH the ignorance it removed
  IMAGES ──────────► the CLEARING-NUT (kataka) that settles the mud and settles with
                      it · the STICK that stirs the pyre and is consumed in it
  => scripture is NOT an eternal second reality alongside the absolute; it is the
    EMPIRICAL LADDER by which the non-empirical truth is recognised

  WHY NOT THE OTHER FIVE?
    perception ..... the absolute is qualityless, non-objectifiable, not sensed
    inference ...... no perceived instance, no mark uniquely co-present; and an
                     inferred absolute becomes an inferred OBJECT (prameya)
    comparison, postulation, non-apprehension ..... derivative / empirically
                     parasitic: similarity, presumption and absence all operate
                     INSIDE the field of knowable objects
  => Śaṅkara: the absolute is ŚĀSTRA-YONI / ŚĀSTRA-PRAMĀṆAKA — known through scripture
  => FOUR GREAT SENTENCES: tat tvam asi · ahaṃ brahmāsmi · prajñānaṃ brahma ·
    ayam ātmā brahma

  FAULT-LINE WITH EARLIER MĪMĀṂSĀ
    Mīmāṃsā ..... Vedic authority is primarily authority for INJUNCTION (vidhi) about
                  duty; statements of fact are subordinate to ritual action
    Advaita ..... Upaniṣadic statements about ACCOMPLISHED REALITY (siddha-vastu) are
                  independently and supremely valid; knowledge (jñāna) is NOT an act
                  to be performed (kārya)
    OBJ ......... if the Veda is for action, how can a sentence stating identity
                  produce liberation?
    REPLY ....... bondage is IGNORANCE, not absence of ritual; therefore the direct
                  remover must be KNOWLEDGE
    => the divide is not ritual-versus-metaphysics but whether Vedic sentences can
      independently reveal a NON-ACTIONAL accomplished reality

  ! TRAP: "Advaita rejects perception and inference" — it denies ULTIMACY, not
    empirical validity.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 8/22: Original-reflection, and the three models of individuation

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 8/22: Original-reflection, and the three models of individuation

  ORIGINAL-REFLECTION MODEL (bimba-pratibimba-vāda) — asked 2025 Q8(a), 20 marks
       ORIGINAL (bimba) = pure consciousness / ultimate reality
                   |
       reflected in v a conditioning medium (internal organ under ignorance)
       REFLECTION (pratibimba) = the empirical self
       * the original is UNAFFECTED by defect, motion or multiplicity in the media,
         as ONE FACE is unchanged though many mirrors — cracked, tilted, moving —
         yield differently conditioned images
       * SOME later formulations: reflection in TOTAL COSMIC appearance = the Lord;
         reflections in INDIVIDUAL ignorance / internal organs = the selves
       ? OTHER formulations make the Lord the ORIGINAL relative to the self, or
         prefer LIMITATION or SEMBLANCE models instead
       ! do NOT write that the Lord is simultaneously "the original" and
         "consciousness reflected in illusion" WITHOUT NAMING the relative or later
         model being used; these are LATER explanatory alternatives, not one uniform
         proposition attributable without qualification to Śaṅkara

  SOTERIOLOGICAL SIGNIFICANCE — the half of the 2025 question most answers miss
    the reflected image has only DERIVATIVE reality
    => when ignorance is removed the finite self is known NEVER to have possessed
      independent reality apart from original consciousness
    => liberation = RECOGNITION of non-difference, NOT movement, merging or the
      destruction of one substance by another
    => this is why liberation can be ATTAINMENT OF WHAT IS ALREADY ATTAINED
      (prāptasya prāptiḥ) and why LIBERATION WHILE LIVING (jīvanmukti) is possible
    ! LIMIT: a formless original, a formless medium and a non-dual reality make
      LITERAL reflection impossible; reflection also normally needs a spatial gap.
      Use the model for SUGGESTIVE value, not optical physics.
    ! Madhva uses the SAME metaphor for the OPPOSITE conclusion — that the dependent
      image is REALLY DIFFERENT from its original — which shows how far the metaphor
      under-determines the metaphysics.

  THREE MODELS OF ONE PROBLEM
    limitation (avaccheda-vāda) ...... one space appearing as pot-space (ghaṭākāśa);
                                       emphasis on DELIMITATION
                                       associated with VĀCASPATI MIŚRA
    reflection (pratibimba-vāda) ..... consciousness reflected in ignorance;
                                       emphasis on DERIVATIVE APPEARANCE and
                                       phenomenological vividness
                                       associated with PADMAPĀDA and the VIVARAṆA line
    semblance (ābhāsa-vāda) .......... the self is a mere SEMBLANCE (ābhāsa);
                                       emphasis on ONTOLOGICAL THINNESS
    COMMON CONCLUSION: individuality LACKS ULTIMATE REALITY

  ! TRAP: swapping the names — limitation is Vācaspati Miśra with pot-space,
    reflection is Padmapāda with the mirrored face.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 9/22: The true self, the witness, and the three states

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 9/22: The true self, the witness, and the three states

  THE STATE-ANALYSIS ARGUMENT (Advaita)
    waking (jāgrat) ......... an EXTERNAL object-world is presented
    dream (svapna) .......... INTERNALLY generated objects are presented
    deep sleep (suṣupti) .... NO manifest subject-object cognition
                                   |
    report on waking: "I slept peacefully; I knew nothing"
    => presupposes CONTINUITY through deep sleep
    => supports a WITNESS not identical with any particular mental mode
                                   |
    the FOURTH standpoint (turīya) is NOT a fourth episode after the three;
    it is the non-dual consciousness that UNDERLIES and ILLUMINES all three
                                   |
    ! HONEST QUALIFICATION: this is an argument WITHIN Advaita, not a neutral proof.
      Rāmānuja and Nyāya retain an ENDURING SELF while REJECTING its identity with
      the non-dual absolute. The argument establishes an enduring witness more
      securely than it establishes identity.

  WITNESS-CONSCIOUSNESS (sākṣī) — WHAT IT IS AND IS NOT
    + illumines all mental states, never altered by them
    + pleasure, pain, doubt, memory, desire are WITNESSED, not essential to it
    + not a passive blank but the CONDITION for all experience
    + self-luminous (svayaṃ-prakāśa): manifestation belongs to consciousness as its
      very nature
    - NOT one more knower sitting inside the person
    => Advaita's elegant split: CHANGING PSYCHOLOGY vs CHANGELESS CONSCIOUSNESS
    => episodic cognitions, objects and the empirical knower remain INSIDE the
      subject-object structure

  THREE SETTLEMENTS OF ONE ENDURING SELF
  +----------------+---------------------------+-------------------------------+
  | Advaita        | pure consciousness        | IDENTICAL in essence          |
  | Viśiṣṭādvaita  | real eternal conscious    | INSEPARABLE MODE, not         |
  |                | self, ATOMIC (aṇu)        | identical                     |
  | Dvaita         | real eternal dependent    | ETERNALLY DIFFERENT           |
  |                | self                      |                               |
  +----------------+---------------------------+-------------------------------+

  RĀMĀNUJA'S MECHANISM — how an ATOMIC self knows a WIDE world
    substantive knower = ATOMIC (aṇu), self-luminous
    ATTRIBUTIVE CONSCIOUSNESS (dharma-bhūta-jñāna) = its quality, which REVEALS
    objects · CONTRACTS under karmic obstruction · EXPANDS without obstruction in
    liberation
    => atomic size does NOT confine cognitive range
    in deep sleep the self PERSISTS, but object-manifesting cognition is UNEXPRESSED
    because no object is presented

  WHY THIS DECIDES EVERYTHING DOWNSTREAM
    self IS the absolute ............ liberation = RECOGNITION
    self is a MODE of it ............ liberation = COMMUNION
    self is permanently DIFFERENT ... liberation = ETERNAL SERVICE
```

```ascii-master
    never reducible to God · never a mere mode · never illusory
    difference AMONG individuals is ALSO real

    bound souls further described as liberation-eligible (mukti-yogya) · eternal
    transmigrators (nitya-saṃsārin) · darkness-eligible (tamo-yogya)

      EVALUATE its severe ethical implications rather than reporting predestination
      casually.

    Advaita ......... the individual is the absolute APPEARING FINITE through ignorance
    Viśiṣṭādvaita ... the individual is a REAL CONSCIOUS MODE of the absolute
```

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 11/22: The world — three levels of reality and three verdicts

  THE THREE LEVELS OF REALITY (Advaita)
    ULTIMATE (pāramārthika) ..... the absolute alone; NEVER SUBLATED (trikālābādhita)
    EMPIRICAL (vyāvahārika) ..... the world, the Lord and individual selves — VALID,
                                  coherent, PUBLIC, law-governed, INTERSUBJECTIVE;
                                  sublated only by knowledge of the absolute

    => the world is denied ABSOLUTE reality, NOT empirical functioning
    => DEPENDENTLY REAL (mithyā) ≠ WHOLLY NON-EXISTENT (tuccha, e.g. a hare's horn)
    => sublation (bādha) is what GRADES the three levels

  THREE VERDICTS ON THE WORLD
    ADVAITA ......... APPARENT TRANSFORMATION (vivarta); the rope-snake substrate is
                      real, the appearance dependent and sublatable; mithyā not tuccha
    VIŚIṢṬĀDVAITA ... the world is REAL and forms the NON-CONSCIOUS (acit) dimension
                      of the absolute's BODY; creation is a real manifestation
                      involving the non-conscious mode (prakṛti); cause-state SUBTLE,
                      effect-state GROSS; because effects are real, the universe is a
                      MEANINGFUL FIELD of duty, devotion and divine manifestation
    DVAITA .......... the world is REAL; dependence does NOT cancel distinctness;
                      God is its EFFICIENT governor, dependent primordial matter its
                      MATERIAL cause; matter is NOT absorbed into identity by
                      knowledge => the STRONGEST realist posture among the three

  |                      |                          | relation                  |
  +----------------------+--------------------------+---------------------------+
    EVALUATIVE LINE: Śaṅkara protects ABSOLUTENESS by reducing world-status;
                     Rāmānuja protects WORLD-STATUS by qualifying absoluteness.
    SHARED GROUND (state it first): total dependence on the absolute · no second
    independent principle · scripture as final authority

  MADHVA ON INDIVIDUAL AND WORLD — asked 2021 Q8(c), 15 marks
    both are REAL and DISTINCT from God · difference is not merely practical but
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 12/22: Apparent transformation versus real transformation

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 12/22: Apparent transformation versus real transformation

  THE CAUSATION FORK — asked 2022 Q5(e), 10 marks:
  "Do you agree that Vivartavāda is the logical development of Pariṇāmavāda?"

  SĀṂKHYA .... REAL transformation (pariṇāma-vāda): the effect is a REAL MODIFICATION
               of the cause .................... MILK => CURD
  ADVAITA .... APPARENT transformation (vivarta): the substrate is UNCHANGED and the
               effect is a SUBLATABLE APPEARANCE .... ROPE => SNAKE

  ARGUMENT FOR "logical development"
    both refuse production FROM NOTHING and both make the effect CAUSE-DEPENDENT
    if the cause is the ABSOLUTE, and the absolute admits NO real modification,
    then the effect cannot be equally real AS TRANSFORMATION
    => transformation must be only APPARENT: not pariṇāma but vivarta
    => vivarta PRESERVES causal dependence while PREVENTING real change in the absolute

  ARGUMENT AGAINST
    real transformation and apparent transformation assign CONTRARY ONTOLOGICAL STATUS
    to the effect
    what real transformation MOST INSISTS ON — genuine change in the cause — is
    precisely what apparent transformation ABANDONS
    => a doctrine that denies the core commitment of another is a REJECTION, not a
      completion

  GRADED VERDICT — the mark-winning line
    apparent transformation DEVELOPS the search for an effect DEPENDENT on its cause,
    but ABANDONS real transformation's central commitment to REAL transformation.
    AGREEMENT IS STRUCTURAL; DISAGREEMENT IS ONTOLOGICAL.

  FIVE-POSITION TRANSFORMATION BANK (one line each, high comparative value)
    Advaita ......... APPARENT transformation — substrate unchanged, effect sublatable
    Sāṃkhya ......... REAL transformation of primordial matter
    Rāmānuja ........ REAL transformation OF MODES; essence uncorrupted
    Nimbārka ........ REAL transformation of the absolute's DEPENDENT POWERS
    Vallabha ........ UNCHANGED REAL MANIFESTATION (avikṛta-pariṇāma) — effect real,
                      cause unaltered; NEITHER vivarta NOR ordinary pariṇāma

  ! TRAP: answering "yes" or "no" flatly. The word in the stem is DEVELOPMENT, and it
    is the graded verdict that is being tested.
```

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 13/22: Illusion, ignorance, two indescribabilities, the locus problem

  TWO SENSES OF INDESCRIBABILITY — asked 2018 Q5(c), 10 marks
  +---------------------------------------+---------------------------------------+
  | ULTIMATE REALITY (brahman)            | INDESCRIBABLE ILLUSION (māyā)         |
  +---------------------------------------+---------------------------------------+
  | non-dual, non-objectifiable reality   | appearance that CANNOT BE CLASSIFIED  |
  | EXCEEDS limiting subject-predicate    | as ultimately real (it is SUBLATED)   |
  | description                           | NOR as sheer non-being (it is         |
  |                                       | EXPERIENCED)                          |
  | does NOT mean uncertain reality       | DOES mean indeterminate ontological   |
  |                                       | status                                |
  | the UNSUBLATED reality BECAUSE OF     | a DEPENDENT and SUBLATABLE            |
  | which description and experience are  | explanatory principle                 |
  | possible                              |                                       |
  | => a SURPLUS: too real to be predicated| => an INDETERMINACY: falls BETWEEN the |
  |                                       |   two ultimate ontological heads      |
  +---------------------------------------+---------------------------------------+
    X THE TWO USES ARE NOT IDENTICAL
    ! critical add-on: realist schools accept the absolute's TRANSCENDENCE far more
      readily than they accept an alleged THIRD ONTOLOGICAL STATUS for illusion

  TWO POWERS OF ILLUSION
    concealment (āvaraṇa-śakti) ..... HIDES the absolute's true nature
    projection (vikṣepa-śakti) ...... THROWS UP the world of names and forms
    => together they explain how ignorance can both COVER and FABRICATE
    => they reappear as the TWO COMPONENTS of superimposition (Panel 16)

  ILLUSION AND IGNORANCE — IDENTITY OR DISTINCTION?  (? genuinely non-uniform)
    ? some formulations treat them as broadly INTERCHANGEABLE
    ? others: illusion = COSMIC / OBJECTIVE, associated with the LORD
              ignorance = INDIVIDUAL / SUBJECTIVE, associated with the INDIVIDUAL
    + both are usually regarded as INDESCRIBABLE
    => EXAM FORMULA: state the distinction carefully AND note that usage varies

  IGNORANCE (avidyā) IN THE THREE SCHOOLS
    ADVAITA ......... BEGINNINGLESS (anādi) yet TERMINABLE (sānta) by knowledge; NOT
                      eternal as the absolute is eternal; POSITIVE IN FORM
                      (bhāva-rūpa), else it could not both conceal and project;
                      through it the self identifies with body, mind, senses,
                      DOERSHIP and ENJOYERSHIP => empirical existence is structured by
                      ignorance, NOT by a real transformation of the self
    VIŚIṢṬĀDVAITA ... NO indescribable cosmic principle; REAL ignorance of God's
                      nature and of the soul's dependence; tied to karma; removed by
                      right knowledge with devotion and especially SELF-SURRENDER
                      (prapatti)
    DVAITA .......... ignorance is REAL; not a metaphysical veil over identity;
                      overcome by correct knowledge, devotion and divine GRACE
                      => Madhva's realism preserves stronger continuity between
                        ignorance, ethics, devotion and salvation

  THE LOCUS-AND-OBJECT PROBLEM (āśraya / viṣaya)
    whose is ignorance?
      in the INDIVIDUAL => CIRCULAR, since the individual arises WITHIN ignorance
      in the ABSOLUTE => the absolute appears COMPROMISED
    => the doorway to Rāmānuja's seven objections (Panels 14-15)

  ! TRAP: flattening illusion and ignorance into a synonym-pair, OR asserting the
    distinction as universal. State it, then record the variation.
```
